# Chapter 22: Traffic Code and Crash Investigations — Full Chapter Bank (Days 1-9)

*Combined bank covering LO 22.1–22.38 — the complete Day 1–9 Chapter 22 material in one bank. Originally split across three files (Day 1-4, Day 5-8, Day 9); merged into a single bank so the whole chapter can be quizzed together, with subject tags (instead of a plain Multiple Choice / Fill-in-Blank split) so you can filter or multi-select by topic — e.g. just "Right-of-Way" and "Turning & Signaling," or everything tagged "Vehicle Equipment & Lighting." A dedicated "Lighting — Model-Year & Effective-Date Rules" tag pulls together every question keyed to a specific model-year or effective-date threshold in the lighting statutes (e.g. TC 547.322(b)'s pre-1960 single-taillamp allowance), whichever LO they originally fell under.*

### Q1 — Vehicle Type Definitions
**LO:** 22.1 · **Source:** TC 541.201(1)

Which of the following, by itself, is enough to make a vehicle an "authorized emergency vehicle" under TC 541.201(1)?

- [ ] Any vehicle displaying a red or blue flashing light
- [x] A private vehicle of a volunteer firefighter responding to a fire alarm
- [ ] Any tow truck responding to a highway collision
- [ ] A personal vehicle equipped with a siren

**Explanation:** 541.201(1) enumerates specific categories (fire/police vehicles, licensed ambulances, certain EMS vehicles, designated municipal/county vehicles, volunteer firefighter/EMS private vehicles while responding, industrial emergency response vehicles, blood/tissue bank vehicles on emergency runs, federal law enforcement vehicles) — simply having warning lights or a siren does not qualify a vehicle.

---

### Q2 — Vehicle Type Definitions
**LO:** 22.1 · **Source:** TC 541.201(2)

Under TC 541.201(2), a "______" is a device, excluding a moped, capable of being ridden solely using human power that has two tandem wheels at least one of which is more than ______ inches in diameter (or three wheels meeting the same threshold, or any number of wheels with adaptive technology for a rider with a disability).

**Answer:** Bicycle | 14

**Explanation:** The 14-inch wheel-diameter threshold is what separates a "bicycle" from a small-wheeled tricycle or similar toy under this definition.

---

### Q3 — Vehicle Type Definitions
**LO:** 22.1 · **Source:** TC 541.201(3)

Under TC 541.201(3), which of the following independently qualifies as a "bus"?

- [x] A motor vehicle, other than a taxicab, designed and used to transport persons for compensation
- [ ] Any vehicle owned by a school district
- [ ] A motor vehicle designed to accommodate exactly 8 passengers
- [ ] A vehicle used to transport cargo for compensation

**Explanation:** "Bus" has two independent prongs: capacity (designed for more than 10 passengers, including the operator) OR for-compensation passenger transport other than a taxicab — either one qualifies.

---

### Q4 — Vehicle Type Definitions
**LO:** 22.1 · **Source:** TC 541.201(4)

"______" means a motor vehicle designed and used primarily as a farm implement to draw an implement of husbandry, including a plow or a mowing machine.

**Answer:** Farm Tractor

**Explanation:** A farm tractor is the vehicle that draws an implement of husbandry — the implement itself is defined separately under 541.201(6).

---

### Q5 — Vehicle Type Definitions
**LO:** 22.1 · **Source:** TC 541.201(5)

TC 541.201(5) defines "house trailer" as a trailer or semitrailer (other than a towable recreational vehicle) built on a permanent chassis with plumbing/heating/electrical systems that is also which of the following, while in traveling mode?

- [x] Less than 45 feet in length, excluding the tow bar
- [ ] Less than 20 feet in length, excluding the tow bar
- [ ] 45 feet or more in length, excluding the tow bar
- [ ] No length restriction applies

**Explanation:** The 45-foot traveling-mode length ceiling is what distinguishes a "house trailer" from a "towable recreational vehicle" (which is expressly excluded from this definition) and from a permanent manufactured home.

---

### Q6 — Vehicle Type Definitions
**LO:** 22.1 · **Source:** TC 541.201(6)

"______" means, among other things, a towed vehicle that transports to the field and spreads fertilizer or agricultural chemicals, or a motor vehicle designed and adapted to deliver feed to livestock.

**Answer:** Implement Of Husbandry

**Explanation:** "Implement of husbandry" and "farm tractor" are separate, cross-referencing definitions in 541.201(4) and (6) — the tractor draws the implement, not the other way around.

---

### Q7 — Vehicle Type Definitions
**LO:** 22.1 · **Source:** TC 541.201(7)

Under TC 541.201(7), a "light truck" (including a pickup, panel delivery, or carryall truck) is one with a manufacturer's rated carrying capacity of:

- [x] 2,000 pounds or less
- [ ] 6,000 pounds or less
- [ ] 10,000 pounds or less
- [ ] 26,000 pounds or less

**Explanation:** 2,000 pounds is the exact statutory ceiling that makes a truck a "light truck" under 541.201(7).

---

### Q8 — Vehicle Type Definitions
**LO:** 22.1 · **Source:** TC 541.201(8)

A "moped" cannot attain a speed of more than ______ miles per hour, cannot produce more than five-brake horsepower, and if it has an internal combustion engine, has a piston displacement of ______ cubic centimeters or less with no required gear shifting.

**Answer:** 30 | 50

**Explanation:** Two thresholds define a moped's engine: 30 mph top speed, five-brake-horsepower ceiling, and (if internal combustion) 50cc or less displacement with an automatic power-drive system.

---

### Q9 — Vehicle Type Definitions
**LO:** 22.1 · **Source:** TC 541.201(8), (9)

An operator's saddle-equipped vehicle with no more than three wheels when propelled tops out at 45 mph and has an internal combustion engine with a piston displacement of 90 cubic centimeters. Under TC 541.201, this vehicle is legally a:

- [x] Motorcycle
- [ ] Moped
- [ ] Farm tractor
- [ ] Special mobile equipment

**Explanation:** It exceeds the moped's 30 mph speed cap and 50cc displacement limit, so by process of elimination it falls under "motorcycle" — a motor vehicle (other than a tractor or moped) with a rider's saddle and no more than three wheels when propelled.

---

### Q10 — Vehicle Type Definitions
**LO:** 22.1 · **Source:** TC 541.201(11)

"______" means a self-propelled vehicle or a vehicle that is propelled by electric power from overhead trolley wires. The term does not include an electric bicycle or an electric personal assistive mobility device.

**Answer:** Motor Vehicle

**Explanation:** "Motor vehicle" under 541.201(11) is broader than "vehicle" (541.201(23)) but expressly excludes electric bicycles and electric personal assistive mobility devices.

---

### Q11 — Vehicle Type Definitions
**LO:** 22.1 · **Source:** TC 541.201(11-a), (15), (16)

A bus used to transport secondary students on a school-related activity trip (not on a route to/from school) that was manufactured to federal school-bus safety standards, except for the standards requiring alternately flashing red lights and movable stop arms, is a:

- [x] Multifunction school activity bus
- [ ] School bus
- [ ] School activity bus
- [ ] Passenger car

**Explanation:** A "school bus" (541.201(16)) must meet all federal school-bus safety standards including flashing lights and stop arms; a "school activity bus" (541.201(15)) is a bus designed for more than 15 passengers owned/operated by a school district that is not built to those federal standards at all. The "multifunction school activity bus" (541.201(11-a)) is the one built to most, but not all, of the federal standards.

---

### Q12 — Vehicle Type Definitions
**LO:** 22.1 · **Source:** TC 541.201(12)

"______" means a motor vehicle, other than a motorcycle, used to transport persons and designed to accommodate ______ or fewer passengers, including the operator.

**Answer:** Passenger Car | 10

**Explanation:** The 10-passenger-or-fewer threshold in 541.201(12) is the mirror image of the "bus" definition's more-than-10-passenger threshold in 541.201(3).

---

### Q13 — Vehicle Type Definitions
**LO:** 22.1 · **Source:** TC 541.201(13), (17), (20)

A vehicle without motive power, designed to be drawn by another vehicle and secured to it by a pole, reach, or boom, and ordinarily used to transport a long or irregularly shaped load like pipes or structural members, is a:

- [x] Pole trailer
- [ ] Semitrailer
- [ ] Trailer
- [ ] Truck tractor

**Explanation:** A "semitrailer" and a "trailer" are both expressly defined as "other than a pole trailer" — the pole/reach/boom connection method and the long/irregular-load use are what set the "pole trailer" apart.

---

### Q14 — Vehicle Type Definitions
**LO:** 22.1 · **Source:** TC 541.201(18)

"______" means a vehicle that is not designed or used primarily to transport persons or property and that is only incidentally operated on a highway, including ditchdigging apparatus, well boring apparatus, and road construction and maintenance machinery.

**Answer:** Special Mobile Equipment

**Explanation:** The definition specifically excludes a vehicle designed to transport persons or property that merely has machinery attached (like a house trailer, dump truck, or truck-mounted crane) — those remain their own vehicle type.

---

### Q15 — Vehicle Type Definitions
**LO:** 22.1 · **Source:** TC 541.201(23)

TC 541.201(23) defines "vehicle" as a device that can be used to transport or draw persons or property on a highway. Which of the following is expressly excluded from that definition?

- [x] A device exclusively used on stationary rails or tracks
- [ ] A motorcycle
- [ ] A trailer
- [ ] Special mobile equipment

**Explanation:** 541.201(23) excludes only (A) a device exclusively used on stationary rails or tracks and (B) manufactured housing as defined by the Occupations Code — everything else that transports or draws persons/property on a highway qualifies as a "vehicle."

---

### Q16 — Persons, Authorities & Penal Code Cross-References
**LO:** 22.2 · **Source:** TC 541.001(1-a)

"______" means, as used in reference to a vehicle, a person who drives or has physical control of a vehicle.

**Answer:** Operator

**Explanation:** "Operator" turns on driving or physical control, not ownership — a distinction the Code draws separately in 541.001(2) for "owner."

---

### Q17 — Persons, Authorities & Penal Code Cross-References
**LO:** 22.2 · **Source:** TC 541.001(2)

Under TC 541.001(2), the term "owner" (as used in reference to a vehicle) specifically excludes:

- [x] A lienholder and a lessee whose lease is not intended as security
- [ ] A person entitled to use and possess a vehicle subject to a security interest
- [ ] Any individual with a property interest in the vehicle
- [ ] A person with title to the vehicle

**Explanation:** "Owner" includes a person entitled to use/possess a vehicle subject to a security interest, but excludes a lienholder and a lessee whose lease is not intended as security.

---

### Q18 — Persons, Authorities & Penal Code Cross-References
**LO:** 22.2 · **Source:** TC 541.001(3), (4)

"______" means a person on foot, while "______" means an individual, firm, partnership, association, or corporation.

**Answer:** Pedestrian | Person

**Explanation:** These are two of the simplest definitions in Subchapter A — "pedestrian" is purely about being on foot, while "person" is the broad catch-all entity definition used throughout the subtitle.

---

### Q19 — Persons, Authorities & Penal Code Cross-References
**LO:** 22.2 · **Source:** TC 541.001(5)

Under TC 541.001(5), a "school crossing guard" must be a responsible person who is designated by a local authority and is at least:

- [x] 18 years of age
- [ ] 16 years of age
- [ ] 21 years of age
- [ ] 25 years of age

**Explanation:** The 18-year minimum age is the specific statutory threshold for a school crossing guard directing traffic in a school crossing zone.

---

### Q20 — Persons, Authorities & Penal Code Cross-References
**LO:** 22.2 · **Source:** TC 541.001(6)

A "______" is an individual lawfully operating a bicycle, a motor-assisted scooter, an electric personal assistive mobility device, a skateboard, roller skates, or a similar device on a sidewalk.

**Answer:** Sidewalk User

**Explanation:** This term matters because several right-of-way rules (like TC 545.256, emerging from an alley or driveway) require yielding specifically to pedestrians and sidewalk users.

---

### Q21 — Persons, Authorities & Penal Code Cross-References
**LO:** 22.2 · **Source:** TC 541.002(3)

Under TC 541.002(3), a school district created under state law counts as a "local authority" only:

- [x] When it is designating school crossing guards for schools it operates
- [ ] For any traffic-related purpose
- [ ] When it owns roads within its boundaries
- [ ] Never — school districts cannot be a local authority

**Explanation:** A school district's "local authority" status under 541.002(3)(B) is narrowly limited to designating school crossing guards — a county, municipality, or other local entity authorized to enact traffic laws is a local authority for all purposes.

---

### Q22 — Persons, Authorities & Penal Code Cross-References
**LO:** 22.2 · **Source:** TC 541.002(4)

"______" means an officer authorized to direct traffic or arrest persons who violate traffic regulations.

**Answer:** Police Officer

**Explanation:** This TC 541.002(4) definition of "police officer" is broader than a municipal police department badge — any officer with authority to direct traffic or arrest traffic violators qualifies.

---

### Q23 — Persons, Authorities & Penal Code Cross-References
**LO:** 22.2 · **Source:** TC 541.101

Under TC 541.101, a "metropolitan area" must contain at least one municipality with a population of at least:

- [x] 100,000
- [ ] 50,000
- [ ] 250,000
- [ ] 500,000

**Explanation:** 541.101 requires at least one municipality of 100,000+ population, plus the adjacent municipalities and unincorporated urban districts.

---

### Q24 — Persons, Authorities & Penal Code Cross-References
**LO:** 22.2 · **Source:** TC 541.102(1)

A "______" is territory adjacent to and including a highway where buildings used for business or industrial purposes are located within a ______-foot segment along the highway and occupy at least 300 feet of frontage.

**Answer:** Business District | 600

**Explanation:** The 600-foot segment and 300-foot frontage thresholds together define "business district" under 541.102(1) — "residence district" under 541.102(2) uses only the 300-foot frontage figure, without the 600-foot segment cap.

---

### Q25 — Persons, Authorities & Penal Code Cross-References
**LO:** 22.2 · **Source:** TC 541.102(3)

Under TC 541.102(3), an "urban district" is territory outside a municipality improved with structures used for business, industry, or dwellings located at intervals of less than 100 feet for a distance of at least:

- [x] One-quarter mile on either side of the highway
- [ ] One-half mile on either side of the highway
- [ ] One mile on either side of the highway
- [ ] 600 feet on either side of the highway

**Explanation:** The one-quarter-mile distance (with structures spaced less than 100 feet apart) is the specific threshold for an "urban district," distinct from the 600-foot/300-foot figures used for business and residence districts.

---

### Q26 — Persons, Authorities & Penal Code Cross-References
**LO:** 22.2 · **Source:** PC 1.07(a)(8), (46)

"______" means physical pain, illness, or any impairment of physical condition, while "______" means bodily injury that creates a substantial risk of death or that causes death, serious permanent disfigurement, or protracted loss or impairment of the function of any bodily member or organ.

**Answer:** Bodily Injury | Serious Bodily Injury

**Explanation:** "Serious bodily injury" is the aggravated version of "bodily injury" — the distinction drives enhanced penalties, such as under TC 542.4045 for failure-to-yield collisions.

---

### Q27 — Persons, Authorities & Penal Code Cross-References
**LO:** 22.2 · **Source:** PC 1.07(a)(26), (40), (49)

Under Penal Code 1.07(a), which of the following is true?

- [x] "Individual" includes an unborn child at every stage of gestation from fertilization until birth
- [ ] "Public place" is limited to government-owned property only
- [ ] "Death" excludes an unborn child's failure to be born alive
- [ ] "Individual" means only a person who is 18 years of age or older

**Explanation:** PC 1.07(a)(26) expressly includes an unborn child at every stage of gestation as an "individual"; (a)(40) defines "public place" broadly as anywhere the public or a substantial group has access (streets, highways, common areas of schools/hospitals/apartments/shops, etc.); and (a)(49) includes, for an unborn child, the failure to be born alive as "death."

---

### Q28 — Traffic & Traffic Control Definitions
**LO:** 22.3 · **Source:** TC 541.301

"______" means pedestrians, ridden or herded animals, and conveyances, including vehicles and streetcars, singly or together while using a highway for the purposes of travel.

**Answer:** Traffic

**Explanation:** "Traffic" is a broad term covering more than just vehicles — it includes pedestrians and animals using the highway for travel.

---

### Q29 — Traffic & Traffic Control Definitions
**LO:** 22.3 · **Source:** TC 541.302(1)

Under TC 541.302(1), an "alley" is a street that:

- [x] Is not used primarily for through traffic and provides access to rear entrances of buildings or lots
- [ ] Is used exclusively for pedestrian traffic
- [ ] Connects two limited-access highways
- [ ] Is designated only by an official traffic-control device

**Explanation:** The defining features of an "alley" are that it is not for through traffic and it provides rear access — this is also why the "intersection" definition in 541.303(a) expressly excludes the junction of an alley and a highway.

---

### Q30 — Traffic & Traffic Control Definitions
**LO:** 22.3 · **Source:** TC 541.302(3), (4)

A "______" is a divided, controlled-access highway for through traffic, and a "______" is a lane on such a highway having an uninterrupted flow of through traffic.

**Answer:** Freeway | Freeway Main Lane

**Explanation:** A freeway is the whole controlled-access highway; a freeway main lane is specifically the uninterrupted-flow lane on it, as distinct from an entrance/exit ramp.

---

### Q31 — Traffic & Traffic Control Definitions
**LO:** 22.3 · **Source:** TC 541.302(8)

Under TC 541.302(8), a "limited-access or controlled-access highway" is one where:

- [x] Access is restricted under law except at a place and manner determined by the authority with jurisdiction
- [ ] Only commercial vehicles may enter
- [ ] The speed limit is set above 70 mph
- [ ] Pedestrians have unrestricted right of access

**Explanation:** The defining feature is restricted access — persons, including abutting property owners, have no automatic right of access; entry/exit is controlled by the jurisdictional authority.

---

### Q32 — Traffic & Traffic Control Definitions
**LO:** 22.3 · **Source:** TC 541.302(12)

A "______" is the area in a roadway officially designated for exclusive pedestrian use and that is protected or so marked or indicated by adequate signs as to be plainly visible at all times while so designated.

**Answer:** Safety Zone

**Explanation:** TC 545.403 makes it a separate offense for an operator to drive through or in a safety zone.

---

### Q33 — Traffic & Traffic Control Definitions
**LO:** 22.3 · **Source:** TC 541.302(13), (14)

Under TC 541.302, which statement correctly distinguishes a "school crossing zone" from a "school crosswalk"?

- [x] A school crossing zone is a reduced-speed zone; a school crosswalk is the marked crossing itself
- [ ] They are two names for the exact same thing
- [ ] A school crosswalk is only found outside a municipality
- [ ] A school crossing zone applies only to private schools

**Explanation:** 541.302(13) defines "school crossing zone" as a reduced-speed zone designated to facilitate safe crossing; 541.302(14) defines "school crosswalk" as the crosswalk itself designated for that purpose — related but distinct concepts.

---

### Q34 — Traffic & Traffic Control Definitions
**LO:** 22.3 · **Source:** TC 541.302(15)

A "______" is the portion of a highway adjacent to the roadway that is designed or ordinarily used for parking, is distinguished from the roadway by different design, construction, or marking, and is not intended for normal vehicular travel.

**Answer:** Shoulder

**Explanation:** An "improved shoulder" (541.302(6)) is simply a paved shoulder — the base "shoulder" definition does not require pavement.

---

### Q35 — Traffic & Traffic Control Definitions
**LO:** 22.3 · **Source:** TC 542.202(b)(2)

Under TC 542.202(b)(2), a "through highway" is one on which:

- [x] Vehicular traffic is given preferential right-of-way and entering traffic must yield in compliance with an official traffic-control device
- [ ] All traffic must come to a complete stop before entering
- [ ] Only one direction of travel is permitted
- [ ] Speed limits cannot be altered by a local authority

**Explanation:** Designating a highway as a "through highway" is one of the specific powers local authorities retain under 542.202(a)(9); the definition itself lives in 542.202(b)(2).

---

### Q36 — Traffic & Traffic Control Definitions
**LO:** 22.3 · **Source:** TC 541.303(a)

"______" means the common area at the junction of two highways, other than the junction of an ______ and a highway.

**Answer:** Intersection | alley

**Explanation:** The alley exclusion matters because alleys are defined (541.302(1)) as not used primarily for through traffic — an alley/highway junction is treated differently from a true intersection for right-of-way purposes.

---

### Q37 — Traffic & Traffic Control Definitions
**LO:** 22.3 · **Source:** TC 541.303(c)

Under TC 541.303(c), if a highway includes two roadways at least how many feet apart, each junction with an intersecting highway is treated as a separate intersection?

- [x] 30 feet
- [ ] 15 feet
- [ ] 50 feet
- [ ] 100 feet

**Explanation:** The 30-foot separation threshold in 541.303(c) also appears in TC 542.204, which lets the commission and local authorities treat divided-highway intersections with medians at least 30 feet apart as components of a single intersection for engineering purposes.

---

### Q38 — Traffic & Traffic Control Definitions
**LO:** 22.3 · **Source:** TC 541.304(1)

An "______" is a sign, signal, marking, or device that is consistent with the subtitle, placed or erected by a public body or officer having jurisdiction, and used to regulate, warn, or guide traffic.

**Answer:** Official Traffic-Control Device

**Explanation:** Only a device meeting all three elements — proper authority, proper purpose, and consistency with the Transportation Code — counts as "official"; TC 544.006 separately makes it an offense to display unauthorized signs that imitate one.

---

### Q39 — Traffic & Traffic Control Definitions
**LO:** 22.3 · **Source:** TC 541.304(3)

Under TC 541.304(3), a "traffic-control signal" is defined as:

- [x] A manual, electric, or mechanical device that alternately directs traffic to stop and to proceed
- [ ] Any sign that displays a speed limit
- [ ] A device used exclusively at railroad crossings
- [ ] A hand signal given by a police officer

**Explanation:** This is the general definition that TC 544.007 (traffic-control signals in general) and TC 544.008 (flashing signals) both build on.

---

### Q40 — Traffic & Traffic Control Definitions
**LO:** 22.3 · **Source:** TC 541.302(9)

A "______" is a privately owned way or place used for vehicular travel and used only by the owner and persons who have the owner's express or implied permission.

**Answer:** Private Road Or Driveway

**Explanation:** This term is central to right-of-way rules like TC 545.155, which requires yielding when entering a highway from a private road or driveway.

---

### Q41 — Traffic & Traffic Control Definitions
**LO:** 22.3 · **Source:** TC 541.302(2)

Under TC 541.302(2), a "crosswalk" includes which of the following?

- [x] The portion of a roadway at an intersection within the connections of the lateral lines of the sidewalks on opposite sides of the highway
- [ ] Any part of a highway where pedestrians commonly cross informally
- [ ] Only crossings marked with overhead signals
- [ ] Only crossings located at least 100 feet from an intersection

**Explanation:** A "crosswalk" can be either a surface-marked pedestrian crossing or, in the absence of markings, the unmarked area at an intersection within the extended lateral lines of the sidewalks.

---

### Q42 — Miscellaneous Chapter 541 Definitions
**LO:** 22.4 · **Source:** TC 541.401(1), (5)

"______" is the period beginning one-half hour before sunrise and ending one-half hour after sunset; "______" is the period beginning one-half hour after sunset and ending one-half hour before sunrise.

**Answer:** Daytime | Nighttime

**Explanation:** These half-hour buffers matter for equipment rules like headlamp requirements and the special speed limitation in TC 545.361(a) for mopeds and small motorcycles at night.

---

### Q43 — Miscellaneous Chapter 541 Definitions
**LO:** 22.4 · **Source:** TC 541.401(4)

Under TC 541.401(4), "gross vehicle weight" means:

- [x] The weight of a vehicle and the weight of its load
- [ ] The manufacturer's rated carrying capacity only
- [ ] The empty weight of the vehicle without any load
- [ ] The weight limit posted on a bridge sign

**Explanation:** "Gross vehicle weight" combines the vehicle's own weight with whatever it is carrying — distinct from "gross vehicle weight rating," which is the manufacturer's specified maximum loaded weight for a single vehicle (defined in Chapter 522).

---

### Q44 — Miscellaneous Chapter 541 Definitions
**LO:** 22.4 · **Source:** TC 541.401(6)

"______" or "______" means to stand an occupied or unoccupied vehicle, other than temporarily while loading or unloading merchandise or passengers.

**Answer:** Park | Parking

**Explanation:** This is one of the terms defined jointly under two names in the same subsection — "park" and "parking" are legally interchangeable.

---

### Q45 — Miscellaneous Chapter 541 Definitions
**LO:** 22.4 · **Source:** TC 541.401(7)

Under TC 541.401(7), a "personal injury" is defined as:

- [x] An injury to any part of the human body that requires treatment
- [ ] Any injury resulting in hospitalization
- [ ] Only an injury that creates a substantial risk of death
- [ ] Damage to personal property in a collision

**Explanation:** "Personal injury" requires treatment but does not need to rise to the level of "bodily injury" or "serious bodily injury" as defined in the Penal Code.

---

### Q46 — Miscellaneous Chapter 541 Definitions
**LO:** 22.4 · **Source:** TC 541.401(8)

"______" means the right of one vehicle or pedestrian to proceed in a lawful manner in preference to another vehicle or pedestrian that is approaching from a direction, at a speed, and within a proximity that could cause a collision unless one grants precedence to the other.

**Answer:** Right-Of-Way

**Explanation:** This definition underlies every yield rule in Chapter 545, Subchapter D — right-of-way is a legal preference to proceed, not an absolute right.

---

### Q47 — Miscellaneous Chapter 541 Definitions
**LO:** 22.4 · **Source:** TC 541.401(9)

Under TC 541.401(9), "stand" or "standing" means to halt an occupied or unoccupied vehicle, other than temporarily while:

- [x] Receiving or discharging passengers
- [ ] Loading or unloading merchandise
- [ ] Waiting at a red traffic signal
- [ ] Refueling at a gas station

**Explanation:** Compare this to "park"/"parking," which carves out an exception for loading/unloading merchandise or passengers, while "stand"/"standing" carves out only receiving/discharging passengers.

---

### Q48 — Miscellaneous Chapter 541 Definitions
**LO:** 22.4 · **Source:** TC 541.401(10)

"______" or "______" means, when required, to completely cease movement, and when prohibited, to halt (including momentarily halting) an occupied or unoccupied vehicle, unless necessary to avoid conflict with other traffic or to comply with a police officer or traffic-control sign or signal.

**Answer:** Stop | Stopping

**Explanation:** This dual definition is why a momentary halt to avoid a collision or to obey an officer's directions isn't a prohibited "stop" even in a no-stopping zone.

---

### Q49 — Miscellaneous Chapter 541 Definitions
**LO:** 22.4 · **Source:** TC 541.401(2), (3)

Which pair of terms from TC 541.401 relates to hazardous cargo carried across a railroad grade crossing under TC 545.254?

- [x] Explosive and Flammable Liquid
- [ ] Daytime and Nighttime
- [ ] Personal Injury and Right-of-Way
- [ ] Park and Stand

**Explanation:** TC 545.254 governs vehicles carrying an "explosive substance or flammable liquid" as principal cargo — both "explosive" (541.401(2)) and "flammable liquid" (541.401(3), a liquid with a flash point of not more than 70°F) are defined in the same miscellaneous-terms section.

---

### Q50 — Litter & Illegal Dumping
**LO:** 22.5 · **Source:** HSC 365.011(5)

"______" and "______" mean to discharge, deposit, inject, spill, leak, or place litter on or into land or water.

**Answer:** Dispose | Dump

**Explanation:** These two words are defined identically and interchangeably in the same subsection.

---

### Q51 — Litter & Illegal Dumping
**LO:** 22.5 · **Source:** HSC 365.011(6)

Under HSC 365.011(6), "litter" includes nondecayable solid waste consisting of noncombustible material that does not burn at ordinary incinerator temperatures of:

- [x] 1,800 degrees Fahrenheit or less
- [ ] 1,000 degrees Fahrenheit or less
- [ ] 2,500 degrees Fahrenheit or less
- [ ] 500 degrees Fahrenheit or less

**Explanation:** The 1,800°F figure distinguishes noncombustible litter (glass, crockery, tin/aluminum cans, metal furniture) from combustible litter (paper, rags, wood, yard trimmings) under the same subsection.

---

### Q52 — Litter & Illegal Dumping
**LO:** 22.5 · **Source:** HSC 365.011(8)

"______" means the entire width between property lines of a road, street, way, thoroughfare, bridge, public beach, or park not privately owned or controlled if any part is opened to the public for vehicular traffic, used as a public recreational area, or under the state's legislative jurisdiction through its police power.

**Answer:** Public Highway

**Explanation:** This HSC definition of "public highway" is notably broader than the TC 541.302(5) "highway or street" definition — it explicitly reaches public beaches and parks, not just roads.

---

### Q53 — Litter & Illegal Dumping
**LO:** 22.5 · **Source:** HSC 365.012(d), (e)

Under HSC 365.012, an illegal-dumping offense involving litter weighing five pounds or less (or five gallons or less by volume) is classified as a:

- [x] Class C misdemeanor
- [ ] Class B misdemeanor
- [ ] Class A misdemeanor
- [ ] State jail felony

**Explanation:** 365.012(d) sets the smallest tier (≤5 lbs/≤5 gal) as a Class C misdemeanor; (e) raises it to a Class B misdemeanor once the weight or volume exceeds that threshold but stays under 500 lbs/100 cu ft.

---

### Q54 — Litter & Illegal Dumping
**LO:** 22.5 · **Source:** HSC 365.012(g)

Under HSC 365.012(g), illegally dumped litter or solid waste weighing ______ pounds or more, or having a volume of ______ cubic feet or more, makes the offense a state jail felony.

**Answer:** 1,000 | 200

**Explanation:** The same 1,000-lb/200-cu-ft threshold (or dumping for a commercial purpose at 200 lbs/200 cu ft or more, or dumping in a closed barrel or drum) elevates illegal dumping to a state jail felony under 365.012(g).

---

### Q55 — Litter & Illegal Dumping
**LO:** 22.5 · **Source:** HSC 365.012(a-1)

Under HSC 365.012(a-1), discarding lighted litter such as a match, cigarette, or cigar onto a public highway or right-of-way is a separate offense specifically when:

- [x] A fire is ignited as a result of the conduct
- [ ] The litter weighs more than 5 pounds
- [ ] The act occurs within a school zone
- [ ] The person has a prior conviction under the section

**Explanation:** 365.012(a-1) creates a distinct offense from the general illegal-dumping offense in (a) — it requires that discarding the lighted material actually ignite a fire, not merely that lighted litter was discarded.

---

### Q56 — Traffic Law Obedience & Effect of the Code
**LO:** 22.6 · **Source:** TC 542.001

A provision of Subtitle C relating to the operation of a vehicle applies only to the operation of a vehicle on a ______ unless the provision specifically applies to a different place.

**Answer:** highway

**Explanation:** This threshold rule in 542.001 is why TC 545.401 (reckless driving) has to specifically extend itself to private parking areas and work zones in its own text — otherwise it would only apply on highways.

---

### Q57 — Traffic Law Obedience & Effect of the Code
**LO:** 22.6 · **Source:** TC 542.002

Under TC 542.002, the rules of the road applicable to vehicle operators apply to the operator of a government-owned vehicle:

- [x] The same as any other operator, except as specifically provided otherwise for an authorized emergency vehicle
- [ ] Only if the vehicle is marked with government insignia
- [ ] Never — government vehicles are exempt from Subtitle C
- [ ] Only within the jurisdiction that owns the vehicle

**Explanation:** Government ownership alone does not create an exemption; only the specific authorized-emergency-vehicle exceptions found elsewhere in the subtitle (like TC 546.001) modify the rules for government vehicles.

---

### Q58 — Traffic Law Obedience & Effect of the Code
**LO:** 22.6 · **Source:** TC 542.003

A person riding an animal on a roadway or operating an animal-drawn vehicle has the rights and duties applicable to the ______ of a vehicle, except for a right or duty that by its nature cannot apply to a person riding an animal or operating an animal-drawn vehicle.

**Answer:** operator

**Explanation:** This provision extends most rules-of-the-road obligations to horseback riders and animal-drawn wagons, carving out only the rules that logically cannot apply to them.

---

### Q59 — Traffic Law Obedience & Effect of the Code
**LO:** 22.6 · **Source:** TC 542.004

Under TC 542.004, Subtitle C does not apply to a person, team, or equipment engaged in work on a highway surface, except:

- [x] While those persons and vehicles are traveling to or from that work
- [ ] During daylight hours only
- [ ] If the work zone lacks warning signs
- [ ] If the highway is state-maintained

**Explanation:** The work-crew exemption from ordinary traffic rules ends the moment the crew or vehicle is traveling to or from the job site — those trips remain subject to Subtitle C.

---

### Q60 — Traffic Law Obedience & Effect of the Code
**LO:** 22.6 · **Source:** TC 542.005

Section 542.005 does not prevent an owner of private property that is a private road from ______ use of the property by the public for vehicular travel, or requiring conditions different from or in addition to those specified by the subtitle.

**Answer:** regulating or prohibiting

**Explanation:** A private landowner retains authority to control or bar public vehicular access on a private road, even though Subtitle C otherwise applies uniformly to highways.

---

### Q61 — Traffic Law Obedience & Effect of the Code
**LO:** 22.6 · **Source:** TC 542.201

Under TC 542.201, the "general rule of uniformity" means a local authority:

- [x] May not enact or enforce an ordinance that conflicts with Subtitle C unless expressly authorized to do so
- [ ] May never regulate traffic in any manner
- [ ] Must adopt the exact wording of state traffic law
- [ ] May only regulate traffic on state highways

**Explanation:** A local authority is free to regulate traffic in ways that don't conflict with Subtitle C — the uniformity rule only blocks ordinances that directly conflict, absent express statutory authorization.

---

### Q62 — Traffic Law Obedience & Effect of the Code
**LO:** 22.6 · **Source:** TC 542.202(a)

Under TC 542.202(a), a local authority may, in the reasonable exercise of the police power, designate a highway as a ______ highway or as a ______-way highway requiring vehicles to move in one specific direction.

**Answer:** through | one

**Explanation:** These are two of the thirteen specific powers 542.202(a) preserves for local authorities, alongside regulating stopping/standing/parking, designating stop or yield intersections, and designating school crossing guards and zones.

---

### Q63 — Traffic Law Obedience & Effect of the Code
**LO:** 22.6 · **Source:** TC 542.301

Under TC 542.301, unless otherwise provided, an offense under Subtitle C is a:

- [x] Misdemeanor
- [ ] Class A misdemeanor specifically
- [ ] State jail felony
- [ ] Civil infraction only, with no criminal penalty

**Explanation:** 542.301(b) sets the default classification as a misdemeanor; specific sections (like TC 545.066, passing a school bus) provide their own enhanced classifications and fine ranges that override this default.

---

### Q64 — Traffic Law Obedience & Effect of the Code
**LO:** 22.6 · **Source:** TC 542.302

A person who owns a vehicle or employs or directs the operator of a vehicle commits an offense if the person requires or ______ permits the operator to operate the vehicle in a manner that violates law.

**Answer:** knowingly

**Explanation:** Liability under 542.302 attaches to the owner/employer's own culpable mental state (requiring or knowingly permitting), not merely to the fact that the operator violated the law.

---

### Q65 — Traffic Law Obedience & Effect of the Code
**LO:** 22.6 · **Source:** TC 542.303

Under TC 542.303, a person who attempts to commit or conspires to commit an act declared to be an offense under Subtitle C is:

- [x] Guilty of the offense itself
- [ ] Guilty of a lesser included offense only
- [ ] Not criminally liable unless the offense is completed
- [ ] Liable only in a civil action

**Explanation:** 542.303 treats an inchoate offense (attempt or conspiracy) under the subtitle the same as completing the offense — there is no separate, lesser "attempt" charge here.

---

### Q66 — Traffic Law Obedience & Effect of the Code
**LO:** 22.6 · **Source:** TC 542.401

A person convicted of a Subtitle C misdemeanor for which another penalty is not provided is punished by a fine of not less than $______ or more than $______.

**Answer:** 1 | 200

**Explanation:** This is the default "general penalty" fine range under 542.401 that applies whenever a more specific fine range is not written into the offense itself.

---

### Q67 — Traffic Law Obedience & Effect of the Code
**LO:** 22.6 · **Source:** TC 542.404

Under TC 542.404, an offense committed in a marked construction or maintenance work zone when workers are present, with the citation stating workers were present:

- [x] Doubles both the minimum and maximum fine that would otherwise apply
- [ ] Adds a flat $500 penalty on top of the base fine
- [ ] Automatically becomes a Class A misdemeanor
- [ ] Only doubles the maximum fine, not the minimum

**Explanation:** 542.404(a) doubles both ends of the fine range for most Subtitle C offenses (with some exceptions, like Chapter 548/552 equipment offenses) when committed in an active, marked work zone.

---

### Q68 — Traffic Law Obedience & Effect of the Code
**LO:** 22.6 · **Source:** TC 542.4045

If a failure to yield the right-of-way results in a collision, the offense is punishable by a fine of $______ to $______ if another person suffered bodily injury, or $______ to $______ if another person suffered serious bodily injury.

**Answer:** 500 | 2,000 | 1,000 | 4,000

**Explanation:** TC 542.4045 layers an enhanced fine schedule onto any failure-to-yield offense where the collision actually caused bodily injury or serious bodily injury (as those terms are defined in Penal Code 1.07).

---

### Q69 — Traffic Law Obedience & Effect of the Code
**LO:** 22.6 · **Source:** TC 542.501

Under TC 542.501, a person may not wilfully fail or refuse to comply with a lawful order or direction of a police officer, a school crossing guard performing crossing-guard duties, or:

- [x] An escort flagger directing traffic under a Texas DMV oversize/overweight vehicle permit
- [ ] Any private security guard
- [ ] A tow truck operator at a collision scene
- [ ] A volunteer traffic monitor without department training

**Explanation:** 542.501 names exactly three categories entitled to this obedience protection: police officers, trained school crossing guards, and escort flaggers directing traffic under a Department of Motor Vehicles oversize/overweight permit.

---

### Q70 — Traffic Law Obedience & Effect of the Code
**LO:** 22.6 · **Source:** TC 542.202(b)(3)

Under TC 542.202(b)(3), "______" means criminal, civil, and administrative enforcement against a person, including the owner or operator of a motor vehicle, in accordance with a state law or a municipal ordinance.

**Answer:** Regulating

**Explanation:** This definition clarifies exactly what "regulating" traffic (as used throughout 542.202(a)'s list of local-authority powers) legally encompasses.

---

### Q71 — Traffic Law Obedience & Effect of the Code
**LO:** 22.6 · **Source:** TC 542.203(b)

Under TC 542.203(b), a local ordinance or rule that alters a speed limit, designates a stop or yield intersection, or designates a highway as one-way or through is not effective until:

- [x] Signs giving notice are posted at or on the entrance to the affected highway or part of it
- [ ] It is published in a newspaper of general circulation
- [ ] It is approved by the Texas Transportation Commission
- [ ] 30 days have passed since adoption

**Explanation:** Posted signage — not publication or state approval — is what makes these specific categories of local traffic ordinances enforceable under 542.203(b).

---

### Q72 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 521.001(a)(6)

"______" means an authorization to operate a motor vehicle issued or granted under the laws of this state, including a driver's license, the privilege to operate a motor vehicle regardless of whether a license is held, and a nonresident's operating privilege.

**Answer:** License

**Explanation:** "License" is broader than "driver's license" (521.001(a)(3)) — it also covers the underlying legal privilege to drive, which is what actually gets suspended or revoked even when no physical card exists.

---

### Q73 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 521.001(a)(7)

Under TC 521.001(a)(7), a "nonresident" is defined as:

- [ ] A person who has lived in Texas for less than 90 days
- [x] A person who is not a resident of this state
- [ ] Any person licensed by another state
- [ ] A person on active military duty stationed in Texas

**Explanation:** This is a simple definitional cross-reference — "nonresident" just means not a Texas resident, which then triggers the specific reciprocal-license and new-resident rules elsewhere in Chapter 521.

---

### Q74 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 521.025

A person required to hold a driver's license must have it in their possession while operating a motor vehicle and display it on demand of a magistrate, court officer, or ______ officer.

**Answer:** peace

**Explanation:** TC 521.025 sets criminal penalties (starting at a fine not to exceed $200) for failing to carry or display a license, though it also provides a "produce it in court" defense.

---

### Q75 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 521.026

Under TC 521.026, a judge may dismiss a charge of driving with an expired license if the defendant remedies the defect within:

- [x] 20 working days, or before the first court appearance, whichever is later
- [ ] 10 calendar days
- [ ] 30 calendar days
- [ ] 90 days

**Explanation:** The judge may also assess a reimbursement fee not to exceed $20 when dismissing an expired-license charge under this section.

---

### Q76 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 521.027(2)

A person is exempt from the driver's license requirement while operating a road machine, farm tractor, or ______ of husbandry on a highway, unless the vehicle is a commercial motor vehicle.

**Answer:** implement

**Explanation:** This license exemption in 521.027(2) tracks the same "implement of husbandry" concept defined in TC 541.201(6) — but the exemption disappears if the vehicle happens to meet the Chapter 522 commercial motor vehicle definition.

---

### Q77 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 521.028

Under TC 521.028, a Texas driver's license held by someone on active military duty and absent from the state remains valid, and after an honorable discharge remains valid until the earlier of the person's return to the state or:

- [x] The 91st day after the date of discharge
- [ ] The 30th day after the date of discharge
- [ ] The 180th day after the date of discharge
- [ ] The original expiration date printed on the license

**Explanation:** The 91-day grace period after honorable discharge is specific to this military-service exception.

---

### Q78 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 521.029

A new Texas resident who is 16 years of age or older may operate a motor vehicle for no more than ______ days after entering the state on the strength of a driver's license from their prior state or country of residence.

**Answer:** 90

**Explanation:** This 90-day grace period lets new residents keep driving on an out-of-state license briefly before Texas licensing requirements apply.

---

### Q79 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 521.144

Under TC 521.144, a new Texas resident applying for a driver's license must submit with the application:

- [x] Evidence the applicant's vehicle is registered under Chapter 502, or an affidavit that the applicant owns no vehicle requiring registration
- [ ] Proof of Texas vehicle insurance only
- [ ] A copy of the applicant's out-of-state license
- [ ] A notarized letter from a Texas resident vouching for the applicant

**Explanation:** The department may not issue a license to a new resident who fails to comply with this vehicle-registration/affidavit requirement.

---

### Q80 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 521.081

Under TC 521.081, a Class A driver's license authorizes operating a combination of vehicles with a gross combination weight rating of ______ pounds or more, if the gross vehicle weight rating of any towed vehicle exceeds ______ pounds.

**Answer:** 26,001 | 10,000

**Explanation:** Both figures must be met together — the 26,001-lb combination threshold and the more-than-10,000-lb towed-vehicle threshold — for the Class A license to be required.

---

### Q81 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 521.082

Under TC 521.082, a Class B driver's license authorizes operating a bus with a seating capacity, including the operator's seat, of:

- [x] 24 passengers or more
- [ ] 10 passengers or more
- [ ] 16 passengers or more
- [ ] 15 passengers or more

**Explanation:** This 24-passenger figure for a Class B license mirrors the same threshold used for the Class B commercial driver's license under TC 522.041(c)(3).

---

### Q82 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 521.083, 521.084

A Class ______ driver's license authorizes operating a vehicle or combination not described by Class A or Class B, while a Class ______ license authorizes operating a motorcycle.

**Answer:** C | M

**Explanation:** Class C is essentially the standard passenger-vehicle license (the residual category), while Class M is the separate motorcycle endorsement/license required under 521.084.

---

### Q83 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 521.101(c)

Under TC 521.101(c), the department must indicate what specific notice on the face of a personal identification certificate issued to a person under 21 years of age?

- [x] "UNDER 21"
- [ ] "MINOR"
- [ ] "NOT VALID FOR ALCOHOL PURCHASE"
- [ ] "TEMPORARY"

**Explanation:** This visible age-marking requirement applies to the personal identification certificate, which must be similar in form to but distinguishable in color from a driver's license.

---

### Q84 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 521.183

A person is not entitled to receive a driver's license until the person surrenders to the department each personal identification ______ in the person's possession that was issued by this state (and vice versa for receiving a certificate).

**Answer:** certificate

**Explanation:** TC 521.183 prevents a person from holding both a Texas driver's license and a Texas personal identification certificate at the same time — it does not itself create a criminal offense, but is an administrative directive to the department.

---

### Q85 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 521.201(1), (2)

Under TC 521.201, the department may not issue any license to a person who is:

- [x] Under 15 years of age
- [ ] Under 21 years of age, with no exceptions
- [ ] A first-time applicant of any age
- [ ] Over 85 years of age

**Explanation:** 521.201(1) sets an absolute floor of 15 years of age; 521.201(2) additionally bars anyone under 18 unless they comply with the minor-driver requirements of Section 521.204.

---

### Q86 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 521.222(a), (d)

A learner license may be issued to a person 15 years of age or older but under 18, and entitles the holder to drive only while accompanied by a person who holds a qualifying license, is ______ years of age or older, and has at least ______ year(s) of driving experience.

**Answer:** 21 | 1

**Explanation:** All three conditions — qualifying license, minimum age 21, and at least one year of driving experience — must be met by the accompanying supervisor under 521.222(d)(2).

---

### Q87 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 521.223(a)

Under TC 521.223(a), the department may issue a hardship license to a person 15 years of age or older if failure to issue the license would cause unusual economic hardship for the family, the license is necessary due to illness of a family member, or:

- [x] The applicant is enrolled in a vocational education program requiring a license to participate
- [ ] The applicant's parents both work full time
- [ ] The applicant lives more than 10 miles from school
- [ ] The applicant has already passed the driving test

**Explanation:** These are the three specific statutory grounds for a hardship license under 521.223(a) — economic hardship, family illness, or a vocational education program requirement.

---

### Q88 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 521.241(1)

Under TC 521.241(1), "______ need" means a need of a person to operate a motor vehicle in the performance of an occupation or trade, in the pursuit of an occupation or trade, for transportation to an educational facility, or in performing essential household duties.

**Answer:** essential

**Explanation:** "Essential need" is the specific standard an occupational driver's license petitioner must establish under Section 521.244 before a judge will grant the petition.

---

### Q89 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 521.242(b)

Under TC 521.242(b), an occupational driver's license:

- [x] Does not authorize operation of a commercial motor vehicle under Chapter 522
- [ ] Authorizes operation of any vehicle the person previously drove
- [ ] Is valid only during daytime hours
- [ ] Requires no restrictions of any kind

**Explanation:** Even a person who once held a CDL cannot use an occupational driver's license to operate a commercial motor vehicle — Section 521.2424 reinforces this by barring courts from granting an occupational license for CMV operation.

---

### Q90 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 521.248(b)

A person operating under an occupational driver's license may not operate a motor vehicle for more than ______ hours in any 24-hour period, except that on a showing of necessity the court may allow up to ______ hours.

**Answer:** four | 12

**Explanation:** These hour limits do not apply, however, to a person restricted to driving only a vehicle equipped with an ignition interlock device, per 521.248(d).

---

### Q91 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 521.271(a)

Under TC 521.271(a), an original Texas driver's license (other than a provisional, learner, or occupational license) issued to an eligible citizen or lawful permanent resident expires on the license holder's first birthday occurring after the:

- [x] Eighth anniversary of the date of application
- [ ] Fourth anniversary of the date of application
- [ ] Sixth anniversary of the date of application
- [ ] Tenth anniversary of the date of application

**Explanation:** The standard 8-year renewal cycle is set by 521.271(a)(1); provisional and learner licenses instead expire on the holder's 18th birthday, and occupational licenses expire after one year.

---

### Q92 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 521.2711(a)

Under TC 521.2711(a), each original driver's license of a person ______ years of age or older expires on the license holder's second birthday after the date of the license application.

**Answer:** 85

**Explanation:** This shortened 2-year renewal cycle for drivers 85 and older (compared to the standard 8-year cycle) allows more frequent medical/vision review.

---

### Q93 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 521.292(b)

Under TC 521.292(b), a person is a "habitual violator" of traffic laws if the person has how many convictions arising out of different transactions within 12 consecutive months?

- [x] Four or more
- [ ] Two or more
- [ ] Six or more
- [ ] Ten or more

**Explanation:** The alternate threshold is seven or more convictions arising out of different transactions within 24 months — either satisfies the "habitual violator" definition that triggers mandatory suspension under 521.292(a)(3).

---

### Q94 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 521.350(a), (b)

A license is automatically suspended for ______ year(s) on conviction of the racing offense under TC 545.420(a).

**Answer:** one

**Explanation:** In addition to the one-year suspension, TC 521.350(d) requires the convicting court to order at least 10 hours of community service.

---

### Q95 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 521.451(a), (b)

Under TC 521.451, knowingly possessing a fictitious or altered driver's license, lending your license to another person, or possessing more than one currently valid driver's license is punishable as a:

- [x] Class A misdemeanor
- [ ] Class C misdemeanor
- [ ] Class B misdemeanor
- [ ] State jail felony

**Explanation:** 521.451(b) classifies this "general violation" offense as a Class A misdemeanor, distinct from the narrower Class C misdemeanor for a minor possessing a fictitious ID under 521.453.

---

### Q96 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 521.457(a), (e)

Under TC 521.457, operating a motor vehicle while your license is suspended, revoked, canceled, or expired-during-suspension is generally punishable as a Class ______ misdemeanor, absent an enhancement.

**Answer:** C

**Explanation:** 521.457(f) enhances this to a Class B misdemeanor for a prior conviction or a financial-responsibility violation, and 521.457(f-2) enhances it further to a Class A misdemeanor if the person was also violating the financial-responsibility law and caused a collision resulting in serious bodily injury or death.

---

### Q97 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 521.461(b)

Under TC 521.461, the general criminal penalty for violating a Chapter 521 provision for which a specific penalty is not otherwise provided is a misdemeanor punishable by a fine not to exceed:

- [x] $200
- [ ] $500
- [ ] $1,000
- [ ] $2,000

**Explanation:** This $200 general-penalty ceiling in Chapter 521 mirrors the $200 general-penalty ceiling for Subtitle C rules-of-the-road offenses under TC 542.401.

---

### Q98 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 522.003(21)

Under TC 522.003(21), for purposes of the Commercial Driver's License Act, a "______ vehicle" means a vehicle, machine, tractor, trailer, or semitrailer propelled or drawn by mechanical power and used on a highway, excluding one operated exclusively on a rail.

**Answer:** motor

**Explanation:** Chapter 522 defines "motor vehicle" independently from — though similarly to — the Chapter 541 definition, for purposes of determining what counts as a "commercial motor vehicle."

---

### Q99 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 522.004(a)(1)

Under TC 522.004(a)(1), the Commercial Driver's License Act does not apply to a vehicle controlled and operated by a farmer that is used to transport agricultural products, farm machinery, or farm supplies to or from a farm and used within how many miles of the farm?

- [x] 150 miles
- [ ] 50 miles
- [ ] 100 miles
- [ ] 250 miles

**Explanation:** This 150-mile radius, combined with the requirement that the vehicle not be used in common or contract motor carrier operations, defines the farm-vehicle exemption from CDL requirements.

---

### Q100 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 522.011(a)

A person may not drive a commercial motor vehicle unless the person has in immediate possession a commercial driver's license appropriate for the vehicle's class and is not ______ or subject to an out-of-service order (or is a permit holder properly accompanied).

**Answer:** disqualified

**Explanation:** Violating this section is a misdemeanor punishable by a fine not to exceed $500 (up to $1,000 for a repeat offense within the preceding year) under 522.011(c).

---

### Q101 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 522.041(b)

Under TC 522.041(b), Commercial Driver's License Class A covers a combination of vehicles with a gross combination weight rating of 26,001 pounds or more, if the gross vehicle weight rating of the towed vehicle exceeds:

- [x] 10,000 pounds
- [ ] 5,000 pounds
- [ ] 20,000 pounds
- [ ] 26,000 pounds

**Explanation:** This CDL Class A threshold under Chapter 522 exactly parallels the noncommercial Class A driver's license threshold under TC 521.081.

---

### Q102 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 522.042(a)(6)

Under TC 522.042(a)(6), the department may issue a commercial driver's license with an endorsement authorizing the driving of a ______ bus.

**Answer:** school

**Explanation:** 522.042 also authorizes endorsements for hazardous materials, double/triple trailers, passenger-carrying vehicles, and tank vehicles.

---

### Q103 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 522.101(a)

Under TC 522.101(a), a person operating a commercial motor vehicle in Texas is prohibited from having in their system:

- [x] Any measurable or detectable amount of alcohol
- [ ] A blood alcohol concentration of 0.08 or more
- [ ] A blood alcohol concentration of 0.04 or more
- [ ] Alcohol only if combined with a controlled substance

**Explanation:** CDL holders face a "zero tolerance" standard under 522.101(a) — any measurable or detectable amount of alcohol, far stricter than the 0.08 standard that applies to noncommercial drivers — and a violation results in a mandatory 24-hour out-of-service placement.

---

### Q104 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 521.224(b)

Under TC 521.224(b), the department may issue a special restricted Class M license that authorizes the holder to operate only a motorcycle with not more than a ______ cubic centimeter piston displacement.

**Answer:** 250

**Explanation:** This restricted Class M license is available to applicants 15 to under 18 years of age who complete an approved motorcycle training course and meet the requirements of Section 521.145.

---

### Q105 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 521.204(a)

Under TC 521.204(a), the department may issue a Class C driver's license to an applicant under 18 years of age only if, among other things, the applicant is at least:

- [x] 16 years of age
- [ ] 15 years of age
- [ ] 14 years of age
- [ ] 17 years of age

**Explanation:** The applicant must also have a driver education certificate, satisfy school-enrollment requirements, and pass the required examination — the 16-year floor is just one of several conditions in 521.204(a).

---

### Q106 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 521.246(a)

Under TC 521.246(a), a judge shall restrict a person to operating a vehicle equipped with an ignition ______ device if the person's license was suspended, revoked, or canceled after a conviction for intoxication-related driving offenses under Sections 49.04-49.08, Penal Code.

**Answer:** interlock

**Explanation:** This mandatory ignition interlock restriction attaches when granting an occupational driver's license following certain intoxication-related convictions, subject to a good-cause waiver under 521.246(b-1).

---

### Q107 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 522.027

Under TC 522.027, the department may not issue a commercial driver's license or commercial learner's permit to a person younger than:

- [x] 18 years of age
- [ ] 16 years of age
- [ ] 21 years of age
- [ ] 25 years of age

**Explanation:** 18 is the absolute minimum age for any CDL or CLP — note that a separate, higher 21-year-old minimum applies under federal law for interstate hazardous materials or passenger transport, but the Texas statutory floor itself is 18.

---

### Q108 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 522.041(d)

Under TC 522.041(d), CDL Class C covers a single vehicle or combination not described by Class A or B that is designed to transport 16 to 23 passengers, including the driver, or is used to transport ______ materials requiring the vehicle to be placarded.

**Answer:** hazardous

**Explanation:** Either condition — the 16-23 passenger capacity or the hazmat placarding requirement — independently qualifies a vehicle for CDL Class C.

---

### Q109 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 521.253(b)

Under TC 521.253(b), a person who holds an occupational driver's license and operates in violation of a license restriction, or fails to carry the required court order, commits an offense classified as a:

- [x] Class B misdemeanor
- [ ] Class C misdemeanor
- [ ] Class A misdemeanor
- [ ] State jail felony

**Explanation:** On conviction under 521.253, the court must also issue an order revoking the occupational driver's license itself, in addition to the Class B misdemeanor penalty.

---

### Q110 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 521.292(a)(7)

Under TC 521.292(a), the department shall suspend a person's license if the department determines the person has been responsible as a driver for a collision resulting in serious personal injury or serious ______ damage.

**Answer:** property

**Explanation:** This is one of nine independent grounds for mandatory license suspension under 521.292(a), alongside habitual recklessness, being a habitual violator, and driving while already suspended.

---

### Q111 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 521.293

Under TC 521.293, if a person whose license is suspended under Section 521.292 does not request a hearing, the period of suspension is:

- [x] 90 days
- [ ] 30 days
- [ ] 180 days
- [ ] One year

**Explanation:** Requesting a hearing gives the person a chance to contest the suspension before it takes effect; failing to request one locks in the 90-day suspension period.

---

### Q112 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 521.201(3)

Under TC 521.201(3), the department may not issue a license to a person shown to be addicted to alcohol, a controlled substance, or another drug that renders the person incapable of ______.

**Answer:** driving

**Explanation:** This eligibility bar focuses on incapacity to drive safely due to addiction, distinct from the separate physical/mental disability ineligibility ground in 521.201(6).

---

### Q113 — Traffic Signs, Signals & Markings
**LO:** 22.8 · **Source:** TC 544.001

The Texas Transportation Commission shall adopt a manual and specifications for a uniform system of traffic-control devices that correlates with the system approved by the American Association of State Highway and ______ Officials.

**Answer:** Transportation

**Explanation:** This adopted manual is the Texas Manual on Uniform Traffic Control Devices (TMUTCD) referenced throughout BPOC Chapter 22's signage material.

---

### Q114 — Traffic Signs, Signals & Markings
**LO:** 22.8 · **Source:** TC 544.004(a)

Under TC 544.004(a), an operator must comply with an applicable official traffic-control device unless the person is otherwise directed by a traffic officer, police officer, or escort flagger, or is:

- [x] Operating an authorized emergency vehicle subject to exceptions under the subtitle
- [ ] Operating a bicycle
- [ ] Driving during nighttime hours
- [ ] A first-time visitor to the state

**Explanation:** 544.004(b) also provides that a traffic-control device cannot be enforced against a driver if it wasn't in proper position and sufficiently legible to an ordinarily observant person at the time.

---

### Q115 — Traffic Signs, Signals & Markings
**LO:** 22.8 · **Source:** TC 544.005

A person may not, without lawful authority, alter, injure, knock down, or remove an official traffic-control device or railroad sign or signal, or an inscription, ______, or insignia on one.

**Answer:** shield

**Explanation:** This offense covers not just the whole device but its components — inscriptions, shields, and insignias — and any other part of it.

---

### Q116 — Traffic Signs, Signals & Markings
**LO:** 22.8 · **Source:** TC 544.006(c)

Under TC 544.006(c), a person may not place or maintain a flashing light or flashing electric sign within how many feet of an intersection, except under a Texas Transportation Commission permit?

- [x] 1,000 feet
- [ ] 500 feet
- [ ] 200 feet
- [ ] 1,500 feet

**Explanation:** This distance restriction is meant to prevent unauthorized flashing lights or signs from being confused with, or distracting from, official traffic-control signals near an intersection.

---

### Q117 — Traffic Signs, Signals & Markings
**LO:** 22.8 · **Source:** TC 544.007(d)

An operator facing only a steady red signal shall stop at a clearly marked stop line and, after stopping and yielding, may turn right, or may turn left if the intersecting streets are both ______-way streets and a left turn is permissible.

**Answer:** one

**Explanation:** A right turn on red is generally allowed after stopping and yielding; a left turn on red is allowed only in the narrow one-way-to-one-way street scenario described in 544.007(d)(2).

---

### Q118 — Traffic Signs, Signals & Markings
**LO:** 22.8 · **Source:** TC 544.008(a)

Under TC 544.008(a), an operator facing a flashing red signal must stop at a clearly marked stop line, and the right to proceed afterward is subject to:

- [x] The rules applicable after stopping at a stop sign
- [ ] No further restriction — the operator may proceed immediately
- [ ] Waiting a mandatory five seconds
- [ ] The rules applicable to a steady red signal

**Explanation:** A flashing red signal is treated exactly like a stop sign for right-of-way purposes once the operator has stopped, per 544.008(a).

---

### Q119 — Traffic Signs, Signals & Markings
**LO:** 22.8 · **Source:** TC 544.009

If a lane-direction-control signal is placed over an individual lane, a vehicle may travel in a lane over which a ______ signal is shown but may not enter or travel in a lane over which a ______ signal is shown.

**Answer:** green | red

**Explanation:** This governs reversible/contraflow lanes, letting the traveled direction of a lane be flipped by signal rather than by fixed lane markings.

---

### Q120 — Traffic Signs, Signals & Markings
**LO:** 22.8 · **Source:** TC 544.010(c)

Under TC 544.010(c), an operator required to stop at a stop or yield sign shall stop at a clearly marked stop line; in its absence, before entering the crosswalk on the near side of the intersection; and in the absence of both:

- [x] At the place nearest the intersecting roadway where the operator has a view of approaching traffic
- [ ] At least 50 feet before the intersection
- [ ] Anywhere within the intersection itself
- [ ] Only if directed by a police officer

**Explanation:** This three-tier fallback (stop line, then crosswalk, then best-view point) applies identically to both stop signs and yield signs under 544.010.

---

### Q121 — Traffic Signs, Signals & Markings
**LO:** 22.8 · **Source:** TC 544.011

Under TC 544.011, if a highway with more than one lane in the same direction has a sign directing slower traffic out of the farthest left lane, the sign must read "______."

**Answer:** left lane for passing only

**Explanation:** This exact statutory wording requirement ensures uniform signage statewide for passing-lane restrictions.

---

### Q122 — Traffic Signs, Signals & Markings
**LO:** 22.8 · **Source:** TC 544.007(i)

Under TC 544.007(i), if an operator faces a traffic-control signal (other than a freeway entrance ramp control signal or pedestrian hybrid beacon) that displays no indication in any signal head, the operator must stop as if the intersection had:

- [x] A stop sign
- [ ] A yield sign
- [ ] A flashing yellow signal
- [ ] No control device at all

**Explanation:** A completely dark or malfunctioning traffic signal is treated the same as a stop sign under 544.007(i), requiring the operator to stop per Section 544.010.

---

### Q123 — Traffic Signs, Signals & Markings
**LO:** 22.8 · **Source:** TC 544.007(b)

An operator facing a circular green signal may proceed straight or turn right or left unless a sign prohibits the turn, but must yield the right-of-way to other vehicles ______ in the intersection and stop and yield to pedestrians or sidewalk users lawfully in the intersection or an adjacent crosswalk.

**Answer:** lawfully

**Explanation:** A green light is not an absolute right to proceed — it is a conditional permission that still requires yielding to traffic and pedestrians already lawfully using the intersection.

---

### Q124 — Driving on the Right Side & Passing
**LO:** 22.9 · **Source:** TC 545.001(2)

"______" or "______," used in reference to a vehicle, means to overtake and proceed past another vehicle moving in the same direction and return to the original lane of travel, or to attempt that maneuver.

**Answer:** Pass | Passing

**Explanation:** Note that merely attempting the pass-and-return maneuver counts as "passing" under this definition, even if the maneuver isn't completed.

---

### Q125 — Driving on the Right Side & Passing
**LO:** 22.9 · **Source:** TC 545.0605(a)

Under TC 545.0605(a), the operator of a motorcycle is entitled to full use of a lane, may operate two abreast in a single lane with another motorcycle, but may not:

- [x] Operate the motorcycle between lanes of traffic moving in the same direction
- [ ] Use the leftmost lane on a highway
- [ ] Ride at night
- [ ] Carry a passenger

**Explanation:** 545.0605 also bars operating more than two abreast in a single lane and passing a motor vehicle while remaining in the same lane as the vehicle being passed — this section codifies that lane-splitting is prohibited in Texas.

---

### Q126 — Driving on the Right Side & Passing
**LO:** 22.9 · **Source:** TC 545.052(2)

An operator moving in the opposite direction of another vehicle shall move to or remain to the right, and on a roadway wide enough for only one line of vehicle movement in each direction, give the other operator at least ______ of the main traveled portion of the roadway.

**Answer:** one-half

**Explanation:** If giving a full half of the roadway is not possible, the operator must give as much of the roadway as possible under 545.052(2)(B).

---

### Q127 — Driving on the Right Side & Passing
**LO:** 22.9 · **Source:** TC 545.053(b)

Under TC 545.053(b), an operator being passed by another vehicle shall, on audible signal, move or remain to the right in favor of the passing vehicle, and may not:

- [x] Accelerate until completely passed by the passing vehicle
- [ ] Use their turn signal
- [ ] Apply the brakes
- [ ] Move onto the improved shoulder

**Explanation:** This duty not to accelerate while being passed does not apply, per 545.053(c), in situations where passing to the right is permitted.

---

### Q128 — Driving on the Right Side & Passing
**LO:** 22.9 · **Source:** TC 545.054(b)(1)

An operator passing another vehicle using a lane for oncoming traffic shall return to an authorized lane of travel before coming within ______ feet of an approaching vehicle.

**Answer:** 200

**Explanation:** If no oncoming-traffic lane is being used to pass, the operator must instead return to the proper lane "as soon as practicable" rather than at a fixed distance.

---

### Q129 — Driving on the Right Side & Passing
**LO:** 22.9 · **Source:** TC 545.056(a)

Under TC 545.056(a), an operator may not drive to the left side of the roadway when approaching within how many feet of an intersection, railroad grade crossing, or bridge/viaduct/tunnel (in a municipality, or outside one if marked)?

- [x] 100 feet
- [ ] 50 feet
- [ ] 200 feet
- [ ] 500 feet

**Explanation:** This 100-foot buffer zone before intersections, marked railroad crossings, and bridges/tunnels prevents passing maneuvers in the most hazard-prone approach areas.

---

### Q130 — Driving on the Right Side & Passing
**LO:** 22.9 · **Source:** TC 545.057(a)

An operator may pass to the right of another vehicle only if conditions permit safely passing to the right and the vehicle being passed is making or about to make a ______ turn.

**Answer:** left

**Explanation:** Passing on the right is otherwise prohibited by leaving the main traveled portion of the roadway, except as separately allowed on an improved shoulder under Section 545.058.

---

### Q131 — Driving on the Right Side & Passing
**LO:** 22.9 · **Source:** TC 545.058(a)

Under TC 545.058(a), an operator may drive on an improved shoulder to the right of the main traveled roadway only for specific listed purposes, which include all of the following except:

- [x] To pass another vehicle traveling at normal speed in the main lane
- [ ] To stop, stand, or park
- [ ] To accelerate before entering the main traveled lane
- [ ] To avoid a collision

**Explanation:** 545.058(a)(4) allows driving on the shoulder to pass a vehicle that is slowing, stopped, disabled, or preparing to turn left — not to pass a vehicle simply traveling normally in the main lane.

---

### Q132 — Driving on the Right Side & Passing
**LO:** 22.9 · **Source:** TC 545.059(c)

An operator moving around a rotary traffic island shall drive only to the ______ of the island.

**Answer:** right

**Explanation:** This simple rule governs traffic circles and rotary intersections statewide.

---

### Q133 — Driving on the Right Side & Passing
**LO:** 22.9 · **Source:** TC 545.060(a)

Under TC 545.060(a), an operator on a roadway divided into two or more clearly marked lanes for traffic shall drive as nearly as practical entirely within a single lane, and:

- [x] May not move from the lane unless the movement can be made safely
- [ ] Must remain in the rightmost lane at all times
- [ ] May change lanes freely without restriction
- [ ] Must signal for at least 200 feet before changing lanes

**Explanation:** The core rule is simply that lane changes must be safe — the specific 100-foot continuous-signal requirement for turns comes from a different section, TC 545.104(b).

---

### Q134 — Driving on the Right Side & Passing
**LO:** 22.9 · **Source:** TC 545.061

On a roadway divided into three or more lanes providing for one-way traffic, an operator entering a lane from a lane to the right shall yield the right-of-way to a vehicle entering the same lane from a lane to the ______.

**Answer:** left

**Explanation:** This merge rule gives priority to vehicles merging from the left over vehicles merging from the right into the same target lane.

---

### Q135 — Driving on the Right Side & Passing
**LO:** 22.9 · **Source:** TC 545.062(a)

Under TC 545.062(a), the "following distance" rule requires an operator to maintain:

- [x] An assured clear distance so the operator can safely stop without colliding, considering speed, traffic, and highway conditions
- [ ] Exactly one car length for every 10 mph of speed
- [ ] A minimum of 100 feet at all times
- [ ] Two seconds of following time, fixed regardless of conditions

**Explanation:** Texas does not codify a fixed following distance in feet or seconds — the standard is the flexible "assured clear distance" that accounts for speed, traffic, and road conditions.

---

### Q136 — Driving on the Right Side & Passing
**LO:** 22.9 · **Source:** TC 545.063(a)

On a highway with two or more roadways separated by a physical barrier or dividing section, an operator shall drive on the ______ roadway unless directed or permitted otherwise by an official traffic-control device or police officer.

**Answer:** right

**Explanation:** Crossing over the divider is separately prohibited under 545.063(b) except through an established opening or at a designated crossover/intersection.

---

### Q137 — Driving on the Right Side & Passing
**LO:** 22.9 · **Source:** TC 545.064

Under TC 545.064, an operator may enter or exit a limited-access or controlled-access roadway only:

- [x] At an entrance or exit established by a public authority
- [ ] Anywhere the shoulder is wide enough
- [ ] At any break in a physical barrier
- [ ] Only during daylight hours

**Explanation:** This "restricted access" rule reinforces the core definition of a limited-access/controlled-access highway under TC 541.302(8) — access is only where the jurisdictional authority permits it.

---

### Q138 — Driving on the Right Side & Passing
**LO:** 22.9 · **Source:** TC 545.066(a)

An operator approaching a school bus stopped on the highway to receive or discharge a student shall stop before reaching the bus when it is operating a ______ signal, and may not proceed until the bus resumes motion, the driver signals to proceed, or the signal is no longer actuated.

**Answer:** visual

**Explanation:** TC 545.066(b) provides limited exceptions — such as when the bus is on a separate roadway of a divided highway, or is stopped in a loading zone on a controlled-access highway where pedestrians cannot cross.

---

### Q139 — Driving on the Right Side & Passing
**LO:** 22.9 · **Source:** TC 545.066(c)

Under TC 545.066(c), a first-offense violation of passing a stopped school bus is punishable by a fine of:

- [x] $500 to $1,250
- [ ] $200 to $500
- [ ] $1,000 to $2,000
- [ ] $50 to $200

**Explanation:** A second or subsequent offense within five years raises the fine to $1,000-$2,000; causing serious bodily injury makes it a Class A misdemeanor, and a repeat injury offense becomes a state jail felony.

---

### Q140 — Driving on the Right Side & Passing
**LO:** 22.9 · **Source:** TC 545.002
