In Chapter 545, a reference to an "______" includes a reference to the vehicle operated by that person, if the reference imposes a duty or limitation on the movement or operation of that vehicle.

**Answer:** operator

**Explanation:** This clarifying rule means duties phrased as belonging to "the operator" also functionally constrain "the vehicle" itself throughout Chapter 545.

---

### Q141 — Driving on the Right Side & Passing
**LO:** 22.9 · **Source:** TC 545.055(b)

Under TC 545.055(b), an operator may not drive on the left side of the roadway in a no-passing zone, but this prohibition does not stop a driver from:

- [x] Crossing the center line to make a left turn into or out of an alley, private road, or driveway
- [ ] Passing any vehicle whatsoever
- [ ] Driving at any speed in the zone
- [ ] Using the improved shoulder

**Explanation:** The no-passing-zone rule targets passing maneuvers specifically — it doesn't prevent an operator from briefly crossing the center line to make a legitimate left turn into or out of an alley or driveway.

---

### Q142 — Turning & Signaling
**LO:** 22.10 · **Source:** TC 545.101(a), (b)

To make a right turn at an intersection, an operator shall approach and turn as closely as practicable to the right-hand curb or edge; to make a left turn, the operator shall approach in the extreme ______-hand lane lawfully available.

**Answer:** left

**Explanation:** The right-turn rule keeps the vehicle tight to the curb throughout; the left-turn rule instead requires positioning in the farthest-left lawful lane before entering the intersection.

---

### Q143 — Turning & Signaling
**LO:** 22.10 · **Source:** TC 545.102

Under TC 545.102, an operator may not turn a vehicle to move in the opposite direction (a U-turn) when approaching a curve or the crest of a grade if the vehicle is not visible to an approaching operator within:

- [x] 500 feet
- [ ] 100 feet
- [ ] 200 feet
- [ ] 1,000 feet

**Explanation:** This 500-foot visibility requirement is specifically about U-turns near curves and hill crests, distinct from the 100-foot passing-restriction distance used in Section 545.056.

---

### Q144 — Turning & Signaling
**LO:** 22.10 · **Source:** TC 545.103

An operator may not turn a vehicle to enter a private road or driveway, otherwise turn from a direct course, or move right or left on a roadway unless the movement can be made ______.

**Answer:** safely

**Explanation:** This general "safely turning" rule under 545.103 is a catch-all safety standard that applies on top of the more specific turning and signaling rules.

---

### Q145 — Turning & Signaling
**LO:** 22.10 · **Source:** TC 545.104(b)

Under TC 545.104(b), an operator intending to turn right or left shall signal continuously for not less than the last:

- [x] 100 feet of movement before the turn
- [ ] 50 feet of movement before the turn
- [ ] 200 feet of movement before the turn
- [ ] 300 feet of movement before the turn

**Explanation:** This same 100-foot continuous-signaling requirement applies to starting from a parked position and to lane changes, per 545.104(a).

---

### Q146 — Turning & Signaling
**LO:** 22.10 · **Source:** TC 545.105

An operator may not stop or suddenly decrease speed without first giving a ______ signal to the operator of a vehicle immediately to the rear, when there is an opportunity to give the signal.

**Answer:** stop

**Explanation:** This duty to warn following traffic before stopping or decelerating sharply exists specifically to prevent rear-end collisions.

---

### Q147 — Turning & Signaling
**LO:** 22.10 · **Source:** TC 545.106(b)

Under TC 545.106(b), a motor vehicle must be equipped with signal lamps (rather than relying on hand-and-arm signals) if the distance from the center of the top of the steering post to the left outside limit of the body, cab, or load is more than:

- [x] Two feet
- [ ] Five feet
- [ ] One foot
- [ ] Four feet

**Explanation:** The section also requires signal lamps if the distance from the steering post to the rear limit of the body or load exceeds 14 feet — either threshold independently requires lamps instead of hand signals.

---

### Q148 — Turning & Signaling
**LO:** 22.10 · **Source:** TC 545.107

To give a hand and arm signal from the left side of the vehicle: extend the hand and arm horizontally for a left turn, extend upward for a right turn, and extend ______ to signal a stop or decrease in speed.

**Answer:** downward

**Explanation:** A bicycle operator may alternatively signal a right turn by extending the hand and arm horizontally from the right side of the vehicle, per 545.107(2).

---

### Q149 — Turning & Signaling
**LO:** 22.10 · **Source:** TC 545.101(c)

Under TC 545.101(c), on a street or roadway designated for two-way traffic, an operator turning left shall, to the extent practicable, turn:

- [x] In the portion of the intersection to the left of the center of the intersection
- [ ] Directly through the center of the intersection
- [ ] Only from the rightmost lane
- [ ] Only after all oncoming traffic has fully cleared the block

**Explanation:** This positioning rule for left turns on two-way streets is distinct from the rule for turning from a one-way street, which instead requires turning as closely as practicable to the left-hand curb or edge.

---

### Q150 — Right-of-Way
**LO:** 22.11 · **Source:** TC 545.151(f)

An operator required to stop and yield the right-of-way at an intersection who is involved in a collision or interferes with traffic to whom right-of-way is owed is ______ not to have yielded the right-of-way.

**Answer:** presumed

**Explanation:** This presumption shifts the practical burden onto the operator who failed to yield when a collision results, and is echoed by a similar presumption for yield-sign violations in TC 545.153(d).

---

### Q151 — Right-of-Way
**LO:** 22.11 · **Source:** TC 545.152

Under TC 545.152, to turn left at an intersection or into an alley or private road/driveway, an operator shall yield the right-of-way to a vehicle:

- [x] Approaching from the opposite direction that is in the intersection or so close as to be an immediate hazard
- [ ] Approaching from the operator's right only
- [ ] Behind the operator's own vehicle
- [ ] Only if the vehicle has its headlights on

**Explanation:** This oncoming-traffic yield duty for left turns applies at both intersections and entrances to alleys or private roads/driveways.

---

### Q152 — Right-of-Way
**LO:** 22.11 · **Source:** TC 545.153(c)

An operator approaching an intersection controlled by a yield sign shall slow to a speed reasonable under existing conditions and yield the right-of-way to a vehicle in the intersection or approaching so closely as to be an ______ hazard.

**Answer:** immediate

**Explanation:** If the operator fails to stop at a yield sign and is then involved in a collision in the intersection, that collision is prima facie evidence the operator failed to yield, under 545.153(d).

---

### Q153 — Right-of-Way
**LO:** 22.11 · **Source:** TC 545.154

Under TC 545.154, on an access or feeder road of a limited-access or controlled-access highway, the operator on the feeder road shall yield the right-of-way to a vehicle:

- [x] Entering or about to enter the feeder road from the highway, or leaving or about to leave the feeder road to enter the highway
- [ ] Traveling in the opposite direction on the same feeder road
- [ ] Parked on the shoulder of the feeder road
- [ ] Only if that vehicle is an authorized emergency vehicle

**Explanation:** This rule governs the interaction between a limited-access highway's main lanes and its feeder/access road, giving priority to vehicles transitioning between the two.

---

### Q154 — Right-of-Way
**LO:** 22.11 · **Source:** TC 545.155

An operator about to enter or cross a highway from an alley, building, or private road or driveway shall yield the right-of-way to a vehicle ______ on the highway to be entered.

**Answer:** approaching

**Explanation:** This is a simple, unconditional yield duty — anyone emerging onto a highway from a private road, driveway, alley, or building must yield to approaching highway traffic.

---

### Q155 — Right-of-Way
**LO:** 22.11 · **Source:** TC 545.156(a-1)

Under TC 545.156(a-1), on the immediate approach of a properly signaling authorized emergency vehicle, an operator (unless directed otherwise by a police officer) shall yield the right-of-way, drive to a position parallel to and as close as possible to the right-hand edge or curb clear of any intersection, and:

- [x] Stop and remain standing until the emergency vehicle has passed
- [ ] Continue driving at reduced speed
- [ ] Pull to the left side of the roadway instead
- [ ] Proceed through the nearest intersection first

**Explanation:** All three duties — yield, move right, and stop and remain standing — apply together; 545.156(b) also clarifies this doesn't excuse the emergency vehicle operator from driving with due regard for others' safety.

---

### Q156 — Right-of-Way
**LO:** 22.11 · **Source:** TC 545.157(b)

On approaching certain stationary vehicles like a working authorized emergency vehicle or tow truck, an operator shall vacate the lane closest to the vehicle on a multi-lane highway, or slow to a speed not to exceed 20 miles per hour less than the posted limit (if 25 mph or more), or ______ miles per hour if the posted limit is less than 25 mph.

**Answer:** five

**Explanation:** This "Move Over" law under 545.157 gives operators a binary choice: change lanes away from the stopped vehicle, or slow down by the specified amount if changing lanes isn't possible.

---

### Q157 — Right-of-Way
**LO:** 22.11 · **Source:** TC 545.157(c)

Under TC 545.157(c), a first violation of the Move Over law is punishable by a fine of:

- [x] $500 to $1,250
- [ ] $200 to $500
- [ ] $50 to $200
- [ ] $1,000 to $2,000

**Explanation:** This fine range exactly matches the first-offense fine for illegally passing a stopped school bus under Section 545.066(c) — both offenses escalate to a Class A misdemeanor if the violation causes bodily injury.

---

### Q158 — Right-of-Way
**LO:** 22.11 · **Source:** TC 545.151(b)

On a single-lane or two-lane street approaching an uncontrolled intersection on a divided highway or a street divided into three or more marked lanes, an operator shall stop, yield, and grant immediate use of the intersection to a vehicle within the intersection or approaching in such proximity as to be a ______.

**Answer:** hazard

**Explanation:** This is a distinct rule from the basic uncontrolled-intersection right-of-way rule in 545.151(d) — it specifically addresses single/two-lane roads meeting a divided or multi-lane roadway.

---

### Q159 — Special Stops & Speed Restrictions
**LO:** 22.12 · **Source:** TC 545.251(a)

An operator approaching a railroad grade crossing required to stop shall stop not closer than ______ feet or farther than ______ feet from the nearest rail if a train or on-track equipment is an immediate hazard.

**Answer:** 15 | 50

**Explanation:** This same 15-to-50-foot stopping window applies consistently across bus, school bus, and hazardous-cargo railroad-crossing stop requirements in the following sections.

---

### Q160 — Special Stops & Speed Restrictions
**LO:** 22.12 · **Source:** TC 545.251(d)

Under TC 545.251(d), an operator commits an offense if the operator:

- [x] Drives around, under, or through a crossing gate or barrier while it is closed, being closed, or being opened
- [ ] Stops more than 50 feet from the nearest rail
- [ ] Fails to honk before crossing the tracks
- [ ] Crosses tracks marked "exempt"

**Explanation:** This gate/barrier offense is a distinct, more serious violation than simply failing to stop at the proper distance — it specifically targets driving around or through an active gate or barrier.

---

### Q161 — Special Stops & Speed Restrictions
**LO:** 22.12 · **Source:** TC 545.252(a)

TxDOT or a local authority may designate a railroad grade crossing as particularly ______ and erect a stop sign or other official traffic-control device at that crossing.

**Answer:** dangerous

**Explanation:** Once a crossing is so designated and signed, TC 545.252(b) requires operators to stop within the same 15-to-50-foot window and proceed only with due care.

---

### Q162 — Special Stops & Speed Restrictions
**LO:** 22.12 · **Source:** TC 545.253(a), (d)

Under TC 545.253, the requirement that a bus carrying passengers for hire stop, look, and listen before crossing a railroad grade crossing does NOT apply:

- [x] At a railway grade crossing in a business or residence district
- [ ] If the bus is empty of passengers
- [ ] At night
- [ ] On a state highway

**Explanation:** 545.253(d) exempts business/residence-district crossings from this specific bus stop-and-look requirement; 545.253(c) separately exempts situations where a police officer or signal directs traffic to proceed.

---

### Q163 — Special Stops & Speed Restrictions
**LO:** 22.12 · **Source:** TC 545.2535(a)

A school bus, before crossing a track at a railroad grade crossing, shall stop not closer than 15 feet or farther than 50 feet from the track, look and listen in both directions, and may not proceed until it is ______ to do so.

**Answer:** safe

**Explanation:** After stopping, the operator may proceed only in a gear that lets the bus complete the crossing without shifting gears while on the track, per 545.2535(b).

---

### Q164 — Special Stops & Speed Restrictions
**LO:** 22.12 · **Source:** TC 545.254(a)

Under TC 545.254(a), an operator of a vehicle carrying an explosive substance or flammable liquid as principal cargo, moving faster than 20 mph, must reduce speed to 20 mph or less before coming within how many feet of the nearest rail?

- [x] 200 feet
- [ ] 500 feet
- [ ] 50 feet
- [ ] 100 feet

**Explanation:** This 200-foot deceleration zone applies in addition to — not instead of — the separate stop-within-15-to-50-feet requirement that applies specifically within a municipality under 545.254(b).

---

### Q165 — Special Stops & Speed Restrictions
**LO:** 22.12 · **Source:** TC 545.256(1), (2)

An operator emerging from an alley, driveway, or building in a business or residence district shall stop before moving onto a sidewalk, yield the right-of-way to a pedestrian or ______ user to avoid collision, and on entering the roadway, yield to an approaching vehicle.

**Answer:** sidewalk

**Explanation:** This three-step duty — stop, yield to pedestrians/sidewalk users, then yield to roadway traffic — sequences the operator's obligations as they move from a private area onto the public street.

---

### Q166 — Special Stops & Speed Restrictions
**LO:** 22.12 · **Source:** TC 545.251(a)(3)

Under TC 545.251(a)(3), an operator must stop for an approaching railroad engine or on-track equipment emitting an audible signal from within approximately how many feet of the crossing, if it is an immediate hazard because of its speed or proximity?

- [x] 1,500 feet
- [ ] 500 feet
- [ ] 1,000 feet
- [ ] 2,000 feet

**Explanation:** This 1,500-foot audible-signal distance is one of five independent triggers in 545.251(a) requiring a stop, alongside a visible railroad signal, a lowered crossing gate or flagger warning, plainly visible hazardous proximity, or another legal stop requirement.

---

### Q167 — Special Stops & Speed Restrictions
**LO:** 22.12 · **Source:** TC 545.251(f)

An offense under Section 545.251 is punishable by a fine of not less than $______ or more than $______, except the fine rises to $100-$400 if the operator disregarded a flagger's warning.

**Answer:** 50 | 200

**Explanation:** This same $50-$200 base fine range recurs across nearly all of Subchapter F's railroad-crossing offenses (Sections 545.252, .253, .254, .255).

---

### Q168 — Special Stops & Speed Restrictions
**LO:** 22.12 · **Source:** TC 545.253(b)

Under TC 545.253(b), after stopping at a railroad grade crossing as required, an operator of a bus carrying passengers for hire shall proceed across the track:

- [x] Without manually shifting gears while crossing
- [ ] At a speed of at least 10 mph
- [ ] Only with a police escort
- [ ] With hazard lights activated

**Explanation:** This no-gear-shifting-on-the-track rule (also found for school buses in 545.2535(b)) exists to prevent a bus from stalling or losing power while still on the rails.

---

### Q169 — Stopping, Standing & Parking
**LO:** 22.13 · **Source:** TC 545.301(a)(3)

Outside a business or residence district, an operator may not stop, park, or leave a vehicle standing on the main traveled part of a highway unless it is impracticable to do otherwise, the remaining width is unobstructed, and the vehicle is in clear view for at least ______ feet in each direction.

**Answer:** 200

**Explanation:** All three conditions in 545.301(a) — impracticability, unobstructed width, and 200-foot visibility — must be satisfied together before stopping outside a business or residence district is lawful.

---

### Q170 — Stopping, Standing & Parking
**LO:** 22.13 · **Source:** TC 545.302(b)(2), (3)

Under TC 545.302(b), except momentarily to pick up or discharge a passenger, an operator may not stand or park within 15 feet of a fire hydrant or within how many feet of a crosswalk at an intersection?

- [x] 20 feet
- [ ] 10 feet
- [ ] 30 feet
- [ ] 50 feet

**Explanation:** 545.302(b) also bars standing/parking within 30 feet on the approach to a flashing signal, stop sign, yield sign, or traffic-control signal, and within 20 feet of a fire station driveway (75 feet on the opposite side).

---

### Q171 — Stopping, Standing & Parking
**LO:** 22.13 · **Source:** TC 545.302(c)(1)

Except temporarily to load or unload merchandise or passengers, an operator may not park within ______ feet of the nearest rail of a railroad crossing.

**Answer:** 50

**Explanation:** This 50-foot no-parking zone near railroad crossings is distinct from the 15-to-50-foot stopping window required when actually stopping for an oncoming train under Section 545.251.

---

### Q172 — Stopping, Standing & Parking
**LO:** 22.13 · **Source:** TC 545.302(a)(5)

Under TC 545.302(a)(5), an operator may not stop, stand, or park between a safety zone and the adjacent curb, or within how many feet of a point on the curb opposite the ends of the safety zone (absent a different municipal designation)?

- [x] 30 feet
- [ ] 15 feet
- [ ] 50 feet
- [ ] 100 feet

**Explanation:** A municipality's governing body may designate a different length by sign or marking, but 30 feet is the statutory default distance.

---

### Q173 — Stopping, Standing & Parking
**LO:** 22.13 · **Source:** TC 545.303(a)

An operator who stops or parks on a two-way roadway shall do so with the right-hand wheels parallel to and within ______ inches of the right-hand curb or edge of the roadway.

**Answer:** 18

**Explanation:** On a one-way roadway, 545.303(b) allows either the right-hand or left-hand wheels to be within 18 inches of the respective curb, depending on the direction of authorized traffic movement.

---

### Q174 — Stopping, Standing & Parking
**LO:** 22.13 · **Source:** TC 545.304

Under TC 545.304, a person may not move a vehicle that is not lawfully under the person's control into a place where a vehicle is prohibited, or:

- [x] Away from a curb a distance that is unlawful under Section 545.303
- [ ] Into a private garage without permission
- [ ] Across a state line
- [ ] Onto a controlled-access highway shoulder

**Explanation:** This section targets a person moving someone else's vehicle into an unlawful position — for example, shoving a parked car so its wheels are no longer within 18 inches of the curb as 545.303 requires.

---

### Q175 — Stopping, Standing & Parking
**LO:** 22.13 · **Source:** TC 545.305(a)(5)

A peace officer may remove or require the removal of a vehicle that is unattended and that the officer has reasonable grounds to believe has been abandoned for longer than ______ hours.

**Answer:** 48

**Explanation:** 545.305(a) lists nine separate grounds for authorized vehicle removal — this 48-hour abandonment threshold is just one of them, alongside blocking a driveway, being reported stolen, or being disabled with no owner available.

---

### Q176 — Stopping, Standing & Parking
**LO:** 22.13 · **Source:** TC 545.3051(b)

Under TC 545.3051(b), a metropolitan or regional transportation authority, a fire department, or a law enforcement agency may remove personal property from a roadway or right-of-way if the property:

- [x] Blocks the roadway or endangers public safety
- [ ] Belongs to an out-of-state resident
- [ ] Has been there for at least 24 hours
- [ ] Is worth less than $500

**Explanation:** 545.3051 authorizes removal of items like spilled cargo, hazardous materials or substances, unattended manufactured homes, and vehicles described in Section 545.305 — without the owner's or carrier's consent — when they block the road or endanger safety.

---

### Q177 — Stopping, Standing & Parking
**LO:** 22.13 · **Source:** TC 545.308

A local authority may provide by ordinance that in a prosecution for unlawfully stopping, standing, or parking an unattended vehicle, it is presumed that the ______ owner of the vehicle is the person who stopped, stood, or parked it.

**Answer:** registered

**Explanation:** This presumption under 545.308 helps local authorities enforce parking violations against unattended vehicles without needing to identify who was actually driving at the time.

---

### Q178 — Stopping, Standing & Parking
**LO:** 22.13 · **Source:** TC 545.302(d)

Under TC 545.302(d), a person may stop, stand, or park a bicycle on a sidewalk if:

- [x] The bicycle does not impede the normal and reasonable movement of pedestrian or other traffic on the sidewalk
- [ ] It is secured with a lock
- [ ] The sidewalk is at least 6 feet wide
- [ ] The owner posts a sign nearby

**Explanation:** This is a specific carve-out from the general stopping/standing/parking prohibitions in 545.302(a) for bicycles on sidewalks, conditioned only on not impeding pedestrian or other traffic.

---

### Q179 — Stopping, Standing & Parking
**LO:** 22.13 · **Source:** TC 545.301(b)(1)

The general prohibition on stopping/parking outside a business or residence district does not apply to the operator of a vehicle that is ______ while on the paved or main traveled part of a highway, if it is impossible to avoid stopping there.

**Answer:** disabled

**Explanation:** 545.301(b) also exempts vehicles used to transport solid/semisolid/liquid waste from an adjacent location, and licensed tow trucks performing towing duties.

---

### Q180 — Stopping, Standing & Parking
**LO:** 22.13 · **Source:** TC 545.302(a)(7), (8)

Under TC 545.302(a), an operator may not stop, stand, or park a vehicle:

- [x] On a bridge or other elevated structure, or on a railroad track
- [ ] Adjacent to a residence at any time
- [ ] Within 100 feet of a private driveway
- [ ] On any street with a speed limit above 30 mph

**Explanation:** 545.302(a) lists nine specific prohibited locations, including on the roadway side of a parked vehicle, on a sidewalk, in an intersection, on a crosswalk, alongside an obstruction, on a bridge/elevated structure/tunnel, on a railroad track, and wherever an official sign prohibits stopping.

---

### Q181 — Stopping, Standing & Parking
**LO:** 22.13 · **Source:** TC 545.302(b)(5)

Except momentarily to pick up or discharge a passenger, an operator may not stand or park within 20 feet of the driveway entrance to a fire station, and on the opposite side of the street may not do so within ______ feet of the entrance if it is properly marked with a sign.

**Answer:** 75

**Explanation:** The asymmetry (20 feet on the same side, 75 feet on the opposite side) reflects that a fire truck exiting the station needs a much wider clear path across the street than along its own side.

---

### Q182 — Speed Restrictions
**LO:** 22.14 · **Source:** TC 545.351(a)

An operator may not drive at a speed greater than is ______ and prudent under the circumstances then existing.

**Answer:** reasonable

**Explanation:** This is the foundational "basic speed rule" under 545.351 — it applies regardless of the posted prima facie speed limit, and requires reduced speed near intersections, curves, hill crests, and narrow or winding roads.

---

### Q183 — Speed Restrictions
**LO:** 22.14 · **Source:** TC 545.352(b)(1)

Under TC 545.352(b)(1), the prima facie speed limit in an urban district on a street other than an alley is:

- [x] 30 miles per hour
- [ ] 20 miles per hour
- [ ] 35 miles per hour
- [ ] 25 miles per hour

**Explanation:** The prima facie limit drops to 15 mph in an alley within an urban district, per the same subsection.

---

### Q184 — Speed Restrictions
**LO:** 22.14 · **Source:** TC 545.352(b)(2), (3)

Outside an urban district, the prima facie speed limit is ______ mph on a highway numbered by the state or United States, and ______ mph on an unnumbered highway outside an urban district.

**Answer:** 70 | 60

**Explanation:** These are the default rural prima facie limits absent a specific alteration by the Texas Transportation Commission, a county commissioners court, or a municipality under the following sections.

---

### Q185 — Speed Restrictions
**LO:** 22.14 · **Source:** TC 545.352(b)(4)

Under TC 545.352(b)(4), a school bus that has passed a commercial motor vehicle inspection and is on a highway numbered by the United States or this state, outside an urban district, may travel at a prima facie speed of:

- [x] 60 miles per hour
- [ ] 70 miles per hour
- [ ] 50 miles per hour
- [ ] 55 miles per hour

**Explanation:** A school bus that has not passed the inspection, or that is traveling on an unnumbered highway, is limited to 50 mph under 545.352(b)(4)(B).

---

### Q186 — Speed Restrictions
**LO:** 22.14 · **Source:** TC 545.353(d)(2)

Except in limited circumstances, the Texas Transportation Commission may not establish a speed limit of more than ______ miles per hour when altering a prima facie speed limit under Section 545.353.

**Answer:** 75

**Explanation:** 545.353(h-2) allows the commission to go as high as 85 mph on select highway segments designed and engineered to safely accommodate that speed, following an engineering and traffic investigation.

---

### Q187 — Speed Restrictions
**LO:** 22.14 · **Source:** TC 545.355(b)(1)

Under TC 545.355(b)(1), when a county commissioners court determines a prima facie speed limit is unreasonable or unsafe, it may declare a lower speed limit on a county road or highway of not less than:

- [x] 30 miles per hour
- [ ] 20 miles per hour
- [ ] 25 miles per hour
- [ ] 15 miles per hour

**Explanation:** 545.355(b)(2) allows an even lower floor of 20 mph specifically in a residence district (unless designated a major thoroughfare) or within 500 feet of certain schools, if approved.

---

### Q188 — Speed Restrictions
**LO:** 22.14 · **Source:** TC 545.361(a)

An operator of a moped or a motorcycle with an engine piston displacement of 250 cubic centimeters or less may not drive faster than ______ miles per hour during nighttime unless the vehicle has a headlamp that reveals a person or vehicle 300 feet ahead.

**Answer:** 35

**Explanation:** This special nighttime speed limitation applies only to small-displacement motorcycles and mopeds — larger motorcycles are not subject to this particular cap.

---

### Q189 — Speed Restrictions
**LO:** 22.14 · **Source:** TC 545.361(b)

Under TC 545.361(b), an operator of a vehicle equipped with solid rubber or cushion tires may not drive at a speed of more than:

- [x] 10 miles per hour
- [ ] 15 miles per hour
- [ ] 20 miles per hour
- [ ] 25 miles per hour

**Explanation:** This special speed limitation reflects the reduced traction and shock absorption of solid rubber or cushion tires compared to pneumatic tires.

---

### Q190 — Speed Restrictions
**LO:** 22.14 · **Source:** TC 545.363(a)

An operator may not drive so slowly as to ______ the normal and reasonable movement of traffic, except when reduced speed is necessary for safe operation or required by law.

**Answer:** impede

**Explanation:** Unlike maximum speed limits, Texas does not set a fixed minimum-speed number in Section 545.363 itself — a local or state authority must separately declare a specific minimum limit after an engineering and traffic investigation.

---

### Q191 — Speed Restrictions
**LO:** 22.14 · **Source:** TC 545.365(a)

Under TC 545.365(a), the speed regulations of Subchapter H do not apply to:

- [x] An authorized emergency vehicle responding to a call, a police patrol, or a physician or ambulance responding to an emergency call
- [ ] Any vehicle owned by a government agency
- [ ] School buses at any time
- [ ] Vehicles registered as farm vehicles

**Explanation:** This exemption is narrower than it might sound — a municipality may still, by ordinance, regulate the speed of ambulances, EMS vehicles, and blood/tissue bank vehicles under 545.365(b).

---

### Q192 — Speed Restrictions
**LO:** 22.14 · **Source:** TC 545.361(d)

An operator of self-propelled machinery designed for applying plant food materials or agricultural chemicals (and not designed solely to transport them) may not drive faster than ______ miles per hour unless the machinery is registered under Chapter 502.

**Answer:** 30

**Explanation:** This 30-mph cap on unregistered agricultural application machinery is a narrower special speed limitation than the general rural prima facie limits.

---

### Q193 — Speed Restrictions
**LO:** 22.14 · **Source:** TC 545.352(b)(5), (6)

Under TC 545.352(b), the prima facie speed limit on a beach, and on a county road adjacent to a public beach if declared by the commissioners court, is:

- [x] 15 miles per hour
- [ ] 25 miles per hour
- [ ] 30 miles per hour
- [ ] 10 miles per hour

**Explanation:** This 15-mph beach speed limit matches the alley speed limit within an urban district under the same subsection.

---

### Q194 — Speed Restrictions
**LO:** 22.14 · **Source:** TC 545.352(a)

A speed in excess of the limits established as prima facie speed limits is ______ facie evidence that the speed is not reasonable and prudent and is unlawful.

**Answer:** prima

**Explanation:** "Prima facie" means the excess speed is evidence of unlawfulness on its face — but under Section 542.206, this does not relieve a civil plaintiff of the burden of proving negligence as the proximate cause of a collision.

---

### Q195 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.401(a)

A person commits an offense if the person drives a vehicle in ______ or wanton disregard for the safety of persons or property.

**Answer:** wilful

**Explanation:** "Reckless driving" under 545.401 is a misdemeanor punishable by a fine not to exceed $200, confinement in county jail for not more than 30 days, or both — and it applies even on certain private parking areas serving a business, not just on highways.

---

### Q196 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.402

Under TC 545.402, an operator may not begin movement of a stopped, standing, or parked vehicle unless:

- [x] The movement can be made safely
- [ ] A passenger confirms it is clear
- [ ] The hazard lights are activated first
- [ ] The vehicle has been stationary for at least one minute

**Explanation:** This is a simple, general safety standard for pulling away from a stopped or parked position.

---

### Q197 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.403

An operator may not drive through or in a ______ zone.

**Answer:** safety

**Explanation:** This offense directly enforces the "safety zone" definition from TC 541.302(12) — an area officially marked for exclusive pedestrian use.

---

### Q198 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.404(a)

Under TC 545.404(a), an operator leaving a vehicle unattended must stop the engine, lock the ignition, remove the key, set the parking brake effectively, and if standing on a grade:

- [x] Turn the front wheels to the curb or side of the highway
- [ ] Chock the rear wheels
- [ ] Leave the vehicle in gear only, without the parking brake
- [ ] Notify local law enforcement

**Explanation:** 545.404(b) provides an exception for remote-starter systems that don't place the key in the ignition but still require the key to be present before the vehicle can actually be driven.

---

### Q199 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.407(a), (b)

An operator, unless on official business, may not follow closer than ______ feet to a fire apparatus responding to a fire alarm or to an ambulance flashing red lights.

**Answer:** 500

**Explanation:** This same 500-foot following-distance restriction applies identically to both responding fire apparatus and ambulances under 545.407.

---

### Q200 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.408

Under TC 545.408, an operator may not drive over an unprotected fire department hose on a street or private driveway intended for use at a fire, without:

- [x] The consent of the fire department official in command
- [ ] A written permit from the municipality
- [ ] Prior notice to the fire department at least 24 hours in advance
- [ ] Escort by a police officer

**Explanation:** Consent from the commanding fire official at the scene is the only exception recognized by this section.

---

### Q201 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.409(a)(2)

The drawbar or other connection between a vehicle drawing another vehicle and the drawn vehicle may not exceed ______ feet between the vehicles, except for connections between vehicles transporting poles, pipe, or similar structural loads.

**Answer:** 15

**Explanation:** 545.409(c) separately caps the "triple saddle-mount method" at no more than three motor vehicles drawn by a single towing vehicle.

---

### Q202 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.410(a)

Under TC 545.410(a), an operator of a passenger car or light truck drawing a trailer, semitrailer, house trailer, or another motor vehicle must use department-approved:

- [x] Safety chains
- [ ] Reflective triangles
- [ ] A CB radio
- [ ] A trailer brake controller

**Explanation:** This safety-chain requirement does not apply to trailers or semitrailers used for agricultural purposes, per 545.410(a).

---

### Q203 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.412(a), (b)

A person commits an offense if they operate a passenger vehicle transporting a child younger than ______ years of age (unless taller than four feet nine inches) without securing the child in a child passenger safety seat system; the offense is punishable by a fine of $25 to $______.

**Answer:** 8 | 250

**Explanation:** The height exception (taller than 4'9") recognizes that some children age out of the safety-seat requirement early based on physical size rather than age alone.

---

### Q204 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.413(a), (d)

Under TC 545.413, failing to wear a safety belt as a person at least 15 years old riding in a seat equipped with one is a misdemeanor punishable by a fine of:

- [x] $25 to $50
- [ ] $100 to $200
- [ ] $25 to $250
- [ ] $50 to $100

**Explanation:** Allowing an unbelted child under 17 to ride (when not required to be in a child safety seat) is a separate offense under 545.413(b), punishable by the higher $100-$200 fine range.

---

### Q205 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.414(a)

A person commits an offense if the person operates an open-bed pickup truck or open flatbed truck, or draws an open flatbed trailer, while a child younger than ______ years of age is occupying the bed.

**Answer:** 18

**Explanation:** Defenses under 545.414(c) include operating in a parade or emergency, transporting farmworkers between fields on certain rural roads, operating on a beach, or operating the only vehicle owned by the household.

---

### Q206 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.4145(a)

Under TC 545.4145(a), a person commits an offense if they operate a motor vehicle on a highway or street while a child younger than 18 is occupying a:

- [x] Boat or personal watercraft being drawn by the motor vehicle
- [ ] Trailer designed for human habitation
- [ ] Bicycle trailer attached to the vehicle
- [ ] Golf cart being towed

**Explanation:** This offense specifically targets towing a boat or personal watercraft with a minor riding inside it — defenses exist for parades, emergencies, and operation on a beach.

---

### Q207 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.415(a), (b)

An operator may not back a vehicle unless the movement can be made ______ and without interference with other traffic, and may not back on a shoulder or roadway of a limited-access or controlled-access highway.

**Answer:** safely

**Explanation:** The additional prohibition on backing on a limited-access highway's shoulder or roadway recognizes the much higher risk of a rear-end collision at highway speeds.

---

### Q208 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.416(d)

Under TC 545.416(d), an operator may not carry another person on a motorcycle or moped unless that other person is at least:

- [x] Five years of age
- [ ] Ten years of age
- [ ] Eight years of age
- [ ] Two years of age

**Explanation:** This age floor does not apply to a person younger than five who is seated in a sidecar attached to the motorcycle or moped, per 545.416(e).

---

### Q209 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.417(a)

An operator may not drive a vehicle loaded, or with more than three persons in the front seat, so that the operator's view to the front or sides is obstructed or there is interference with the operator's control over the driving ______.

**Answer:** mechanism

**Explanation:** 545.417(b) places a parallel duty on passengers — they may not ride in a position that interferes with the operator's view or control either.

---

### Q210 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.418

Under TC 545.418, a person may not open the door of a motor vehicle on the side available to moving traffic unless:

- [x] The door may be opened in reasonable safety without interfering with the movement of other traffic
- [ ] The vehicle is completely stopped for at least 10 seconds
- [ ] A passenger checks behind the vehicle first
- [ ] Hazard lights are activated

**Explanation:** This section also prohibits leaving a door open on the traffic side longer than necessary to load or unload a passenger.

---

### Q211 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.419

A person may not occupy a house trailer while it is being ______.

**Answer:** moved

**Explanation:** This is a short, absolute prohibition on riding inside a house trailer during transport, regardless of amenities inside.

---

### Q212 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.4191(a), (c)

Under TC 545.4191, a person may not operate a truck, road tractor, or truck tractor when another person occupies a trailer or semitrailer being drawn by it; a violation is classified as a:

- [x] Class B misdemeanor
- [ ] Class C misdemeanor
- [ ] Class A misdemeanor
- [ ] State jail felony

**Explanation:** Defenses include operating in a parade or emergency, transporting farmworkers between fields on certain rural roads, a permitted hayride, the operator not knowing anyone occupied the trailer, or the passenger being in a part of the trailer designed for human habitation.

---

### Q213 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.420(d)

Except as otherwise enhanced, an offense of racing on a highway under Section 545.420(a) is a Class ______ misdemeanor.

**Answer:** B

**Explanation:** The offense escalates to a Class A misdemeanor for a prior conviction or if the person was intoxicated or possessed an open container, a state jail felony for a second prior conviction, a third-degree felony if a person suffered bodily injury, and a second-degree felony if a person suffered serious bodily injury or death.

---

### Q214 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.421(a), (d)

Under TC 545.421, fleeing or attempting to elude a police officer is a Class B misdemeanor, escalating to a Class A misdemeanor if:

- [x] The person recklessly engages in conduct placing another in imminent danger of serious bodily injury
- [ ] The offense occurs at night
- [ ] The person was driving a commercial vehicle
- [ ] The pursuit lasted longer than five minutes

**Explanation:** 545.421(e) creates a presumption of reckless endangerment if the person was intoxicated while knowingly operating the vehicle during the offense.

---

### Q215 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.422(a), (c)

A person may not drive a motor vehicle on a sidewalk, sidewalk area, or hike and bike trail except on a permanent or authorized temporary ______.

**Answer:** driveway

**Explanation:** A "hike and bike trail" is defined in 545.422(c) as a trail designed for the exclusive use of pedestrians, bicyclists, or both — and operating a vehicle there for trail maintenance purposes is separately exempted under 545.422(b).

---

### Q216 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.424(a-1)

Under TC 545.424(a-1), a person under 18 years of age may not operate a motor vehicle after midnight and before 5 a.m. unless necessary for employment, a school-related activity, a medical emergency, or:

- [x] Not applicable — those are the only listed exceptions besides the passenger-limit rule
- [ ] Accompanied by any adult passenger
- [ ] The vehicle has passed a safety inspection
- [ ] The driver has held a license for at least one year

**Explanation:** 545.424(a-1) also independently bars a driver under 18 from having more than one passenger under 21 who is not a family member in the vehicle at any time.

---

### Q217 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.424(a)

A person under 18 years of age may not operate a motor vehicle while using a ______ communication device, except in case of emergency.

**Answer:** wireless

**Explanation:** A peace officer may not stop a vehicle or detain its operator for the sole purpose of determining whether this particular subsection has been violated, per 545.424(e).

---

### Q218 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.4251(b), (f)

Under TC 545.4251, using a portable wireless communication device to read, write, or send an electronic message while driving is generally punishable by a fine of $25-$99 (first offense), escalating to a Class A misdemeanor with a fine up to $4,000 and up to one year in jail if:

- [x] The defendant caused the death or serious bodily injury of another person
- [ ] The offense occurred in a school zone
- [ ] The defendant was under 18 years of age
- [ ] The device was a smartphone rather than a basic cell phone

**Explanation:** This statewide texting-while-driving ban preempts local ordinances on the same subject and includes affirmative defenses for hands-free use, GPS navigation, reporting illegal activity or emergencies, and certain occupational dispatch communications.

---

### Q219 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.425(b)

An operator may not use a wireless communication device while operating a motor vehicle within a school ______ zone unless the vehicle is stopped or the device is used with a hands-free device.

**Answer:** crossing

**Explanation:** 545.425(c) separately bans using a wireless communication device while operating a school bus or passenger bus with a minor passenger aboard, unless the bus itself is stopped.

---

### Q220 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.426(a)

Under TC 545.426(a), a person may not operate a school bus if the door of the bus is open or if:

- [x] The number of passengers exceeds the manufacturer's design capacity for the bus
- [ ] The bus is more than 10 years old
- [ ] The bus lacks a two-way radio
- [ ] The route has not been pre-approved by the district

**Explanation:** 545.426(b) further requires the operator to prohibit passengers from standing or sitting on the floor or in any non-seat location while the bus is operating.

---

### Q221 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.427(a)

An operator may not drive on or cross a railroad grade crossing unless the vehicle being operated has sufficient undercarriage ______.

**Answer:** clearance

**Explanation:** A violation of this section is a misdemeanor punishable by a fine of $50 to $200, matching the base fine range used for most other railroad-crossing offenses in Subchapter F.

---

### Q222 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.451(2), (7)

Under TC 545.451, an "automated motor vehicle" is one equipped with an automated driving system capable of Level 4 or Level 5 automation; the "human driver" is defined as:

- [x] A natural person in the automated motor vehicle who controls all or part of the dynamic driving task
- [ ] Any occupant of the vehicle, regardless of role
- [ ] Only a person holding a valid Class A, B, or C driver's license
- [ ] The remote operator monitoring the vehicle from off-site

**Explanation:** "Dynamic driving task" is separately defined in 545.451(6) to include real-time operational functions (steering, braking, monitoring) and tactical functions (responding to events, changing lanes, turning), but not strategic functions like choosing a destination.

---

### Q223 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.454(a)

When an automated driving system installed on a motor vehicle is engaged, the automated driving system is the ______ of the vehicle, including for purposes of assessing compliance with applicable traffic or motor vehicle laws.

**Answer:** operator

**Explanation:** Under 545.454(c), neither a licensed human driver nor a license issued under Chapter 521 or 522 is required to operate an automated motor vehicle while its automated driving system is engaged — but 545.454(b) still directs any citation for a traffic violation to the vehicle's owner or authorization holder.

---

### Q224 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.455(b)(4)

Under TC 545.455(b), an automated motor vehicle may not operate on a highway or street with its automated driving system engaged unless, among other requirements, it is capable of achieving a:

- [x] Minimal risk condition if the automated driving system fails
- [ ] Top speed of at least 75 miles per hour
- [ ] Full stop within 50 feet at highway speed
- [ ] Remote shutdown within 10 seconds

**Explanation:** "Minimal risk condition" is defined in 545.451(11) as a stable and stopped condition the vehicle can reach to reduce collision risk when a trip cannot or should not continue — one of six conditions 545.455(b) requires before an automated motor vehicle may legally operate with the system engaged.

---

### Q225 — Emergency Vehicles
**LO:** 22.16 · **Source:** TC 546.001

In operating an authorized emergency vehicle, the operator may park or stand irrespective of another provision, proceed past a red or stop signal or stop sign after slowing as necessary for safe operation, exceed a maximum speed limit as long as the operator does not endanger life or property, and disregard a regulation governing the ______ of movement or turning in specified directions.

**Answer:** direction

**Explanation:** 546.001(4) is the fourth permissible-conduct category — disregarding direction-of-movement or turning regulations — alongside parking/standing, proceeding past signals, and exceeding speed limits.

---

### Q226 — Emergency Vehicles
**LO:** 22.16 · **Source:** TC 546.001

Under TC 546.001, before proceeding past a red light or stop sign, the operator of an authorized emergency vehicle must first:

- [ ] Come to a complete stop
- [x] Slow as necessary for safe operation
- [ ] Activate audible signals only
- [ ] Obtain dispatcher authorization

**Explanation:** 546.001(2) requires slowing as necessary for safe operation before proceeding past a red/stop signal or sign — it does not require a full stop.

---

### Q227 — Emergency Vehicles
**LO:** 22.16 · **Source:** TC 546.001(3)

Under TC 546.001(3), an authorized emergency vehicle operator may exceed a maximum speed limit as long as the operator does not ______ life or property.

**Answer:** endanger

**Explanation:** The speeding exception in 546.001(3) is conditioned on not endangering life or property — there is no numeric mph cap written into the statute itself.

---

### Q228 — Emergency Vehicles
**LO:** 22.16 · **Source:** TC 546.001(3)

TC 546.001(3) permits an authorized emergency vehicle to exceed the maximum speed limit subject to which condition?

- [x] The operator must not endanger life or property
- [ ] The operator must notify dispatch first
- [ ] The speed may never exceed 20 mph over the limit
- [ ] The exception only applies on highways, not city streets

**Explanation:** No mph ceiling is specified — the only express limit is that the operator not endanger life or property.

---

### Q229 — Emergency Vehicles
**LO:** 22.16 · **Source:** TC 546.002(a)

Under TC 546.002(a), a "______" means facilitating the movement of a funeral, oversized or hazardous load, or other traffic disruption for public safety purposes by a peace officer described by specified articles of the Code of Criminal Procedure.

**Answer:** police escort

**Explanation:** 546.002(a) defines "police escort" specifically around funerals, oversized/hazardous loads, and other public-safety traffic disruptions.

---

### Q230 — Emergency Vehicles
**LO:** 22.16 · **Source:** TC 546.002(a)

Under TC 546.002(a), a "police escort" is defined as facilitating the movement of which of the following?

- [x] A funeral, oversized or hazardous load, or other traffic disruption for public safety purposes
- [ ] Any commercial vehicle convoy crossing county lines
- [ ] A school bus route during inclement weather
- [ ] Any vehicle requested by a private citizen

**Explanation:** The definition is narrow — funerals, oversized/hazardous loads, and other public-safety traffic disruptions — not any escort a private party might request.

---

### Q231 — Emergency Vehicles
**LO:** 22.16 · **Source:** TC 546.002(b)

Under TC 546.002(b), Sections 546.001(2), (3), and (4) apply only when the operator is responding to an emergency call, pursuing an actual or suspected violator of the law, responding to but not returning from a fire alarm, directing or diverting traffic for public safety purposes, or conducting a ______.

**Answer:** police escort

**Explanation:** 546.002(b) lists five qualifying circumstances; police escort (defined in 546.002(a)) is the fifth.

---

### Q232 — Emergency Vehicles
**LO:** 22.16 · **Source:** TC 546.002(b)

Which of the following is NOT one of the five circumstances under TC 546.002(b) that make the emergency-vehicle privileges under 546.001(2)-(4) available?

- [x] Returning from a fire alarm
- [ ] Responding to an emergency call
- [ ] Pursuing an actual or suspected violator of the law
- [ ] Conducting a police escort

**Explanation:** 546.002(b)(3) specifically covers responding to but NOT returning from a fire alarm — the privileges end once the emergency response itself is over.

---

### Q233 — Emergency Vehicles
**LO:** 22.16 · **Source:** TC 546.003

Under TC 546.003, the operator of an authorized emergency vehicle engaging in permitted conduct shall use audible or visual signals that meet the pertinent requirements of Sections 547.305 and ______.

**Answer:** 547.702

**Explanation:** 546.003 cross-references 547.305 (restrictions on use of lights) and 547.702 (siren/signal equipment) as the standard for required audible or visual signals.

---

### Q234 — Emergency Vehicles
**LO:** 22.16 · **Source:** TC 546.003

TC 546.003 requires an authorized emergency vehicle operator engaging in 546.001 conduct to use audible or visual signals meeting the requirements of which two sections?

- [x] 547.305 and 547.702
- [ ] 547.321 and 547.322
- [ ] 546.004 and 546.007
- [ ] 547.333 and 547.334

**Explanation:** The two cross-referenced sections govern light restrictions (547.305) and audible/visual signal equipment (547.702).

---

### Q235 — Emergency Vehicles
**LO:** 22.16 · **Source:** TC 546.004(a)

Under TC 546.004(a), a volunteer firefighter operating a private vehicle as an authorized emergency vehicle may engage in 546.001 conduct only when using ______ signals meeting the requirements of Sections 547.305 and 547.702.

**Answer:** visual

**Explanation:** Unlike the general rule in 546.003 (audible OR visual, at the operator's discretion), the volunteer-firefighter private-vehicle exception in 546.004(a) requires visual signals specifically.

---

### Q236 — Emergency Vehicles
**LO:** 22.16 · **Source:** TC 546.004(a)

Under TC 546.004(a), a volunteer firefighter operating a private vehicle as an authorized emergency vehicle may invoke 546.001 privileges only while using:

- [x] Visual signals only
- [ ] Audible signals only
- [ ] Either audible or visual signals, at the firefighter's discretion
- [ ] Both audible and visual signals simultaneously

**Explanation:** The private-vehicle volunteer-firefighter exception is narrower than the general officer rule — visual signals are required, not merely one of two options.

---

### Q237 — Emergency Vehicles
**LO:** 22.16 · **Source:** TC 546.004(b)

Under TC 546.004(b), an authorized emergency vehicle operated as a police vehicle is not required to be equipped with or display a red light visible from the ______ of the vehicle.

**Answer:** front

**Explanation:** 546.004(b) exempts police vehicles from the front-red-light requirement — this dovetails with 547.305(b), which otherwise restricts front-facing red lights to police vehicles.

---

### Q238 — Emergency Vehicles
**LO:** 22.16 · **Source:** TC 546.004(b)

Under TC 546.004(b), which authorized emergency vehicle is exempt from displaying a red light visible from the front?

- [x] A vehicle operated as a police vehicle
- [ ] A vehicle operated as an ambulance
- [ ] A vehicle operated as a fire truck
- [ ] A vehicle operated as a tow truck

**Explanation:** 546.004(b) singles out police vehicles specifically for this front-red-light exemption.

---

### Q239 — Emergency Vehicles
**LO:** 22.16 · **Source:** TC 546.004(c)

Under TC 546.004(c), a police officer may operate an authorized emergency vehicle without using required audible or visual signals if, among other things, the officer has probable cause to believe that knowledge of the officer's presence will cause a suspect to destroy or lose evidence, end a continuing felony, or ______ apprehension or identification.

**Answer:** evade

**Explanation:** 546.004(c)(1)(A) lists destruction/loss of evidence, ending an ongoing felony, or evading apprehension/identification as the three suspect-behavior justifications for skipping required signals.

---

### Q240 — Emergency Vehicles
**LO:** 22.16 · **Source:** TC 546.004(c)

Under TC 546.004(c), an officer may forgo the audible/visual signals otherwise required by 546.003 in all of the following situations EXCEPT:

- [x] Responding to a low-priority, non-emergency call for service
- [ ] Pursuing a suspect who might destroy evidence of a felony if alerted
- [ ] Complying with a written department regulation on signal use
- [ ] Multilaned-roadway traffic conditions where signals could increase collision risk

**Explanation:** 546.004(c) lists specific justifications (evidence destruction, ongoing felony, evasion, multilane collision risk, or department policy) — a routine non-emergency call isn't one of them.

---

### Q241 — Emergency Vehicles
**LO:** 22.16 · **Source:** TC 546.005

TC 546.005 states that Chapter 546 does not relieve an emergency vehicle operator of the duty to operate the vehicle with appropriate regard for the safety of all persons, or the consequences of ______ disregard for the safety of others.

**Answer:** reckless

**Explanation:** 546.005 preserves liability for reckless disregard of others' safety — the emergency-vehicle privileges of 546.001 are not a blanket immunity.

---

### Q242 — Emergency Vehicles
**LO:** 22.16 · **Source:** TC 546.005

TC 546.005 (Duty of Care) means an authorized emergency vehicle operator invoking 546.001 privileges is still liable for:

- [x] Reckless disregard for the safety of others
- [ ] Any collision regardless of fault
- [ ] Civil liability only if off duty
- [ ] Nothing, as long as signals were used

**Explanation:** The statute expressly preserves both the general duty of care and reckless-disregard liability, regardless of signal use.

---

### Q243 — Emergency Vehicles
**LO:** 22.16 · **Source:** TC 546.006

Under TC 546.006, a vehicle designated by the department may be operated by a designated organization as if it were an authorized emergency vehicle only if the governor declares a state of disaster, the department requests assistance from the organization, and the vehicle is operated by that organization in response to the ______.

**Answer:** state of disaster

**Explanation:** 546.006(b) requires all three conditions together: designation, a gubernatorial disaster declaration, a department request, and disaster-response use.

---

### Q244 — Emergency Vehicles
**LO:** 22.16 · **Source:** TC 546.006

Under TC 546.006, which condition is NOT required before a designated vehicle may be operated as an authorized emergency vehicle during a declared disaster?

- [x] The vehicle must be a government-owned vehicle
- [ ] The governor must declare a state of disaster under Section 418.014, Government Code
- [ ] The department must request assistance from the designated organization
- [ ] The vehicle must be operated in response to the state of disaster

**Explanation:** 546.006 does not require government ownership of the vehicle — it requires designation, a disaster declaration, a department request, and disaster-response use.

---

### Q245 — Emergency Vehicles
**LO:** 22.16 · **Source:** TC 546.007(c)

Under TC 546.007(c), a road or highway closure made by a firefighter shall be limited to the affected lane or lanes and ______ additional lane, unless the safety of emergency personnel requires more lanes closed.

**Answer:** one

**Explanation:** 546.007(c) caps the closure at the affected lane(s) plus one additional lane, absent a safety need for more.

---

### Q246 — Emergency Vehicles
**LO:** 22.16 · **Source:** TC 546.007

Under TC 546.007, which firefighters may close one or more lanes of a road or highway to protect the safety of persons or property?

- [x] Firefighters employed by or a member of an emergency services district, volunteer fire department, or general-law municipality fire department
- [ ] Any private security officer at the scene
- [ ] Only paid, full-time municipal firefighters
- [ ] Any first responder including EMS and police

**Explanation:** 546.007(a) limits this lane-closure authority to firefighters connected to an emergency services district, a volunteer fire department, or a general-law municipality's fire department.

---

### Q247 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.001(2-b)

Under TC 547.001(2-b), a "______" means a highway or traffic maintenance or construction vehicle designated by the Texas Department of Transportation, including equipment for road maintenance/construction and road construction or off-road use.

**Answer:** Highway Maintenance or Construction Vehicle

**Explanation:** TxDOT designates these vehicles, which include equipment like snow removal, line striping, aerial platform lifts, road profilers, motor graders, road rollers, and excavators.

---

### Q248 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.001(2-b)

Under TC 547.001(2-b), which agency designates a vehicle as a "highway maintenance or construction vehicle"?

- [x] The Texas Department of Transportation
- [ ] The Texas Department of Public Safety
- [ ] The Texas Department of Motor Vehicles
- [ ] The Texas Commission on Environmental Quality

**Explanation:** TxDOT is the designating agency for this term, as well as the standard-setter for maintenance/service vehicle lighting under 547.105.

---

### Q249 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.001(3)

Under TC 547.001(3), "______" means the ratio of the amount of light that passes through a material to the amount of light that falls on the material and the glazing.

**Answer:** Light Transmission

**Explanation:** "Light transmission" (pass-through ratio) is distinct from "luminous reflectance" (547.001(4)), which measures light reflected rather than transmitted.

---

### Q250 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.001(3)

TC 547.001(3) defines "light transmission" as a ratio comparing the light that passes through a material to:

- [x] The amount of light that falls on the material and the glazing
- [ ] The amount of light reflected by the material
- [ ] The total light emitted by the vehicle's headlamps
- [ ] The luminous reflectance of an adjacent surface

**Explanation:** The ratio's denominator is the light falling on the material and glazing, not reflected light (that's a separate, related definition).

---

### Q251 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.001(5-a)

Under TC 547.001(5-a), "______" means a self-propelled vehicle that was originally and permanently designed as machinery, is not designed or used primarily to transport persons or property, and is only incidentally operated on a highway.

**Answer:** Road Machinery

**Explanation:** "Road machinery" is one of the vehicle categories exempted from Chapter 547 entirely under 547.002.

---

### Q252 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.001(5-a)

Under TC 547.001(5-a), "road machinery" is defined by all of the following EXCEPT:

- [x] It must be towed by a farm tractor or motor vehicle
- [ ] It is self-propelled
- [ ] It was originally and permanently designed as machinery
- [ ] It is only incidentally operated on a highway

**Explanation:** "Road machinery" is self-propelled, not towed — towed implements fall under other definitions like "slow-moving vehicle" (547.001(7)(B)).

---

### Q253 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.001(6)

Under TC 547.001(6), "______" includes only a glazing material that is constructed, treated, or combined with another material to reduce substantially, as compared to ordinary sheet or plate glass, the likelihood of injury to persons by an external object or by cracked or broken glazing material.

**Answer:** Safety Glazing Material

**Explanation:** The definition's baseline comparison point is ordinary sheet or plate glass.

---

### Q254 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.001(6)

Under TC 547.001(6), "safety glazing material" is defined relative to which baseline comparison?

- [x] Ordinary sheet or plate glass
- [ ] Federal safety glass standards
- [ ] Tempered automotive glass
- [ ] Polycarbonate window material

**Explanation:** The reduced-injury-risk standard is measured "as compared to ordinary sheet or plate glass" — that comparison point is built directly into 547.001(6).

---

### Q255 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.001(6-a)

Under TC 547.001(6-a), a "______" is a highway or traffic maintenance vehicle that is owned/operated on a highway by or for a governmental agency and performs a function requiring a lamp per Section 547.105 standards, or that has a public service function such as public utility vehicles, tow trucks, or escort flag vehicles.

**Answer:** Service Vehicle

**Explanation:** "Service vehicle" has two independent prongs — governmental highway-maintenance use, or a public service function.

---

### Q256 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.001(6-a)

Under TC 547.001(6-a), which of the following independently qualifies a vehicle as a "service vehicle"?

- [x] A tow truck performing a public service function
- [ ] Any vehicle owned by a private citizen
- [ ] Any vehicle registered as a passenger car
- [ ] A farm tractor used off-road

**Explanation:** Tow trucks are expressly named under the public-service-function prong of the "service vehicle" definition.

---

### Q257 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.001(7)(A)

Under TC 547.001(7)(A), a "slow-moving vehicle" includes a motor vehicle designed to operate at a maximum speed of ______ miles per hour or less (not including an electric personal assistive mobility device).

**Answer:** 25

**Explanation:** 25 mph is the exact statutory ceiling for a "slow-moving vehicle" under 547.001(7)(A); the definition also covers vehicles/implements towed by an animal or by a ≤25 mph motor vehicle.

---

### Q258 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.001(7)

Under TC 547.001(7), a "slow-moving vehicle" includes which of the following?

- [x] A motor vehicle designed to operate at a maximum speed of 25 mph or less
- [ ] A motor vehicle designed to operate at a maximum speed of 20 mph or less
- [ ] Any vehicle towing a boat trailer
- [ ] Any vehicle operating below the posted speed limit

**Explanation:** The 25 mph design-speed threshold is the operative number — actual speed being driven is irrelevant to the definition.

---

### Q259 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.001(8)

Under TC 547.001(8), a "slow-moving-vehicle emblem" means a ______-shaped emblem that conforms to standards and specifications adopted by the director under Section 547.104.

**Answer:** triangular

**Explanation:** The triangular shape is specified directly in the definition, with detailed color/size/mounting standards left to 547.104.

---

### Q260 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.001(8), 547.104

Under TC 547.104, the slow-moving-vehicle emblem's standards (color, size, mounting position) are set to conform as closely as practicable to standards adopted by which organization?

- [x] The American Society of Agricultural Engineers
- [ ] The American Association of State Highway and Transportation Officials
- [ ] The Society of Automotive Engineers
- [ ] The National Highway Traffic Safety Administration

**Explanation:** 547.104 ties the slow-moving-vehicle emblem to American Society of Agricultural Engineers standards, while 547.105 (maintenance/service vehicle lighting) instead references AASHTO.

---

### Q261 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.001(9)

Under TC 547.001(9), a "______" is a film, material, or device that meets the department's standards for reducing effects of the sun.

**Answer:** Sun-Screening Device

**Explanation:** The statute itself spells this term as one word ("sunscreening device"); either spelling scores the same once hyphens are ignored and case is folded.

---

### Q262 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.001(9)

Under TC 547.001(9), a sunscreening device is defined by which requirement?

- [x] It meets the department's standards for reducing the effects of the sun
- [ ] It reduces light transmission to below 25%
- [ ] It is limited to the windshield only
- [ ] It must be factory-installed

**Explanation:** The statute leaves specific numeric limits to department standards; the definition itself just requires meeting the department's sun-reduction standards.

---

### Q263 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.002

Under TC 547.002, Chapter 547's equipment standards do not apply to an implement of husbandry, road machinery, a road roller, a farm tractor, a bicycle, an electric bicycle, a golf cart, a neighborhood electric vehicle, or a(n) ______ vehicle.

**Answer:** off-highway

**Explanation:** "Off-highway vehicle" (547.002(9)) rounds out the nine exempt categories.

---

### Q264 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.002

Which of the following vehicles IS subject to TC Chapter 547's general equipment standards (i.e., is NOT listed as exempt under 547.002)?

- [x] A standard passenger car
- [ ] A golf cart
- [ ] A farm tractor
- [ ] A road roller

**Explanation:** 547.002 exempts nine specific categories — an ordinary passenger car isn't among them.

---

### Q265 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.003

Under TC 547.003, Chapter 547 does not prohibit, and the department may not prohibit, the use of equipment required by an agency of the United States, or a part or accessory not ______ with this chapter or a rule adopted under it.

**Answer:** inconsistent

**Explanation:** 547.003 shields two categories: federally-required equipment, and parts/accessories that don't conflict with Chapter 547 or its rules.

---

### Q266 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.003

Under TC 547.003, which type of equipment is protected from a department prohibition?

- [x] Equipment required by a federal agency
- [ ] Equipment banned in another state
- [ ] Any aftermarket accessory regardless of function
- [ ] Equipment that predates 1995

**Explanation:** Federally-required equipment cannot be prohibited by department rule under 547.003(1).

---

### Q267 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.004(a)

Under TC 547.004(a), a person commits a misdemeanor if the person operates or moves, or as an owner knowingly permits another to operate or move, a vehicle that is unsafe so as to endanger a person, is not equipped to comply with this chapter's standards, or is equipped in a manner ______ by this chapter.

**Answer:** prohibited

**Explanation:** 547.004(a) has three prongs: unsafe/endangering, non-compliant equipment, or affirmatively prohibited equipment.

---

### Q268 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.004(b)

Under TC 547.004(b), a person commits an offense if the person operates a vehicle equipped with an item of vehicle equipment that the person knows has been determined non-compliant in a proceeding under which section?

- [x] Section 547.206
- [ ] Section 547.105
- [ ] Section 547.301
- [ ] Section 547.003

**Explanation:** 547.004(b) cross-references the compliance-proceeding determination process in 547.206.

---

### Q269 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.004(c)

Under TC 547.004(c), a court may dismiss a Section 547.004 charge if the defendant remedies the defect before the defendant's first court appearance and pays a reimbursement fee not to exceed $______.

**Answer:** 10

**Explanation:** The $10 cap makes this a low-cost "fix it" dismissal option for a first appearance.

---

### Q270 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.004(d)

Under TC 547.004(d), the $10 reimbursement-fee dismissal option in Subsection (c) does NOT apply to an offense involving:

- [x] A commercial motor vehicle
- [ ] A passenger car
- [ ] A motorcycle
- [ ] A farm tractor

**Explanation:** 547.004(d) carves commercial motor vehicles out of the low-cost dismissal option available for other vehicles under Subsection (c).

---

### Q271 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.005(a)

Under TC 547.005(a), a person may not use a slow-moving-vehicle emblem on a stationary object or a vehicle other than a ______ vehicle.

**Answer:** slow-moving

**Explanation:** This is the misuse offense for the triangular slow-moving-vehicle emblem defined in 547.001(8).

---

### Q272 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.005(a)

Under TC 547.005(a), the misuse offense involving a slow-moving-vehicle emblem covers which improper use?

- [x] Displaying it on a stationary object or a non-slow-moving vehicle
- [ ] Displaying it after dark
- [ ] Displaying it without a permit fee paid
- [ ] Displaying more than one emblem per vehicle

**Explanation:** 547.005(a) is about the emblem being used where it doesn't belong — not about timing, fees, or quantity.

---

### Q273 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.005(b)

Under TC 547.005(b), a person may not operate a motor vehicle bearing the words "school bus" unless the vehicle is used primarily to transport persons to or from school or a school-related ______.

**Answer:** activity

**Explanation:** The exception is for vehicles genuinely used for school transportation or school-related activities.

---

### Q274 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.005(b)

Under TC 547.005(b), the definition of "school" for this offense includes a church only if the church is:

- [x] Engaged in providing formal education
- [ ] Located within one mile of the school
- [ ] Operating a licensed day-care center
- [ ] Registered as a nonprofit organization

**Explanation:** 547.005(b)'s broad "school" definition extends to a church only when the church itself provides formal education.

---

### Q275 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.105(a)

Under TC 547.105(a), TxDOT shall adopt lighting standards for highway maintenance/construction vehicles and service vehicles that correlate with and conform as closely as possible to standards approved by the ______.

**Answer:** American Association of State Highway and Transportation Officials

**Explanation:** 547.105(a) references AASHTO — a different reference organization than those used for slow-moving-vehicle emblems (547.104) or school bus/air-conditioning standards (547.102-.103).

---

### Q276 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.105(a)

Under TC 547.105(a), TxDOT's lamp standards for highway maintenance/construction and service vehicles are meant to conform to standards approved by which organization?

- [x] American Association of State Highway and Transportation Officials (AASHTO)
- [ ] American Society of Agricultural Engineers
- [ ] Society of Automotive Engineers
- [ ] Federal Motor Carrier Safety Administration

**Explanation:** AASHTO is the specific reference body named in 547.105(a).

---

### Q277 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.105(b)

Under TC 547.105(b), TxDOT may adopt lighting standards that permit the use of ______ lights for identification purposes on highway maintenance/construction vehicles and service vehicles.

**Answer:** flashing

**Explanation:** 547.105(b) is narrowly about permitting flashing lights for identification, distinct from the general lighting equipment requirements elsewhere in Chapter 547.

---

### Q278 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.105(b)

Under TC 547.105(b), what purpose may flashing lights on highway maintenance/construction and service vehicles serve under TxDOT-adopted standards?

- [x] Identification purposes
- [ ] Warning purposes only during pursuits
- [ ] Signaling turns
- [ ] Displaying speed limits electronically

**Explanation:** The statute limits this authorization to identification purposes.

---

### Q279 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.105(c)

Under TC 547.105(c), the standards and specifications adopted under Section 547.105 are in ______ of the standards otherwise provided by Chapter 547 for lamps on vehicles.

**Answer:** lieu

**Explanation:** Because 547.105 standards replace (rather than supplement) the general Chapter 547 lamp rules, TxDOT's adopted standards can authorize things the general chapter otherwise restricts.

---

### Q280 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.105(c)

Per the BPOC instructor note on TC 547.105(c), which color of light may be authorized on certain highway maintenance or service vehicles under the TxDOT lighting standards adopted in lieu of the general Chapter 547 rules?

- [x] Blue
- [ ] Red
- [ ] Purple
- [ ] Green only

**Explanation:** Because 547.105(c) standards replace the general Chapter 547 lamp rules, TxDOT's standards can authorize blue lights on certain maintenance/service vehicles — an exception to the general restriction on blue lights elsewhere in the chapter.

---

### Q281 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.301(a)

Under TC 547.301(a), a visibility distance requirement is measured as if the vehicle were unloaded and on a straight, level, unlighted highway under normal ______ conditions.

**Answer:** atmospheric

**Explanation:** 547.301(a) standardizes visibility-distance testing to remove variables like cargo weight or weather.

---

### Q282 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.301(a)

Under TC 547.301(a), a lamp's visibility distance requirement is measured under which assumed conditions?

- [x] Vehicle unloaded, on a straight, level, unlighted highway, normal atmospheric conditions
- [ ] Vehicle fully loaded, on a curved roadway, at dusk
- [ ] Vehicle unloaded, on a lighted highway, in rain
- [ ] Vehicle loaded to capacity, on level ground, at night

**Explanation:** The statute specifies a single standardized test condition — unloaded, straight, level, unlighted, normal atmosphere.

---

### Q283 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.301(b)

Under TC 547.301(b), a mounted height requirement is measured from the ______ of the lamp or device to the ground, with the vehicle unloaded and on level ground.

**Answer:** center

**Explanation:** Height is measured to the lamp's center, not its top or bottom edge.

---

### Q284 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.301(b)

Under TC 547.301(b), a lamp's mounted-height requirement is measured from the ground to which point on the lamp?

- [x] The center of the lamp or device
- [ ] The top edge of the lamp housing
- [ ] The bottom edge of the lamp lens
- [ ] The highest point of light emission

**Explanation:** This measurement always assumes an unloaded vehicle on level ground, measured to the lamp's center.

---

### Q285 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.302(a)(2)

Under TC 547.302(a)(2), lights are required when light is insufficient or atmospheric conditions are unfavorable so that a person or vehicle on the highway is not clearly discernible at a distance of ______ feet ahead.

**Answer:** 1,000

**Explanation:** The 1,000-foot discernibility threshold is the general trigger for daytime low-visibility lighting.

---

### Q286 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.302(a)

Under TC 547.302(a), required lamps must be displayed at nighttime and also when visibility drops below what distance ahead?

- [x] 1,000 feet
- [ ] 500 feet
- [ ] 1,500 feet
- [ ] 300 feet
