**Explanation:** This threshold is distinct from the 500-foot and 300-foot thresholds used elsewhere for specific lamp types.

---

### Q287 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.302(c)

Under TC 547.302(c), at least ______ lighted lamp shall be displayed on each side of the front of a motor vehicle.

**Answer:** one

**Explanation:** 547.302(c) sets a floor of one lighted lamp per side of the front — the general "duty to display" baseline.

---

### Q288 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.302(c)

Under TC 547.302(c), how many lighted lamps must be displayed on each side of the front of a motor vehicle at minimum?

- [x] One
- [ ] Two
- [ ] Three
- [ ] Four

**Explanation:** This is a general baseline duty, separate from the specific two-headlamp requirement in 547.321.

---

### Q289 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.302(d)

Under TC 547.302(d), not more than ______ of the listed front lamps (headlamps, or lamps projecting a beam brighter than 300 candlepower) may be lighted at one time on the front of a motor vehicle.

**Answer:** four

**Explanation:** 547.302(d) caps simultaneous front lighting at four qualifying lamps.

---

### Q290 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.302(d)

Under TC 547.302(d), the four-lamp cap on the front of a motor vehicle applies to headlamps and any other lamp that projects a beam brighter than what intensity?

- [x] 300 candlepower
- [ ] 500 candlepower
- [ ] 150 candlepower
- [ ] 1,000 candlepower

**Explanation:** The 300-candlepower threshold reappears in 547.305(a) as well, where it also triggers a 75-foot beam-aiming restriction.

---

### Q291 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.303(a)

Under TC 547.303(a), unless expressly provided otherwise, a lighting device or reflector mounted on the rear of a vehicle must be or reflect ______.

**Answer:** red

**Explanation:** Red is the default rear color absent an express exception (like turn signals or stoplamps, which get their own color rules).

---

### Q292 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.303(a)

Under TC 547.303(a), the default color rule for a rear-mounted lighting device or reflector is:

- [x] Red
- [ ] Amber
- [ ] White
- [ ] Red or amber, operator's choice

**Explanation:** This is the baseline rule that 547.303(b) then carves an exception out of for signaling devices.

---

### Q293 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.303(b)

Under TC 547.303(b), a signaling device mounted on the rear of a vehicle may be red, amber, or ______.

**Answer:** yellow

**Explanation:** 547.303(b) carves signaling devices (like turn signals) out of the strict "must be red" rule in (a).

---

### Q294 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.303(b)

Under TC 547.303(b), which three colors are permitted for a rear-mounted signaling device?

- [x] Red, amber, or yellow
- [ ] Red, white, or blue
- [ ] Amber, green, or white
- [ ] Red, blue, or yellow

**Explanation:** This exception applies specifically to signaling devices, not to lighting devices or reflectors generally.

---

### Q295 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.304(a)-(b)

Under TC 547.304, the fixed-electric-lights exemption fully applies to a farm/fertilizer trailer or a boat trailer with a gross weight of ______ pounds or less (a), while a boat trailer under ______ pounds gets a partial exemption that still requires stoplamps and turn signal lamps (b).

**Answer:** 3,000 | 4,500

**Explanation:** 3,000 pounds is the full-exemption threshold in 547.304(a); a separate, heavier 4,500-pound threshold in (b) grants only a partial exemption that still requires 547.323 stoplamps and 547.324 turn signals.

---

### Q296 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.304(b)

Under TC 547.304(b), which two lighting requirements still apply to a boat trailer under 4,500 pounds even though most fixed-electric-lights rules don't?

- [x] Stoplamps (547.323) and turn signal lamps (547.324)
- [ ] Taillamps (547.322) and reflectors (547.325)
- [ ] Headlamps (547.321) and fog lamps (547.328)
- [ ] Hazard lamps (547.331) and spotlamps (547.327)

**Explanation:** 547.304(b) and (c) both carve out the same two exceptions — stoplamps and turn signals remain required even for otherwise-exempt boat trailers and mobile homes.

---

### Q297 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.304(d)

Under TC 547.304(d), a mobile home lighted as provided by this section may be moved only during ______.

**Answer:** daytime

**Explanation:** Because a permitted mobile home is exempt from most lighting rules, the tradeoff is that it can only be moved during daytime hours.

---

### Q298 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.304(d)

Under TC 547.304(d), a mobile home moved under the lighting exemption of Subsection (c) is restricted to being moved:

- [x] Only during daytime
- [ ] Only on state highways
- [ ] Only with a police escort
- [ ] Only below 45 mph

**Explanation:** No escort, highway-type, or speed restriction is imposed — only the daytime-only limitation.

---

### Q299 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.305(a)

Under TC 547.305(a), a lamp (other than a headlamp, spotlamp, auxiliary lamp, turn signal, or emergency/tow truck/school bus warning lamp) that projects a beam brighter than 300 candlepower must be directed so that no part of the high-intensity beam strikes the roadway more than ______ feet from the vehicle.

**Answer:** 75

**Explanation:** The 75-foot limit in 547.305(a) pairs with the same 300-candlepower threshold used in 547.302(d).

---

### Q300 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.305(a)

Under TC 547.305(a), a non-exempt lamp projecting a beam brighter than 300 candlepower must be aimed so the high-intensity portion doesn't strike the roadway beyond:

- [x] 75 feet from the vehicle
- [ ] 25 feet from the vehicle
- [ ] 150 feet from the vehicle
- [ ] 300 feet from the vehicle

**Explanation:** 75 feet is the specific aiming restriction distance in 547.305(a).

---

### Q301 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.305(b), (c)

Except as expressly authorized by law, TC 547.305(b) bars a red light visible from directly in front of the center of a vehicle other than a ______ vehicle, and 547.305(c) restricts red, white, or blue beacon, flashing, or alternating lights to specifically authorized uses.

**Answer:** police

**Explanation:** This ties back to 546.004(b), which confirms police vehicles aren't even required to display a front red light, but they're the one vehicle type allowed to; 547.305(c) then separately restricts red/white/blue beacon-type lights generally to authorized uses.

---

### Q302 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.305(c)

Under TC 547.305(c), which three colors of beacon/flashing/alternating lights are restricted to specifically authorized uses?

- [x] Red, white, and blue
- [ ] Red, amber, and blue
- [ ] Amber, green, and white
- [ ] Red, blue, and green

**Explanation:** Red, white, and blue beacon/flashing/alternating lights are restricted under 547.305(c) — amber/green lights (used for security patrol vehicles, escort flag vehicles, etc.) are handled under separate subsections.

---

### Q303 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.305(d)

Under TC 547.305(d), alternately flashing lighting equipment (547.701/547.702) is permitted only on a school bus, an authorized emergency vehicle, a properly marked church bus, or a ______ truck under specified conditions.

**Answer:** tow

**Explanation:** 547.305(d) lists exactly five permitted vehicle categories, including two tow-truck scenarios.

---

### Q304 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.305(d)

Under TC 547.305(d), a church bus is permitted to use alternately flashing lighting equipment only if it displays which words, front and rear, clearly discernible to other operators?

- [x] "Church bus"
- [ ] "School bus"
- [ ] "Emergency vehicle"
- [ ] "Caution: stopping"

**Explanation:** 547.305(d)(3) specifically requires the words "church bus" printed front and rear before a church bus may use alternately flashing equipment.

---

### Q305 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.305(e-1)

Under TC 547.305(e-1), a security patrol vehicle may only be equipped with green, amber, or ______ lights.

**Answer:** white

**Explanation:** Security patrol vehicles are restricted to green, amber, or white — notably excluding red or blue, which are reserved for authorized emergency vehicles and police.

---

### Q306 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.305(e-1)

Under TC 547.305(e-1), which colors may a security patrol vehicle be equipped with?

- [x] Green, amber, or white
- [ ] Red, amber, or white
- [ ] Blue, amber, or white
- [ ] Green, blue, or white

**Explanation:** This is a distinct color set from the escort flag vehicle rule (blue and amber) in 547.305(e-3).

---

### Q307 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.305(e-3)

Under TC 547.305(e-3), an escort flag vehicle may be equipped with alternating or flashing ______ and amber lights.

**Answer:** blue

**Explanation:** The escort flag vehicle exception specifically allows blue and amber together — a combination otherwise reserved for very few vehicle types.

---

### Q308 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.305(e-3)

Under TC 547.305(e-3), an escort flag vehicle (which precedes or follows an oversize/overweight vehicle) may be equipped with which light colors?

- [x] Alternating or flashing blue and amber
- [ ] Alternating or flashing red and amber
- [ ] Steady green and white
- [ ] Alternating or flashing red and blue

**Explanation:** 547.305(f)(1) defines "escort flag vehicle" as one that precedes or follows an oversize/overweight vehicle to facilitate safe movement.

---

### Q309 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.306(a)

Under TC 547.306(a), "LED ground effect lighting equipment" means light emitting diode technology attached to the ______ of a motorcycle or moped to illuminate the body or the ground below it.

**Answer:** underbody

**Explanation:** 547.306(a) defines the equipment by its mounting location (underbody) and illumination targets (the vehicle's body or the ground beneath it).

---

### Q310 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.306(a)

Under TC 547.306(a), LED ground effect lighting equipment is defined as LED technology attached to a motorcycle or moped's underbody to illuminate which of the following?

- [x] The body of the motorcycle/moped, or the ground below it
- [ ] The wheels only
- [ ] The license plate only
- [ ] The headlamp assembly

**Explanation:** The definition covers either illumination target — the vehicle's own body or the ground beneath it.

---

### Q311 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.306(b)

Under TC 547.306(b), a person may operate a motorcycle or moped equipped with LED ground effect lighting only if it emits a non-flashing amber or ______ light, and no other type of light.

**Answer:** white

**Explanation:** The light must be non-flashing and limited strictly to amber or white.

---

### Q312 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.306(b)

Under TC 547.306(b), LED ground effect lighting on a motorcycle or moped must emit which color(s) of light, and how?

- [x] Non-flashing amber or white light only
- [ ] Flashing amber or white light only
- [ ] Any color, as long as it's non-flashing
- [ ] Non-flashing red or blue light only

**Explanation:** No other color or flashing pattern is legal for this equipment under 547.306(b).

---

### Q313 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.321(a)-(b)

Under TC 547.321(a)-(b), a motor vehicle shall be equipped with at least ______ headlamps, with at least one mounted on each side of the front.

**Answer:** two

**Explanation:** 547.321(a)-(b) requires at least two headlamps arranged one per side of the front — matching the general "one lamp per side" duty in 547.302(c).

---

### Q314 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.321

How many headlamps must a motor vehicle have at minimum, and how must they be arranged?

- [x] At least two, with one mounted on each side of the front
- [ ] At least two, both mounted at the center of the front
- [ ] At least four, two on each side
- [ ] At least one, mounted at the center

**Explanation:** 547.321(a)-(b) requires at least two headlamps, one per side of the front.

---

### Q315 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.321(c)

Under TC 547.321(c), each headlamp shall be mounted at a height from ______ to ______ inches.

**Answer:** 24 | 54

**Explanation:** 24-54 inches is the headlamp height range — distinct from taillamp height (15-72 inches) and fog lamp height (12-30 inches) elsewhere in Subchapter E.

---

### Q316 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.321(c)

Under TC 547.321(c), the legal mounting height range for a headlamp is:

- [x] 24 to 54 inches
- [ ] 15 to 72 inches
- [ ] 12 to 30 inches
- [ ] 16 to 42 inches

**Explanation:** This range applies specifically to headlamps, not taillamps, fog lamps, or auxiliary driving lamps.

---

### Q317 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.3215

Under TC 547.3215, lighting, reflective devices, and associated equipment must comply with the current federal standards in 49 C.F.R. Section 571.108, or the federal standards in effect at the time the vehicle was ______.

**Answer:** manufactured

**Explanation:** 547.3215 ties Texas lighting law to the federal FMVSS 108 standard, either the current version or the version in effect when the vehicle was manufactured.

---

### Q318 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.3215

Under TC 547.3215, a vehicle's required lighting equipment must comply with which federal regulation?

- [x] 49 C.F.R. Section 571.108
- [ ] 49 C.F.R. Section 393.11
- [ ] 49 C.F.R. Section 571.208
- [ ] 29 C.F.R. Section 1910.132

**Explanation:** 547.3215 references FMVSS 108 (49 CFR 571.108) specifically.

---

### Q319 — Lighting — Model-Year & Effective-Date Rules
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.3215 (instructor note)

Per the instructor note on TC 547.3215, this federal-standard provision was adopted by the Texas Legislature in ______ and is retroactive to model year 1968, the year federal lighting standards were first written.

**Answer:** 1997

**Explanation:** TC 547.3215 was added in 1997 but applies retroactively back to the 1968 model year, when FMVSS 108 first took effect.

---

### Q320 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.3215 (instructor note)

Per the instructor note on TC 547.3215, on issues of light performance, mounting height, color, and number, when the federal standard and the Transportation Code differ, which one controls?

- [x] The federal standard
- [ ] The Transportation Code
- [ ] Whichever was adopted more recently
- [ ] Neither — the officer's department policy controls

**Explanation:** TC 547.3215 makes federal FMVSS 108 requirements control over the Transportation Code's own lighting provisions whenever the two differ.

---

### Q321 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.3215 (instructor note, FMVSS 108 Sec. 6.2.1)

Per the instructor note on TC 547.3215, Section 6.2.1 of the Federal 108 Lighting Standard provides that no additional lamp, reflective device, or other equipment may be installed that ______ the effectiveness of lighting equipment required by the standard.

**Answer:** impairs

**Explanation:** This "impairing the effectiveness of a required lamp" provision is what makes accessory lighting or tinting modifications a violation.

---

### Q322 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.3215 (instructor note, FMVSS 108 Sec. 6.2.1)

Per the instructor note on TC 547.3215, which of the following is given as an example of "impairing the effectiveness of a required lamp" under Section 6.2.1 of FMVSS 108?

- [x] Smoked taillamp covers or tinted headlamp lenses
- [ ] Installing an additional spotlamp
- [ ] Using factory fog lamps
- [ ] Replacing a bulb with an OEM-equivalent part

**Explanation:** The instructor note also lists stickers/decals obstructing a high-mounted stop lamp and bright accessory lights obscuring a side marker lamp as examples.

---

### Q323 — Lighting — Model-Year & Effective-Date Rules
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.322(a)-(b)

Under TC 547.322(a)-(b), a motor vehicle shall be equipped with at least two taillamps, except that a passenger car or truck manufactured or assembled before model year ______ needs only at least one taillamp.

**Answer:** 1960

**Explanation:** The 1960 cutoff for the reduced one-taillamp requirement is distinct from other cutoff years used elsewhere in Chapter 547 (1948, 1968, 1972).

---

### Q324 — Lighting — Model-Year & Effective-Date Rules
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.322

The general taillamp requirement is at least two, with an exception for passenger cars/trucks manufactured or assembled before which model year?

- [x] 1960
- [ ] 1948
- [ ] 1972
- [ ] 1968

**Explanation:** 1960 is the taillamp cutoff year, separate from the 1948 beam-equipment cutoff, 1968 federal-standard retroactivity, and 1972 reflector-visibility cutoff.

---

### Q325 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.322(c)

Under TC 547.322(c), taillamps shall be mounted on the rear of the vehicle at a height from ______ to ______ inches.

**Answer:** 15 | 72

**Explanation:** 15-72 inches is the taillamp height range, matching the reflector height range in 547.325(b) but differing from headlamp (24-54") and fog lamp (12-30") heights.

---

### Q326 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.322(c)

Under TC 547.322(c), the legal mounting height range for a taillamp is:

- [x] 15 to 72 inches
- [ ] 24 to 54 inches
- [ ] 12 to 30 inches
- [ ] 24 to 60 inches

**Explanation:** This range is specific to taillamps, distinct from headlamps and fog lamps.

---

### Q327 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.322(d)

Under TC 547.322(d), a taillamp shall emit a red light plainly visible at a distance of ______ feet from the rear of the vehicle.

**Answer:** 1,000

**Explanation:** The 1,000-foot taillamp visibility distance matches the general "duty to display" discernibility threshold in 547.302(a)(2).

---

### Q328 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.322(d)

Under TC 547.322(d), a taillamp's red light must be plainly visible from the rear at a distance of:

- [x] 1,000 feet
- [ ] 500 feet
- [ ] 300 feet
- [ ] 1,500 feet

**Explanation:** 1,000 feet is the taillamp visibility distance, shared with several other "general" lighting distances in Chapter 547.

---

### Q329 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.322(f)

Under TC 547.322(f), a taillamp or separate lamp shall be constructed and mounted to emit a white light that illuminates the rear license plate and makes it clearly legible at a distance of ______ feet from the rear.

**Answer:** 50

**Explanation:** 50 feet is the specific legibility distance for the white rear license-plate lamp, much shorter than the red taillamp's 1,000-foot visibility requirement.

---

### Q330 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.322(f)

Under TC 547.322(f), the rear license-plate lamp must make the plate legible from what distance?

- [x] 50 feet
- [ ] 100 feet
- [ ] 25 feet
- [ ] 150 feet

**Explanation:** This white-light plate-illumination distance (50 feet) is much shorter than the red taillamp visibility distance (1,000 feet).

### Q331 — Lighting — Model-Year & Effective-Date Rules
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.323(a)-(b)

Under TC 547.323(a)-(b), a motor vehicle shall be equipped with at least two stoplamps, except a passenger car manufactured or assembled before model year ______ needs only at least one.

**Answer:** 1960

**Explanation:** The general stoplamp rule mirrors the taillamp rule — two required, with a pre-1960 passenger car exception allowing just one.

---

### Q332 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.323(a)

Under TC 547.323, how many stoplamps must a motor vehicle generally have?

- [x] At least two
- [ ] At least one
- [ ] At least three
- [ ] At least four

**Explanation:** Two is the general rule, with the pre-1960 passenger car exception allowing just one.

---

### Q333 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.323(d)

Under TC 547.323(d), a stoplamp shall emit a red or amber light, or a color between red and amber, visible in normal sunlight at a distance of at least ______ feet from the rear of the vehicle.

**Answer:** 300

**Explanation:** 300 feet is the stoplamp's daytime visibility distance — shorter than the 1,000-foot nighttime taillamp distance because it's measured "in normal sunlight."

---

### Q334 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.323(d)

Under TC 547.323(d), a stoplamp must be visible in normal sunlight from the rear at a minimum distance of:

- [x] 300 feet
- [ ] 500 feet
- [ ] 1,000 feet
- [ ] 150 feet

**Explanation:** 300 feet is measured "in normal sunlight," a daytime standard distinct from the nighttime 1,000-foot taillamp rule.

---

### Q335 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.323(g)

Under TC 547.323(g), a high-mounted stoplamp complies with federal standards if it varies in intensity ______ or fewer times but never deactivates while the vehicle brakes for not more than two seconds.

**Answer:** four

**Explanation:** This provision (added 2025) specifically permits limited intensity-varying (pulsing) high-mount stoplamps as still compliant with FMVSS 108, so long as the lamp doesn't go dark during braking.

---

### Q336 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.323(g)

Under TC 547.323(g), a high-mounted stoplamp that varies in intensity may do so how many times, without deactivating, during up to a two-second brake application?

- [x] Four or fewer times
- [ ] Two or fewer times
- [ ] Six or fewer times
- [ ] Any number of times

**Explanation:** The lamp must never fully deactivate during that two-second window, regardless of how many times its intensity varies (up to four).

---

### Q337 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.324(a)

Under TC 547.324(a), turn signal lamps must indicate the operator's intent to turn by displaying flashing lights to the front and rear of the vehicle, on the side toward which the ______ is to be made.

**Answer:** turn

**Explanation:** Turn signals must flash on the side of the intended turn, front and rear simultaneously.

---

### Q338 — Lighting — Model-Year & Effective-Date Rules
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.324(b)

Under TC 547.324(b), the turn-signal-lamp requirement does not apply to which vehicles?

- [x] A passenger car or truck less than 80 inches wide manufactured or assembled before model year 1960
- [ ] Any vehicle manufactured before 1948
- [ ] Any boat trailer under 3,000 pounds
- [ ] Any mobile home moved during daytime

**Explanation:** 547.324(b)'s exception requires both conditions — under 80 inches wide AND pre-1960 — to be exempt from turn signal lamps.

---

### Q339 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.324(d)(1)

Under TC 547.324(d)(1), a turn signal lamp mounted on the front of the vehicle must emit a white or amber light, or a color between white and ______.

**Answer:** amber

**Explanation:** Front turn signals use the white/amber range, while rear turn signals use the red/amber range.

---

### Q340 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.324(d)

Under TC 547.324(d), a front-mounted turn signal lamp must emit which color(s)?

- [x] White or amber, or a color between white and amber
- [ ] Red or amber, or a color between red and amber
- [ ] White or red, or a color between white and red
- [ ] Amber only

**Explanation:** The white/amber range is specific to front-mounted turn signals; rear-mounted signals use red/amber instead.

---

### Q341 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.324(d)(2)

Under TC 547.324(d)(2), a turn signal lamp mounted on the rear of the vehicle must emit a red or amber light, or a color between red and ______.

**Answer:** amber

**Explanation:** Rear turn signals share the same red/amber color rule as rear stoplamps (547.323(d)).

---

### Q342 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.324(d)

Under TC 547.324(d), a rear-mounted turn signal lamp's color options match which other rear lamp's color rule?

- [x] The stoplamp (red or amber, or between)
- [ ] The taillamp (red only)
- [ ] The reflector (red only)
- [ ] The backup lamp (amber or white)

**Explanation:** Both rear stoplamps (547.323(d)) and rear turn signals (547.324(d)(2)) share the identical red-or-amber-or-between color rule.

---

### Q343 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.324(e)

Under TC 547.324(e), a turn signal lamp must be visible in normal sunlight at a distance of at least 500 feet if the vehicle is at least 80 inches wide, or at least ______ feet if the vehicle is less than 80 inches wide.

**Answer:** 300

**Explanation:** Wider vehicles get the longer 500-foot visibility requirement; narrower vehicles only need 300 feet.

---

### Q344 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.324(e)

Under TC 547.324(e), which vehicle width triggers the longer 500-foot turn-signal visibility requirement (versus 300 feet)?

- [x] At least 80 inches wide
- [ ] At least 96 inches wide
- [ ] At least 102 inches wide
- [ ] Any width — 500 feet always applies

**Explanation:** 80 inches is the width threshold used repeatedly in Subchapter F (large-vehicle lighting) as well.

---

### Q345 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.325(a)

Under TC 547.325(a), a motor vehicle, trailer, semitrailer, or pole trailer shall be equipped with at least ______ red reflectors on the rear (which may be part of a taillamp).

**Answer:** two

**Explanation:** 547.325(a) explicitly allows a red reflector to double as part of the taillamp, rather than requiring a fully separate unit.

---

### Q346 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.325(a)

Under TC 547.325(a), a rear reflector may be:

- [x] Included as part of a taillamp
- [ ] Only a standalone unit, never combined with another lamp
- [ ] Amber instead of red if the vehicle is a trailer
- [ ] Optional if the vehicle has stoplamps

**Explanation:** 547.325(a) permits combining the reflector with the taillamp.

---

### Q347 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.325(b)(1)

Under TC 547.325(b)(1), a reflector shall be mounted at a height from ______ to ______ inches.

**Answer:** 15 | 60

**Explanation:** The reflector height range (15-60") is close to, but not identical to, the taillamp range (15-72") — the reflector's upper bound is lower.

---

### Q348 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.325(b)(1)

Under TC 547.325(b), the legal mounting height range for a rear reflector is:

- [x] 15 to 60 inches
- [ ] 15 to 72 inches
- [ ] 24 to 60 inches
- [ ] 12 to 30 inches

**Explanation:** This range is specific to reflectors under 547.325, distinct from the taillamp range in 547.322(c).

---

### Q349 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.325(b)(2)(A)

Under TC 547.325(b)(2)(A), a reflector shall be visible at night at all distances from ______ to ______ feet when directly in front of lawful lower beams of headlamps.

**Answer:** 100 | 600

**Explanation:** 100-600 feet is the standard reflector visibility range against lower beams — a shorter range (100-350 feet) applies instead for upper beams on pre-1972 vehicles.

---

### Q350 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.325(b)(2)(A)

Under TC 547.325(b)(2)(A), the standard nighttime visibility range for a rear reflector, measured against lawful lower headlamp beams, is:

- [x] 100 to 600 feet
- [ ] 50 to 500 feet
- [ ] 100 to 350 feet
- [ ] 200 to 600 feet

**Explanation:** 100-350 feet is the shorter alternate range that applies only for upper beams on pre-1972 vehicles (547.325(b)(2)(B)).

---

### Q351 — Lighting — Model-Year & Effective-Date Rules
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.325(b)(2)(B)

Under TC 547.325(b)(2)(B), a reflector on a vehicle manufactured or assembled before January 1, ______, must be visible from 100 to 350 feet when directly in front of lawful upper beams.

**Answer:** 1972

**Explanation:** This pre-1972 cutoff for upper-beam reflector visibility reappears identically in 547.355(b)(2) for large-vehicle reflectors.

---

### Q352 — Lighting — Model-Year & Effective-Date Rules
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.325(b)(2)(B)

Under TC 547.325(b)(2)(B), the shorter 100-350-foot reflector visibility standard (measured against upper headlamp beams) applies only to vehicles manufactured or assembled before:

- [x] January 1, 1972
- [ ] January 1, 1960
- [ ] September 4, 1948
- [ ] September 1, 1995

**Explanation:** 1972 is the specific cutoff date, distinct from the 1960 taillamp/stoplamp cutoff and the 1948 single-beam-equipment cutoff.

---

### Q353 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.326(a)

Under TC 547.326(a), a vehicle not specifically required to have lamps shall be equipped with at least one lamp that emits a white light visible at a distance of at least ______ feet from the front.

**Answer:** 1,000

**Explanation:** This minimum equipment provision exists for vehicles that don't fall under the specific headlamp/taillamp rules, such as animal-drawn vehicles or vehicles exempted under 547.002.

---

### Q354 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.326(a)

Under TC 547.326(a), the minimum lighting equipment for an otherwise-unequipped vehicle includes a white front lamp visible from what distance?

- [x] 1,000 feet
- [ ] 500 feet
- [ ] 300 feet
- [ ] 1,500 feet

**Explanation:** 1,000 feet matches the general visibility distance used for taillamps and the 547.302(a)(2) discernibility threshold.

---

### Q355 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.326(a)(2)

Under TC 547.326(a)(2), as an alternative to two red rear lamps, a vehicle may use one red lamp visible 1,000 feet from the rear plus two red reflectors visible when illuminated by lawful lower beams at all distances from 100 to ______ feet.

**Answer:** 600

**Explanation:** This alternative combination (one red lamp plus two reflectors) uses the same 100-600 foot reflector visibility range found in 547.325(b)(2)(A).

---

### Q356 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.326(b)

Under TC 547.326(b), the minimum-lighting-equipment rule of Section 547.326 applies to which additional category of vehicles besides those lacking specific lamp requirements?

- [x] Animal-drawn vehicles and vehicles exempted from Chapter 547 by Section 547.002
- [ ] Only commercial motor vehicles
- [ ] Only farm tractors
- [ ] Only vehicles manufactured before 1948

**Explanation:** 547.326(b) extends this baseline lighting floor to animal-drawn vehicles and to vehicles otherwise exempted from Chapter 547 under 547.002.

---

### Q357 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.327

Under TC 547.327, a motor vehicle may be equipped with not more than ______ spotlamps, aimed so no part of the high-intensity beam strikes the windshield, window, mirror, or occupant of another vehicle in use.

**Answer:** two

**Explanation:** 547.327(a) caps spotlamps at two, and (b) restricts their aim to avoid blinding other road users.

---

### Q358 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.327(b)

Under TC 547.327, a spotlamp must be aimed to avoid striking which of the following on another vehicle in use?

- [x] The windshield, window, mirror, or occupant
- [ ] Only the license plate
- [ ] Only the rearview mirror
- [ ] Only the headlamps

**Explanation:** 547.327(b) lists four specific things a spotlamp's high-intensity beam must not strike.

---

### Q359 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.328(a)-(b)(1)

Under TC 547.328(a)-(b)(1), a motor vehicle may be equipped with not more than two fog lamps, mounted on the front at a height from ______ to ______ inches.

**Answer:** 12 | 30

**Explanation:** 12-30 inches is the lowest lamp-height range in Subchapter E, appropriate for a lamp meant to illuminate close to the road surface under fog.

---

### Q360 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.328(b)(1)

Under TC 547.328, the legal mounting height range for a fog lamp is:

- [x] 12 to 30 inches
- [ ] 16 to 42 inches
- [ ] 24 to 42 inches
- [ ] 24 to 54 inches

**Explanation:** This is the lowest of the mounting-height ranges in Subchapter E.

---

### Q361 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.328(b)(2)

Under TC 547.328(b)(2), a fog lamp mounted left of center must be aimed so the high-intensity beam at a distance of 25 feet is no higher than ______ inches below the level of the center of the lamp.

**Answer:** four

**Explanation:** The four-inch-below-center rule at 25 feet is the fog lamp's specific aiming check.

---

### Q362 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.328(b)(2)

Under TC 547.328(b)(2), the fog lamp aiming restriction is measured at what distance ahead of the vehicle?

- [x] 25 feet
- [ ] 75 feet
- [ ] 50 feet
- [ ] 100 feet

**Explanation:** 25 feet is the fog lamp check distance; 75 feet is instead used for one of the single-beam headlamp aiming checks in 547.334(b)(2)(B).

---

### Q363 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.330

Under TC 547.330, a motor vehicle may be equipped with no more than two auxiliary driving lamps, mounted on the front at a height from ______ to ______ inches.

**Answer:** 16 | 42

**Explanation:** 16-42 inches for auxiliary driving lamps is close to, but distinct from, the fog lamp range (12-30") and headlamp range (24-54").

---

### Q364 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.330

Under TC 547.330, the legal mounting height range for an auxiliary driving lamp is:

- [x] 16 to 42 inches
- [ ] 24 to 42 inches
- [ ] 12 to 30 inches
- [ ] 24 to 54 inches

**Explanation:** This range is specific to auxiliary driving lamps, distinct from fog lamps and headlamps.

---

### Q365 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.331(b)(2)

Under TC 547.331(b)(2), hazard lamps shall be visible at a distance of at least ______ feet in normal sunlight.

**Answer:** 500

**Explanation:** 500 feet matches the wide-vehicle turn-signal visibility distance in 547.324(e)(1).

---

### Q366 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.331(b)

Under TC 547.331(b), hazard lamps must be mounted at the same level and spaced as widely apart as practicable on which parts of the vehicle?

- [x] The front and the rear
- [ ] The front only
- [ ] The rear only
- [ ] The sides only

**Explanation:** Like turn signal lamps, hazard lamps must be mounted symmetrically front and rear.

---

### Q367 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.331(c)

Under TC 547.331(c), hazard lamps display simultaneously flashing lights that emit a white or amber light on the front, or a red or amber light on the ______.

**Answer:** rear

**Explanation:** Hazard lamps use the identical white/amber-front, red/amber-rear color scheme as turn signal lamps (547.324(d)).

---

### Q368 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.331(c)

Under TC 547.331(c), the color scheme for hazard lamps matches the color scheme used for which other lamp type?

- [x] Turn signal lamps
- [ ] Taillamps
- [ ] Fog lamps
- [ ] Backup lamps

**Explanation:** Both warn of directional or hazard conditions and share the same white/amber-front, red/amber-rear color rule.

---

### Q369 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.332(1)-(2)

Under TC 547.332(1)-(2), a motor vehicle may have not more than two side cowl or fender lamps, and not more than two running board courtesy lamps (one per side), all emitting an amber or white light without ______.

**Answer:** glare

**Explanation:** Both of these optional accessory lamp types share the same amber-or-white-without-glare color rule.

---

### Q370 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.332(1)-(2)

Under TC 547.332, side cowl/fender lamps and running board courtesy lamps must emit which color(s)?

- [x] Amber or white, without glare
- [ ] Red or amber
- [ ] White only
- [ ] Any color, as long as non-flashing

**Explanation:** These two optional lamp types share an identical color and no-glare rule.

---

### Q371 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.332(3)

Under TC 547.332(3), one or more backup lamps may emit an amber or white light only when the vehicle is not moving ______.

**Answer:** forward

**Explanation:** Backup lamps are conditioned on the vehicle not moving forward — i.e., stopped or in reverse.

---

### Q372 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.332(3)

Under TC 547.332(3), a backup lamp may be displayed:

- [x] Separately or in combination with another lamp
- [ ] Only as a standalone unit
- [ ] Only when combined with the taillamp
- [ ] Only when combined with the stoplamp

**Explanation:** 547.332(3)(B) explicitly allows a backup lamp to be shown alone or combined with another lamp.

---

### Q373 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.333(b)(1)

Under TC 547.333(b)(1), the uppermost distribution of light (high beam) must be aimed and emit light sufficient to reveal a person or vehicle at a distance of at least ______ feet ahead.

**Answer:** 450

**Explanation:** 450 feet for the high beam contrasts with the shorter 150-foot minimum for the low beam under 547.333(b)(2).

---

### Q374 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.333(b)

Under TC 547.333(b), the uppermost (high) beam must reveal a person or vehicle at a distance of at least:

- [x] 450 feet
- [ ] 150 feet
- [ ] 200 feet
- [ ] 500 feet

**Explanation:** 450 feet is the high-beam minimum; 150 feet is the low-beam minimum under the same section.

---

### Q375 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.333(b)(2)

Under TC 547.333(b)(2), the lowermost distribution of light (low beam) must be aimed and emit light sufficient to reveal a person or vehicle at a distance of at least ______ feet ahead.

**Answer:** 150

**Explanation:** 150 feet is the low-beam minimum reveal distance, well below the 450-foot high-beam minimum.

---

### Q376 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.333(b)(2)

Under TC 547.333(b)(2), the low beam's minimum reveal distance is aimed so that it also:

- [x] Does not project into the eyes of an approaching vehicle's operator
- [ ] Exceeds the high beam's distance requirement
- [ ] Illuminates only the shoulder of the roadway
- [ ] Matches the fog lamp's 25-foot aim check

**Explanation:** Beyond the 150-foot minimum, the low beam must specifically avoid glaring an oncoming operator — the core anti-glare purpose of a low beam.

---

### Q377 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.333(c)

Under TC 547.333(c), an operator approaching an oncoming vehicle within 500 feet must select the lowermost beam, and an operator approaching a vehicle from the rear within ______ feet may not select the uppermost distribution of light.

**Answer:** 300

**Explanation:** The oncoming-vehicle dimming distance (500 feet) is longer than the following-vehicle dimming distance (300 feet), reflecting that head-on glare is more hazardous.

---

### Q378 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.333(c)

Under TC 547.333(c), at what distance from an oncoming vehicle must a driver switch to low beams?

- [x] Within 500 feet
- [ ] Within 300 feet
- [ ] Within 1,000 feet
- [ ] Within 150 feet

**Explanation:** 500 feet is the oncoming-vehicle dimming trigger; 300 feet is instead the following-vehicle (rear-approach) dimming trigger.

---

### Q379 — Lighting — Model-Year & Effective-Date Rules
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.333(d)

Under TC 547.333(d), a motor vehicle of model year 1948 or later with multiple-beam lighting equipment shall have a beam indicator that is visible without glare to the operator and lighted only when the ______ distribution of light is in use.

**Answer:** uppermost

**Explanation:** The beam indicator only lights up when the high beams are engaged, alerting the driver.

---

### Q380 — Lighting — Model-Year & Effective-Date Rules
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.333(d)

Under TC 547.333(d), the beam (high-beam) indicator requirement applies to motor vehicles of what model year or later?

- [x] 1948
- [ ] 1960
- [ ] 1968
- [ ] 1972

**Explanation:** 1948 is also the cutoff year referenced in 547.334(a)(2) for single-beam equipment eligibility.

---

### Q381 — Lighting — Model-Year & Effective-Date Rules
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.334(a)

Under TC 547.334(a), single-beam lighting equipment is permitted in lieu of multiple-beam equipment for a farm tractor or a motor vehicle manufactured and sold before September 4, ______.

**Answer:** 1948

**Explanation:** The permitted single-beam category has two prongs — farm tractors (regardless of age) and pre-September-4-1948 motor vehicles.

---

### Q382 — Lighting — Model-Year & Effective-Date Rules
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.334(a)

Under TC 547.334(a), single-beam headlamp systems are permitted as an alternative to multiple-beam equipment for which vehicles?

- [x] Farm tractors, and motor vehicles manufactured and sold before September 4, 1948
- [ ] Any motorcycle or moped
- [ ] Any vehicle registered as an antique
- [ ] Farm tractors only

**Explanation:** The single-beam exception has two independent prongs, not just farm tractors alone.

---

### Q383 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.334(b)(1)

Under TC 547.334(b)(1), the single-beam headlamp system must emit a light sufficient to reveal a person or vehicle at a distance of at least ______ feet.

**Answer:** 200

**Explanation:** 200 feet sits between the multiple-beam system's 150-foot low-beam and 450-foot high-beam minimums under 547.333(b).

---

### Q384 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.334(b)

Under TC 547.334(b), the single-beam system's minimum reveal distance falls between which two multiple-beam distances?

- [x] Between the 150-foot low beam and 450-foot high beam minimums
- [ ] Between the 300-foot and 500-foot turn signal distances
- [ ] Between the 1,000-foot taillamp and 500-foot hazard lamp distances
- [ ] Between the 25-foot and 75-foot fog lamp distances

**Explanation:** At 200 feet, the single-beam minimum sits between the low-beam and high-beam minimums of the multiple-beam system.

---

### Q385 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.334(b)(2)(A)

Under TC 547.334(b)(2)(A), the single-beam system must be aimed so no part of the high-intensity beam projects higher than ______ inches below the level of the center of the lamp at a distance of 25 feet ahead.

**Answer:** five

**Explanation:** Five inches below center at 25 feet for single-beam equipment is close to, but distinct from, the four-inches-below-center rule for fog lamps under 547.328(b)(2).

---

### Q386 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.334(b)(2)(A)

Under TC 547.334(b)(2)(A), the single-beam aiming check at 25 feet ahead limits the beam to no higher than how far below the lamp's center?

- [x] Five inches
- [ ] Four inches
- [ ] Ten inches
- [ ] Two inches

**Explanation:** Four inches below center is instead the fog lamp aiming limit at the same 25-foot distance (547.328(b)(2)).

---

### Q387 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.334(b)(2)(B)

Under TC 547.334(b)(2)(B), the single-beam system must be aimed so no part of the high-intensity beam projects higher than ______ inches above the ground at a distance of 75 feet ahead.

**Answer:** 42

**Explanation:** 42 inches above ground at 75 feet is the second of two aiming checks for single-beam equipment.

---

### Q388 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.334(b)(2)(B)

Under TC 547.334(b)(2)(B), the second single-beam aiming check, measured 75 feet ahead, limits the beam to no higher than what height above the ground?

- [x] 42 inches
- [ ] 54 inches
- [ ] 30 inches
- [ ] 60 inches

**Explanation:** 42 inches above ground at 75 feet is the second single-beam aiming check; note 42 inches also happens to be the upper end of the auxiliary-driving-lamp mounting height range (547.330), a coincidental overlap.

---

### Q389 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.351

Under TC 547.351, the color, mounting, and visibility requirements of Subchapter F apply only to equipment on a vehicle described by Section ______.

**Answer:** 547.352

**Explanation:** 547.351 is purely a scoping provision that points to 547.352 for the actual list of covered vehicles.

---

### Q390 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.351

Under TC 547.351, which section defines which vehicles the Subchapter F (additional large-vehicle lighting) color/mounting/visibility rules apply to?

- [x] Section 547.352
- [ ] Section 547.301
- [ ] Section 547.321
- [ ] Section 547.002

**Explanation:** 547.351 relies entirely on 547.352's vehicle list for its scope.

---

### Q391 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.352(1)

Under TC 547.352(1), a bus, truck, trailer, or semitrailer that is at least ______ inches wide must be equipped with front and rear clearance lamps, four side marker lamps, four reflectors, and hazard lamps.

**Answer:** 80

**Explanation:** 80 inches is the width threshold that triggers the full additional-lighting package under 547.352(1).

---

### Q392 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.352(1)

Under TC 547.352(1), a bus, truck, trailer, or semitrailer at least 80 inches wide must have how many side marker lamps and how many reflectors?

- [x] Four side marker lamps and four reflectors
- [ ] Two side marker lamps and two reflectors
- [ ] Four side marker lamps and two reflectors
- [ ] Two side marker lamps and four reflectors

**Explanation:** 547.352(1)(C)-(D) requires four side marker lamps and four reflectors — one of each near the front and near the rear, on each side.

---

### Q393 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.352(2)-(3)

Under TC 547.352(2)-(3), a bus or truck at least ______ feet long, and a trailer or semitrailer of the same length, must be equipped with hazard lamps meeting Section 547.331.

**Answer:** 30

**Explanation:** 30 feet is the length threshold that triggers mandatory hazard lamps for buses, trucks, trailers, and semitrailers under 547.352(2)-(3).

---

### Q394 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.352(3)

Under TC 547.352(3), a trailer or semitrailer at least 30 feet long must be equipped with side marker lamps and reflectors mounted how?

- [x] Centrally mounted on each side, with respect to the length of the vehicle
- [ ] Near the front only
- [ ] Near the rear only
- [ ] At both the front and rear on each side

**Explanation:** The 30-foot-long trailer/semitrailer rule requires just one centrally-mounted side marker lamp and reflector per side — different from the near-front/near-rear pattern used for the 80-inch-wide rule.

---

### Q395 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.352(4)(C)

Under TC 547.352(4)(C), a pole trailer's combination marker lamp must emit an ______ light to the front and a red light to the rear and side, mounted on the rearmost support for the load.

**Answer:** amber

**Explanation:** This single combination lamp does triple duty — amber forward, red to the rear and side — mounted at the rearmost load support to mark the trailer's maximum width.

---

### Q396 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.352(4)

Under TC 547.352(4), a pole trailer's combination marker lamp is mounted where, and indicates what?

- [x] On the rearmost support for the load, to indicate the maximum width of the trailer
- [ ] On the front bumper, to indicate the vehicle's presence
- [ ] On the tractor cab, to indicate the trailer's length
- [ ] On the pole trailer's bolster, to indicate reflector compliance

**Explanation:** The rearmost-support mounting location is specific to marking the pole trailer's maximum width.

---

### Q397 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.353(a)

Under TC 547.353(a), a clearance lamp, identification lamp, side marker lamp, or reflector mounted on the front, side near the front, or center of the vehicle must be or reflect ______.

**Answer:** amber

**Explanation:** Amber is required at the front, matching the front color used for the pole trailer's combination marker lamp in 547.352(4)(C).

---

### Q398 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.353(a)

Under TC 547.353(a), the front-area color rule (amber) for large-vehicle marker equipment matches which other Chapter 547 color rule?

- [x] The pole trailer's front-facing combination marker lamp color (547.352(4))
- [ ] The taillamp color rule (547.322)
- [ ] The security patrol vehicle color rule (547.305(e-1))
- [ ] The LED ground effect lighting color rule (547.306)

**Explanation:** Both provisions use amber for front-facing large-vehicle marker equipment.

---

### Q399 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.353(b)

Under TC 547.353(b), a clearance lamp, identification lamp, side marker lamp, or reflector mounted on the rear or the side near the rear of the vehicle must be or reflect ______.

**Answer:** red

**Explanation:** Red is required at the rear, mirroring the general rear-lamp default color rule in 547.303(a).

---

### Q400 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.353(b)

Under TC 547.353(b), the rear-area color rule for large-vehicle marker equipment is:

- [x] Red
- [ ] Amber
- [ ] White
- [ ] Red or amber

**Explanation:** Red is required at the rear, without the amber option that rear signaling devices get under 547.303(b).

---

### Q401 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.354(a)(1)

Under TC 547.354(a)(1), a large-vehicle reflector under Subchapter F shall be mounted at a height from ______ to ______ inches.

**Answer:** 24 | 60

**Explanation:** Large vehicles under Subchapter F have a higher reflector-height floor (24 inches) than the general reflector rule in 547.325(b) (15 inches), reflecting their typically taller structure.

---

### Q402 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.354(a)

Under TC 547.354(a), the Subchapter F reflector height range (24-60 inches) differs from the general reflector height range in 547.325(b) (15-60 inches) in which way?

- [x] It has a higher minimum (24 inches instead of 15)
- [ ] It has a lower minimum (10 inches instead of 15)
- [ ] It has a higher maximum (72 inches instead of 60)
- [ ] It is identical, with no difference

**Explanation:** Both ranges share the same 60-inch maximum, but Subchapter F raises the floor to 24 inches.

---

### Q403 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.355(a)

Under TC 547.355(a), a clearance lamp, identification lamp, or side marker lamp shall be visible and recognizable under normal atmospheric conditions at all distances from ______ to ______ feet from the vehicle.

**Answer:** 50 | 500

**Explanation:** 50-500 feet applies to clearance, identification, and side marker lamps specifically under 547.355(a).

---

### Q404 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.355(a)

Under TC 547.355(a), the visibility range for a large-vehicle clearance, identification, or side marker lamp is:

- [x] 50 to 500 feet
- [ ] 100 to 600 feet
- [ ] 15 to 72 feet
- [ ] 24 to 60 feet

**Explanation:** 50-500 feet is a distance range for lamps; the 100-600 foot range instead applies to reflectors under 547.355(b).

---

### Q405 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.355(b)

Under TC 547.355(b), a reflector on a Subchapter F vehicle must be visible at nighttime at all distances from ______ to ______ feet from the vehicle when directly in front of lawful lower beams.

**Answer:** 100 | 600

**Explanation:** This mirrors the general reflector visibility range in 547.325(b)(2)(A) exactly.

---

### Q406 — Lighting — Model-Year & Effective-Date Rules
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.355(b)

Under TC 547.355(b), which vehicles get the shorter 100-350-foot reflector visibility range instead of the standard 100-600-foot range?

- [x] Vehicles manufactured or assembled before January 1, 1972, measured against upper beams
- [ ] Vehicles manufactured after 1995
- [ ] Any pole trailer regardless of age
- [ ] Any vehicle under 30 feet long

**Explanation:** This mirrors 547.325(b)(2)(B) exactly — pre-1972 vehicles get a shorter reflector-visibility range when measured against upper (rather than lower) headlamp beams.

---

### Q407 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.371(a)

Under TC 547.371(a), a farm tractor, self-propelled unit of farm equipment, or implement of husbandry shall be equipped with at least one red lamp visible at a distance of at least ______ feet from the rear, and at least two red reflectors visible at all distances from 100 to ______ feet from the rear when directly in front of lawful lower beams of headlamps.

**Answer:** 1,000 | 600

**Explanation:** The rear lamp's 1,000-foot visibility matches the general taillamp rule in 547.322(d), and the 100-600 foot reflector range matches the general reflector rule in 547.325(b)(2)(A).

---

### Q408 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.371(a)(3)

Under TC 547.371(a)(3), a farm tractor's required red reflectors must be visible at all distances from 100 feet to how many feet from the rear when directly in front of lawful lower headlamp beams?

- [x] 600 feet
- [ ] 500 feet
- [ ] 350 feet
- [ ] 1,000 feet

**Explanation:** 100-600 feet matches the general reflector-visibility range used elsewhere in Chapter 547.

---

### Q409 — Lighting — Model-Year & Effective-Date Rules
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.371(b)

Under TC 547.371(b), a farm tractor, self-propelled unit of farm equipment, or implement of husbandry manufactured or assembled on or before January 1, 1972, is required to be equipped as provided by Subsection (a) only at the times specified by Section 547.302(a), and ______ lamps are not required.

**Answer:** hazard

**Explanation:** The pre-1972 exception drops the hazard-lamp requirement while still requiring the other equipment at 547.302(a) times (nighttime or low visibility).

---

### Q410 — Lighting — Model-Year & Effective-Date Rules
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.371(b)

Under TC 547.371(b), which requirement is waived for a farm tractor, self-propelled farm equipment unit, or implement of husbandry manufactured on or before January 1, 1972?

- [x] The hazard lamp requirement
- [ ] The headlamp requirement
- [ ] The rear red lamp requirement
- [ ] The reflector requirement

**Explanation:** Only the hazard-lamp requirement is dropped for pre-1972 equipment; the other equipment items still apply at the times specified by 547.302(a).

---

### Q411 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.372

Under TC 547.372, a towed unit of farm equipment or implement of husbandry that extends more than four feet to the rear of the tractor (or obscures a light) must display a rear reflector that is ______, while a towed object extending more than four feet to the left of the tractor's centerline must display a front reflector that is ______.

**Answer:** red | amber

**Explanation:** 547.372(a) requires rear red reflectors for rearward extensions beyond four feet; 547.372(b) requires a front amber reflector for leftward extensions beyond four feet — both visible from 100 to 600 feet under lawful lower beams.

---

### Q412 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.372

Under TC 547.372, at what minimum extension distance does a towed farm implement trigger the additional reflector requirements (either to the rear or to the left of the tractor's centerline)?

- [x] More than four feet
- [ ] More than two feet
- [ ] More than six feet
- [ ] More than eight feet

**Explanation:** Both the rearward and leftward reflector requirements in 547.372 are triggered by an extension of more than four feet.

---

### Q413 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.382(a)

Under TC 547.382(a), a vehicle transporting a load that extends to the rear at least four feet beyond the bed or body must display, at the extreme end of the load, two red lamps visible at a distance of at least ______ feet from the rear.

**Answer:** 500

**Explanation:** 500 feet is the projecting-load rear-lamp visibility distance, shorter than the 1,000-foot general taillamp distance because the load itself (not the vehicle body) is the light source.

---

### Q414 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.382(a)

Under TC 547.382(a), a vehicle with a load extending at least four feet beyond its rear must display which combination of equipment at the extreme end of the load?

- [x] Two red lamps, two red reflectors, and two red side lamps indicating maximum overhang
- [ ] One amber lamp and one red reflector only
- [ ] A slow-moving-vehicle emblem only
- [ ] Two white lamps and one red flag

**Explanation:** 547.382(a) requires all three: red rear lamps, red reflectors indicating width, and red side lamps indicating overhang.

---

### Q415 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.382(b)

Under TC 547.382(b), at all other times, a vehicle transporting a load extending beyond the vehicle's sides or more than four feet beyond the rear shall display red flags that are at least ______ inches square.

**Answer:** 12

**Explanation:** The 12-inch-square red flag requirement for projecting loads mirrors the flag size required elsewhere for visible warning devices under 547.502.

---

### Q416 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.382(b)

Under TC 547.382(b), red flags marking a projecting load's extremities must be placed:

- [x] Where a lamp is required by Section 547.382
- [ ] Only on the extreme left side of the load
- [ ] At least 100 feet from the vehicle
- [ ] Only on loads exceeding 30 feet in length

**Explanation:** The flags substitute for lamps at times when lamps aren't required, so they go wherever a lamp would otherwise be required under the section.

---

### Q417 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.383(a)

Under TC 547.383(a), a vehicle other than a moped or a motorcycle with an engine piston displacement of ______ cubic centimeters or less shall be equipped with at least one lamp that emits a white or amber light visible at 1,000 feet from the front and a red light visible at 1,000 feet from the rear.

**Answer:** 250

**Explanation:** The 250 cc exemption threshold for mopeds/small motorcycles reappears in 547.383, matching the same threshold used for other lighting exceptions in Chapter 547.

---

### Q418 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.383(a)

Under TC 547.383(a), the parked-vehicle lamp (or combination of lamps) must emit which colors, and be visible at what distance?

- [x] White or amber to the front and red to the rear, each visible at 1,000 feet
- [ ] Amber to the front and amber to the rear, each visible at 500 feet
- [ ] White to the front and white to the rear, each visible at 1,000 feet
- [ ] Red to the front and red to the rear, each visible at 500 feet

**Explanation:** The front color option is white or amber; the rear must be red — both at the standard 1,000-foot Chapter 547 visibility distance.

---

### Q419 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.383(d)

Under TC 547.383(d), a lighted headlamp on a parked vehicle shall be ______.

**Answer:** dimmed

**Explanation:** This dimming requirement prevents a parked vehicle's headlamps from blinding approaching traffic.

---

### Q420 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.383(d)

Under TC 547.383(d), what must be done with a lighted headlamp on a vehicle that is parked?

- [x] It must be dimmed
- [ ] It must be turned off entirely
- [ ] It must be covered
- [ ] It must flash intermittently

**Explanation:** 547.383(d) requires dimming, not shutting the headlamp off completely.

---

### Q421 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.401(b)

Under TC 547.401(b), a trailer, semitrailer, or pole trailer is not required to have brakes if its gross weight is ______ pounds or less, or if its gross weight is heavier than that but not heavier than ______ pounds and it is drawn at a speed of not more than 30 miles per hour.

**Answer:** 4,500 | 15,000

**Explanation:** These same two weight thresholds (4,500 and 15,000 lbs) reappear throughout Subchapter I for brake-related exceptions (e.g., 547.402(c), 547.405(d)).

---

### Q422 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.401(b)

Under TC 547.401(b), a trailer with a gross weight heavier than 4,500 pounds but not heavier than 15,000 pounds is exempt from brake requirements only if it is drawn at a speed of not more than:

- [x] 30 miles per hour
- [ ] 20 miles per hour
- [ ] 45 miles per hour
- [ ] 55 miles per hour

**Explanation:** 30 mph is the speed ceiling for this weight-based brake exemption.

---

### Q423 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.402(b)

Under TC 547.402(b), a truck or truck-tractor that has at least ______ axles is not required to have brakes on the front wheels, but must have brakes on the wheels of at least one steerable axle.

**Answer:** three

**Explanation:** 547.402(b)'s three-axle threshold triggers this front-wheel-brake exception, provided the vehicle has at least two steerable axles.

---

### Q424 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.402(b)

Under TC 547.402(b), a truck or truck-tractor with at least three axles that is not required to have front-wheel brakes must still have brakes that operate on:

- [x] The wheels of one steerable axle, if equipped with at least two steerable axles
- [ ] All wheels regardless of axle configuration
- [ ] Only the rearmost axle
- [ ] No wheels are required if the vehicle has three axles

**Explanation:** The exception isn't a blanket brake waiver — it still requires brakes on a steerable axle's wheels.

---

### Q425 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.402(c)

Under TC 547.402(c), a trailer or semitrailer that has a gross weight of ______ pounds or less may use surge or inertia brake systems to satisfy the brake-operation requirements of this subchapter.

**Answer:** 15,000

**Explanation:** The 15,000-pound ceiling for surge/inertia brakes matches the upper weight threshold used in the 547.401(b) brake exemption.

---

### Q426 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.402(c)

Under TC 547.402(c), which type of brake system is permitted for a trailer or semitrailer weighing 15,000 pounds or less?

- [x] Surge or inertia brake systems
- [ ] Air brakes only
- [ ] Vacuum brakes only
- [ ] Hydraulic brakes only

**Explanation:** Surge or inertia brakes are a permitted alternative specifically for lighter trailers/semitrailers.

---

### Q427 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.403(b)

Under TC 547.403(b), a vehicle required to have brakes shall be equipped so that ______ control device operates the service brakes.

**Answer:** one

**Explanation:** The single-control-device rule doesn't prohibit an additional device for operating a towed vehicle's brakes — it just requires one device to control the towing vehicle's own service brakes.

---

### Q428 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.403(b)

Under TC 547.403(b), how many control devices must operate a vehicle's service brakes?

- [x] One
- [ ] Two
- [ ] Three
- [ ] As many as the manufacturer specifies

**Explanation:** One control device is the rule, though an additional device may be added to operate a towed vehicle's brakes.

---

### Q429 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.404(a)

Under TC 547.404(a), required parking brakes must be adequate to hold the vehicle on any grade on which it is operated, under all loading conditions, and on a surface free from snow, ice, or ______ material.

**Answer:** loose

**Explanation:** The three parking-brake performance conditions are grade, loading, and a surface free of snow/ice/loose material.

---

### Q430 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.404(a)

Under TC 547.404(a), a vehicle's parking brakes must be adequate to hold the vehicle under all of the following conditions EXCEPT:

- [x] On a wet surface with standing water
- [ ] On any grade on which the vehicle is operated
- [ ] Under all loading conditions
- [ ] On a surface free from snow, ice, or loose material

**Explanation:** The statute specifies snow/ice/loose material as the surface condition — standing water on a wet surface isn't one of the listed conditions.

---

### Q431 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.405(d)

Under TC 547.405(d), a trailer, semitrailer, or pole trailer equipped with air or vacuum brakes, or with a gross weight heavier than 4,500 pounds, must have brakes that are promptly applied automatically and remain applied for at least ______ minutes in case of a breakaway from the towing vehicle.

**Answer:** 15

**Explanation:** The 15-minute automatic-brake-holding requirement applies once a breakaway occurs.

---

### Q432 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.405(d)

Under TC 547.405(d), after a breakaway from the towing vehicle, the towed vehicle's automatically-applied brakes must remain applied for at least:

- [x] 15 minutes
- [ ] 5 minutes
- [ ] 30 minutes
- [ ] 1 hour

**Explanation:** 15 minutes is the specific statutory holding period for breakaway brakes.

---

### Q433 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.405(e)

Under TC 547.405(e), a motor vehicle used to tow a trailer, semitrailer, or pole trailer equipped with brakes shall be equipped with service brakes arranged so that, in case of a ______ of the towed vehicle, the towing vehicle is capable of stopping by use of its service brakes.

**Answer:** breakaway

**Explanation:** This ensures the towing vehicle alone can still stop safely if the towed vehicle separates.

---

### Q434 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.405(e)

Under TC 547.405(e), if a towed trailer breaks away, the towing vehicle's own service brakes must be capable of:

- [x] Stopping the towing vehicle by themselves
- [ ] Automatically releasing to prevent jackknifing
- [ ] Applying only after a 15-minute delay
- [ ] Operating only if air pressure remains above 50 percent

**Explanation:** The towing vehicle's independent stopping capability is the safety backstop required by 547.405(e).

---

### Q435 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.501(a)

Under TC 547.501(a), a motor vehicle shall be equipped with a horn in good working condition that emits a sound audible under normal conditions at a distance of at least ______ feet.

**Answer:** 200

**Explanation:** 200 feet is the horn's minimum audible distance — shorter than the 500-foot audible distance required for an authorized emergency vehicle's siren under 547.702(a).

---

### Q436 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.501(a)

Under TC 547.501(a), a vehicle's horn must be audible under normal conditions at a minimum distance of:

- [x] 200 feet
- [ ] 500 feet
- [ ] 100 feet
- [ ] 1,000 feet

**Explanation:** 200 feet is specific to the ordinary horn requirement, distinct from the 500-foot siren distance for authorized emergency vehicles.

---

### Q437 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.501(b)

Under TC 547.501(b), a vehicle may not be equipped with a siren, whistle, or bell unless it is a commercial vehicle equipped with a theft alarm signal device arranged so the device cannot be used as an ordinary warning signal, or it is an authorized emergency vehicle equipped with a siren, whistle, or bell that complies with Section ______.

**Answer:** 547.702

**Explanation:** 547.702 sets the equipment standards for an authorized emergency vehicle's siren/whistle/bell.

---

### Q438 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.501(b)

Under TC 547.501(b), which of the following vehicles may lawfully be equipped with a siren, whistle, or bell?

- [x] A commercial vehicle with a theft alarm device that cannot be used as an ordinary warning signal
- [ ] Any vehicle registered as a passenger car
- [ ] Any vehicle operated by a security patrol company
- [ ] Any tow truck responding to a scene

**Explanation:** Only the two categories named in 547.501(b) — the restricted commercial theft-alarm vehicle and the authorized emergency vehicle — may use these devices.

---

### Q439 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.502(a)

Under TC 547.502(a), a person operating certain vehicles outside an urban district or on a divided highway must carry, at daytime, at least ______ red flags at least 12 inches square, and at nighttime, at least ______ flares and at least three red-burning fusees (or equivalent lanterns/reflectors).

**Answer:** two | three

**Explanation:** The daytime flag count (two) and nighttime device count (three) are the two baseline quantities required under 547.502(a).

---

### Q440 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.502(a)

Under TC 547.502(a), which vehicles must carry the visible warning devices described in the section?

- [x] A truck, bus, or truck-tractor, or a motor vehicle towing a house trailer, operating outside an urban district or on a divided highway
- [ ] Any passenger car operating anywhere in the state
- [ ] Only explosive cargo vehicles
- [ ] Only vehicles operating at night

**Explanation:** 547.502(a) targets trucks, buses, truck-tractors, and house-trailer tow vehicles specifically outside urban districts or on divided highways.

---

### Q441 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.502(c)

Under TC 547.502(c), a flare, electric lantern, or portable reflector must be visible and distinguishable at a distance of at least ______ feet at night under normal atmospheric conditions.

**Answer:** 600

**Explanation:** 600 feet is the warning-device visibility distance, shorter than the 1,000-foot general "duty to display" threshold but matching the 100-600 foot reflector range used elsewhere.

---

### Q442 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.502(d)

Under TC 547.502(d), a portable reflector unit must reflect a red light clearly visible at all distances from 100 feet to how many feet under normal atmospheric conditions at night?

- [x] 600 feet
- [ ] 500 feet
- [ ] 350 feet
- [ ] 1,000 feet

**Explanation:** The 100-600 foot range for a portable reflector unit matches the standard reflector-visibility range used throughout Chapter 547.

---

### Q443 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.503(b)

Under TC 547.503(b), the hazard-lamp display requirement applies to a truck, bus, truck-tractor, trailer, semitrailer, or pole trailer that is at least ______ inches wide or at least ______ feet long.

**Answer:** 80 | 30

**Explanation:** These are the same width/length thresholds (80 inches, 30 feet) used to trigger additional lighting equipment requirements for large vehicles under Subchapter F.

---

### Q444 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.503(a)

Under TC 547.503(a), the operator of a covered vehicle stopped on a roadway or shoulder must immediately display vehicular hazard warning lamps UNLESS the vehicle is:

- [x] Parked lawfully in an urban district
- [ ] Stopped for more than 10 minutes outside an urban district
- [ ] Disabled on a divided highway
- [ ] Within 500 feet of a curve or hillcrest

**Explanation:** 547.503(a) lists specific exceptions (lawful urban parking, loading/unloading, avoiding traffic conflict, complying with a police/traffic-control direction, or already displaying other required devices) — being stopped outside an urban district for over 10 minutes isn't an exception; it's what triggers 547.504's additional device-placement rules instead.

---

### Q445 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.504(b)

Under TC 547.504(b), the operator of a covered vehicle that is disabled, or stopped for more than ______ minutes, on a roadway outside an urban district shall place specified warning devices near the vehicle and down the roadway.

**Answer:** 10

**Explanation:** The 10-minute threshold distinguishes a brief stop from a "disabled or stopped" condition requiring full warning-device placement.

---

### Q446 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.504(b)

Under TC 547.504(b), once a covered vehicle has been disabled or stopped for more than 10 minutes outside an urban district, warning devices must be placed toward approaching traffic at approximately what distance from the vehicle?

- [x] 100 feet
- [ ] 50 feet
- [ ] 200 feet
- [ ] 300 feet

**Explanation:** 100 feet is the placement distance on an ordinary two-way roadway; a divided highway instead uses both 100 and 200 feet under 547.504(c).

---

### Q447 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.505(c)

Under TC 547.505, when lighted lamps are not required, if traffic moves in one direction, one red flag shall be placed approximately ______ feet and one approximately ______ feet to the rear of the vehicle.

**Answer:** 100 | 200

**Explanation:** For one-directional traffic, both flags go to the rear (100 and 200 feet); for two-directional traffic, one flag instead goes 100 feet ahead and the other 100 feet to the rear.

---

### Q448 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.505(b)

Under TC 547.505(b), if traffic on the roadway moves in two directions, where should the two required red flags be placed?

- [x] One approximately 100 feet to the rear and one approximately 100 feet ahead of the vehicle
- [ ] Both approximately 100 feet to the rear of the vehicle
- [ ] One approximately 200 feet ahead and one at the vehicle itself
- [ ] Both approximately 500 feet from the vehicle

**Explanation:** Two-way traffic requires flags on both sides of the vehicle (ahead and behind), unlike one-way traffic where both flags go to the rear at different distances.

---

### Q449 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.601

Under TC 547.601, a motor vehicle shall be equipped with front safety belts if safety belt ______ were part of the manufacturer's original equipment on the vehicle.

**Answer:** anchorages

**Explanation:** The requirement is tied to whether anchorages were originally installed, not simply the vehicle's model year.

---

### Q450 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.601

Under TC 547.601, a motor vehicle must be equipped with front safety belts if:

- [x] Safety belt anchorages were part of the manufacturer's original equipment
- [ ] The vehicle was manufactured after 1968
- [ ] The vehicle has a gross weight over 4,500 pounds
- [ ] The vehicle is registered as a commercial motor vehicle

**Explanation:** The anchorage condition is what triggers the requirement, regardless of model year or weight.

---

### Q451 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.602

Under TC 547.602, a motor vehicle shall be equipped with a mirror located to reflect to the operator a view of the highway for a distance of at least ______ feet from the rear of the vehicle.

**Answer:** 200

**Explanation:** This 200-foot mirror-view distance also appears as an exception condition for rear windows under the window-tint restrictions of 547.613(b)(3).

---

### Q452 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.602

Under TC 547.602, a required mirror must reflect to the operator a view of the highway for a distance of at least:

- [x] 200 feet from the rear
- [ ] 500 feet from the rear
- [ ] 100 feet from the rear
- [ ] 1,000 feet from the rear

**Explanation:** 200 feet is the mirror-view distance, matching the horn's audible distance in 547.501(a) but for a different purpose.

---

### Q453 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.603

Under TC 547.603, a motor vehicle shall be equipped with a device that is operated or controlled by the vehicle operator and that cleans ______ from the windshield.

**Answer:** moisture

**Explanation:** The device (windshield wiper) must also be maintained in good working condition, per the statute's second sentence.

---

### Q454 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.603

Under TC 547.603, the required windshield-cleaning device must be:

- [x] Operated or controlled by the vehicle operator and maintained in good working condition
- [ ] Automatic and rain-sensing only
- [ ] Operable only at speeds under 45 mph
- [ ] Equipped with a heating element

**Explanation:** The statute only requires operator control and good working condition — no automatic or rain-sensing feature is mandated.

---

### Q455 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.604(b)

Under TC 547.604(b), a person may not use a muffler cutout, bypass, or similar device on a ______ vehicle.

**Answer:** motor

**Explanation:** 547.604(a) requires a muffler in good working condition to prevent excessive noise; (b) then bans defeat devices like cutouts and bypasses.

---

### Q456 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.604

Under TC 547.604, which of the following is prohibited on a motor vehicle?

- [x] A muffler cutout, bypass, or similar device
