- [ ] A factory-installed catalytic converter
- [ ] A dual exhaust system
- [ ] An aftermarket muffler that meets OEM specifications

**Explanation:** Only devices that defeat or bypass the muffler are prohibited — properly functioning aftermarket or dual-exhaust systems are not banned by this section.

---

### Q457 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.605(d)

Under TC 547.605(d), a person commits an offense if the person operates a vehicle that emits visible smoke for ______ seconds or longer (except when travel conditions require downshifting or lower gears).

**Answer:** 10

**Explanation:** The 10-second visible-smoke threshold applies both to continuous smoke and to smoke that remains suspended in the air for 10 seconds or longer before dissipating.

---

### Q458 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.605(b)

Under TC 547.605(b), a motor vehicle or engine of a model year after which year must be equipped to prevent the discharge of crankcase emissions into the ambient atmosphere?

- [x] 1967
- [ ] 1972
- [ ] 1960
- [ ] 1995

**Explanation:** 1967 is the crankcase-emissions cutoff year, distinct from the 1972 reflector/safety-glazing cutoffs and 1960 taillamp/stoplamp cutoffs used elsewhere in Chapter 547.

---

### Q459 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.606(a)

Under TC 547.606(a), required safety guards or flaps must be located and suspended behind the rearmost wheels of the vehicle within ______ inches of the surface of the highway.

**Answer:** eight

**Explanation:** The 8-inch clearance ensures the guard/flap effectively blocks road spray and debris.

---

### Q460 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.606(b)

Under TC 547.606, which vehicle configuration is exempt from the safety guard or flap requirement?

- [x] A truck-tractor operated alone, or a pole trailer
- [ ] A road tractor with four tires on the rearmost axle
- [ ] A truck-tractor in combination with a semitrailer
- [ ] A truck with super single tires on the rearmost axle

**Explanation:** 547.606(b) exempts a solo truck-tractor and pole trailers — combinations and vehicles with the specified tire configurations are covered by the requirement.

---

### Q461 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.609

Under TC 547.609, a sunscreening device's label must contain information required by the department on light transmission and ______ of the device.

**Answer:** luminous reflectance

**Explanation:** These are the same two measured properties defined in 547.001(3) and (4).

---

### Q462 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.609

Under TC 547.609, a sunscreening device's required label must be:

- [x] Legible and permanently installed between the material and the surface to which it is applied
- [ ] Removable at the consumer's discretion
- [ ] Printed only in red lettering
- [ ] Optional for windshields but mandatory for side windows

**Explanation:** The label must be legible and permanently installed — no color requirement or windshield/side-window distinction is imposed on the label itself.

---

### Q463 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.611(a)

Under TC 547.611(a), a motor vehicle may be equipped with video receiving equipment only if the video display is not visible from the operator's seat unless the vehicle's transmission is in park or the vehicle's ______ is applied.

**Answer:** parking brake

**Explanation:** Either condition (in park, or parking brake applied) satisfies the exception.

---

### Q464 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.611(c)

Under TC 547.611(c), which use of video/television equipment is NOT prohibited by this section?

- [x] Equipment used exclusively for a safety or law enforcement purpose, approved by the department
- [ ] A dashboard-mounted television visible to the operator while driving
- [ ] A rear video display visible from the driver's seat while the vehicle is in drive
- [ ] Any personal entertainment system regardless of location

**Explanation:** 547.611(c) also exempts commercial digital-information equipment, remote TV transmission trucks, utility-monitoring equipment, and navigation-system displays — but not a display visible to the operator while driving.

---

### Q465 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.613(b)(1)

Under TC 547.613(b)(1), a windshield sunscreening device is excepted from the window-tint offense only if, in combination with the windshield, it has a light transmission of ______ percent or more and a luminous reflectance of ______ percent or less.

**Answer:** 25 | 25

**Explanation:** The windshield exception requires both a light-transmission floor and a luminous-reflectance ceiling, each set at 25 percent, plus color and AS-1-line restrictions.

---

### Q466 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.613(b)(1)

Under TC 547.613(b)(1), which colors are NOT permitted for an excepted windshield sunscreening device?

- [x] Red, blue, or amber
- [ ] Clear or smoke gray only
- [ ] Any color is permitted if light transmission is 25% or more
- [ ] Green or bronze

**Explanation:** 547.613(b)(1)(C) specifically bars red, blue, or amber tinting on an otherwise-excepted windshield device.

---

### Q467 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.615(b)

Under TC 547.615(b), a manufacturer of a new motor vehicle sold or leased in this state that is equipped with a recording device shall disclose that fact in the vehicle's ______.

**Answer:** owner's manual

**Explanation:** Disclosure in the owner's manual is the manufacturer's basic obligation under 547.615(b).

---

### Q468 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.615(c)

Under TC 547.615(c), information recorded or transmitted by a vehicle's recording device may be retrieved by someone other than the owner:

- [x] On court order, with owner consent, or for specified safety/research/emergency-response purposes
- [ ] Only by law enforcement without any court order
- [ ] Only by the vehicle's manufacturer
- [ ] Never, under any circumstance

**Explanation:** 547.615(c) lists four narrow retrieval exceptions — the information isn't absolutely locked to the owner, but access is still tightly limited.

---

### Q469 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.616(d)

Under TC 547.616(d), a person who violates the radar interference device prohibitions commits an offense classified as a Class ______ misdemeanor.

**Answer:** C

**Explanation:** This is one of the lower-level Chapter 547 offenses — most Chapter 547 offenses are unclassified misdemeanors, but this one is expressly a Class C.

---

### Q470 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.616(a)

Under TC 547.616(a), a "radar interference device" does NOT include which of the following?

- [x] A ham radio, band radio, or similar electronic device
- [ ] A radar jamming device
- [ ] A scrambler
- [ ] A diffuser

**Explanation:** The statute expressly excludes ham/band radios from the definition, even though they're also electronic transmission equipment.

---

### Q471 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.617(a)

Under TC 547.617(a), a motorcycle or moped designed to carry more than ______ person must be equipped with footrests and handholds for use by the passenger.

**Answer:** one

**Explanation:** The requirement is triggered by the vehicle's passenger-carrying design, not by whether a passenger is actually riding at a given time.

---

### Q472 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.617(b)

Under TC 547.617(b), the footrest/handhold requirement does NOT apply to:

- [x] An autocycle or a motorcycle as defined by Section 521.001(a)(6-a)
- [ ] A moped designed to carry two people
- [ ] A motorcycle manufactured after 1975
- [ ] Any motorcycle with a sidecar

**Explanation:** 547.617(b) carves out autocycles and the specific motorcycle definition in 521.001(a)(6-a) from this requirement.

---

### Q473 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.618(b)

Under TC 547.618(b), an automated motor vehicle designed to be operated exclusively by the automated driving system for all trips is not subject to motor vehicle equipment laws that relate to or support operation by a ______ driver and are not relevant for an automated driving system.

**Answer:** human

**Explanation:** The exemption is narrowly tied to human-driver-support equipment (e.g., steering-related requirements) that wouldn't make sense for a fully automated vehicle.

---

### Q474 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.618(c)

Under TC 547.618(c), if a vehicle safety inspection is required for a fully automated motor vehicle exempted under Subsection (b), how does the inspection treat equipment the vehicle is exempt from?

- [x] The vehicle is automatically considered to pass inspection with respect to that equipment
- [ ] The vehicle must still pass inspection on all equipment regardless of the exemption
- [ ] The vehicle is exempt from inspection entirely
- [ ] The vehicle must obtain a special waiver from TxDOT before each inspection

**Explanation:** 547.618(c) builds the equipment exemption directly into the inspection outcome — no separate waiver process is required.

---

### Q475 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.701(a)(2)

Under TC 547.701(a)(2), a school bus's signal lamps must display ______ alternately flashing red lights, two located on the front and two located on the rear, each at the same level, visible at a distance of 500 feet in normal sunlight.

**Answer:** four

**Explanation:** The four-light configuration (two front, two rear) matches the identical requirement for authorized emergency vehicles under 547.702(c).

---

### Q476 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.701(a)(1)

Under TC 547.701(a), in addition to the required signal lamps, a school bus must be equipped with which device to help the operator see the area immediately in front of the vehicle?

- [x] A convex mirror or other device that reflects a clear view of the area immediately in front
- [ ] A backup camera system
- [ ] A second rearview mirror mounted on the dash
- [ ] An ultrasonic proximity sensor

**Explanation:** 547.701(a)(1) requires a convex mirror (or equivalent device), not a camera or sensor system.

---

### Q477 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.7012

Under TC 547.7012, a multifunction school activity bus may not be painted National School Bus Glossy ______.

**Answer:** Yellow

**Explanation:** This single-sentence statute exists to visually distinguish multifunction school activity buses (which don't stop traffic like a standard school bus) from actual school buses.

---

### Q478 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.7012

Under TC 547.7012, what color is a multifunction school activity bus specifically prohibited from being painted?

- [x] National School Bus Glossy Yellow
- [ ] Fire-engine red
- [ ] Federal Safety Orange
- [ ] Ambulance white

**Explanation:** The color restriction prevents other drivers from mistaking the vehicle for an actual school bus subject to stop-arm/flashing-light traffic laws.

---

### Q479 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.702(a), (c)

Under TC 547.702(a) and (c), an authorized emergency vehicle's siren must emit a sound audible at a distance of at least ______ feet, and its signal lamps must display four alternately flashing red lights visible at a distance of at least ______ feet in normal sunlight.

**Answer:** 500 | 500

**Explanation:** Both the siren's audible range and the signal lamps' visible range share the same 500-foot standard under 547.702.

---

### Q480 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.702(e)

Under TC 547.702(e), a private vehicle operated by a volunteer firefighter responding to a fire alarm or medical emergency may be equipped with:

- [x] A signal lamp temporarily attached to the vehicle roof that flashes a red light visible at 500 feet
- [ ] Permanently mounted red and blue alternating lights identical to a police vehicle
- [ ] A siren audible at 200 feet only
- [ ] No warning equipment of any kind

**Explanation:** 547.702(e) allows a temporarily-attached roof lamp for volunteer firefighters — a narrower option than the permanently mounted equipment authorized emergency vehicles generally may use.

---

### Q481 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.703(a)

Under TC 547.703(a), a slow-moving-vehicle emblem must have a reflective surface clearly visible in daylight or at night from the light of standard automobile headlamps at a distance of at least ______ feet, and be mounted base down on the rear of the vehicle.

**Answer:** 500

**Explanation:** 500 feet is the emblem's visibility distance — the same distance used for the school bus and authorized emergency vehicle signal lamps in this subchapter.

---

### Q482 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.703(d)

Under TC 547.703(d), which vehicle, when operated at a speed of not more than 25 miles per hour on a highway, is required to display a slow-moving-vehicle emblem?

- [x] A golf cart, neighborhood electric vehicle, or off-highway vehicle
- [ ] A bicycle or electric bicycle
- [ ] Road machinery or a road roller
- [ ] A farm tractor manufactured before 1972

**Explanation:** 547.703(d) specifically extends the emblem requirement to these three vehicle types when operated at 25 mph or less on a highway.

---

### Q483 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.801(a)

Under TC 547.801(a), a motorcycle or moped's headlamps must be mounted at a height from 24 to ______ inches, and its taillamp must be mounted at a height from ______ to 72 inches.

**Answer:** 54 | 20

**Explanation:** The headlamp height range (24-54 inches) matches the general vehicle rule in 547.321(c); the taillamp range (20-72 inches) has a higher floor than the general vehicle taillamp range (15-72 inches) in 547.322(c).

---

### Q484 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.801(a)(1)

Under TC 547.801(a)(1), a motorcycle or moped may be equipped with not more than how many headlamps?

- [x] Two
- [ ] One
- [ ] Three
- [ ] Four

**Explanation:** 547.801(a)(1) caps motorcycle/moped headlamps at two, mounted at 24-54 inches.

---

### Q485 — Lighting — Model-Year & Effective-Date Rules
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.801(d)

Under TC 547.801(d), a motorcycle may not be operated at any time unless at least one headlamp is illuminated, except this does not apply to a motorcycle manufactured before the model year ______.

**Answer:** 1975

**Explanation:** 1975 is the specific exemption cutoff for this always-on-headlamp rule — distinct from the 1948, 1960, 1967, and 1972 cutoffs used elsewhere in Chapter 547.

---

### Q486 — Lighting — Model-Year & Effective-Date Rules
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.801(d)

Under TC 547.801(d), which requirement applies to a motorcycle operated at any time, regardless of ambient light conditions?

- [x] At least one headlamp must be illuminated (unless manufactured before model year 1975)
- [ ] The taillamp must flash continuously
- [ ] Both headlamps must be illuminated simultaneously
- [ ] The horn must sound every time the motorcycle starts

**Explanation:** This "headlamp-on" rule is unique to motorcycles among Chapter 547's general lighting provisions — most vehicles only need lights at nighttime or low visibility per 547.302(a).

---

### Q487 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 548.001(1)(A)

Under TC 548.001(1)(A), a vehicle or combination of vehicles qualifies as a "commercial motor vehicle" if it has a gross weight, registered weight, or gross weight rating of more than ______ pounds.

**Answer:** 26,000

**Explanation:** This 26,000-pound threshold is one of three independent ways a vehicle can qualify as a "commercial motor vehicle" under 548.001(1).

---

### Q488 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 548.001(1)

Under TC 548.001(1), the definition of "commercial motor vehicle" generally excludes which type of vehicle regardless of weight or passenger capacity?

- [x] A farm vehicle (with a gross/registered/rated weight under 48,000 pounds)
- [ ] A school activity bus
- [ ] A hazardous materials carrier
- [ ] A passenger bus

**Explanation:** The definition's opening clause excludes farm vehicles under 48,000 lbs from "commercial motor vehicle" status entirely, while school activity buses are expressly included when they meet the weight or passenger criteria.

---

### Q489 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 548.001(1)(B)

Under TC 548.001(1)(B), a vehicle also qualifies as a "commercial motor vehicle" if it is designed or used to transport more than ______ passengers, including the driver.

**Answer:** 15

**Explanation:** This passenger-count threshold is independent of the 26,000-pound weight threshold — a vehicle can qualify under either prong.

---

### Q490 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 548.001(1)(C)

Under TC 548.001(1)(C), a vehicle also qualifies as a "commercial motor vehicle" if it is used to transport:

- [x] Hazardous materials in a quantity requiring placarding under the Hazardous Materials Transportation Act
- [ ] More than 10 passengers regardless of weight
- [ ] Any cargo across state lines
- [ ] Livestock exceeding 5,000 pounds

**Explanation:** The third independent prong of the CMV definition is hazmat-placarding quantity, regardless of the vehicle's weight or passenger capacity.

---

### Q491 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 548.001(1)(A)

Under TC 548.001(1)(A), a vehicle qualifies as a "commercial motor vehicle" on weight grounds if its gross weight, registered weight, or gross weight rating exceeds:

- [ ] 10,000 pounds
- [x] 26,000 pounds
- [ ] 48,000 pounds
- [ ] 80,000 pounds

**Explanation:** 26,000 pounds is the weight-based threshold under 548.001(1)(A) — one of three independent ways a vehicle can qualify as a "commercial motor vehicle."

---

### Q492 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 548.001(1)

Under TC 548.001(1), a farm vehicle is excluded from the "commercial motor vehicle" definition entirely, regardless of weight or passenger capacity, as long as its gross weight, registered weight, or gross weight rating is under ______ pounds.

**Answer:** 48,000

**Explanation:** The farm-vehicle exclusion has its own 48,000-pound ceiling — higher than the 26,000-pound general CMV weight threshold — below which a farm vehicle never counts as a "commercial motor vehicle" under this chapter.

---

### Q493 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 548.001(1)(B)

Under TC 548.001(1)(B), a vehicle qualifies as a "commercial motor vehicle" if it is designed or used to transport, including the driver:

- [ ] More than 10 passengers
- [ ] More than 12 passengers
- [x] More than 15 passengers
- [ ] More than 26 passengers

**Explanation:** The passenger-count prong (more than 15, including the driver) is independent of the 26,000-pound weight threshold — a vehicle can qualify as a CMV under either prong alone.

---

### Q494 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 548.001(1)(C)

Under TC 548.001(1)(C), a vehicle also qualifies as a "commercial motor vehicle" if it is used to transport hazardous materials in a quantity requiring ______ under the Hazardous Materials Transportation Act.

**Answer:** placarding

**Explanation:** The hazmat-placarding prong is the third independent way a vehicle can qualify as a "commercial motor vehicle," regardless of its weight or passenger capacity.

---

### Q495 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.001

Under TC 551.001, unless specifically provided otherwise, a provision of Chapter 551 that applies to a person operating a bicycle applies only on a highway or on a path set aside for the exclusive operation of ______.

**Answer:** bicycles

**Explanation:** This limits Chapter 551's default bicycle provisions to highways and bicycle-exclusive paths, not sidewalks generally.

---

### Q496 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.001

Under TC 551.001, Chapter 551's bicycle provisions apply, by default, to a person operating a bicycle on which locations?

- [x] A highway, or a path set aside for the exclusive operation of bicycles
- [ ] Any sidewalk in the state
- [ ] Any private parking lot
- [ ] Any location, without limitation

**Explanation:** The default scope is limited to highways and bicycle-exclusive paths unless a specific provision states otherwise.

---

### Q497 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.002

Under TC 551.002, a provision of this subtitle applicable to a bicycle also applies to a moped and to an ______ bicycle, except for a provision that by its nature cannot apply.

**Answer:** electric

**Explanation:** This extension clause is what makes bicycle-specific rules (like 551.101-551.104) apply equally to mopeds and electric bicycles.

---

### Q498 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.002

Under TC 551.002, bicycle-applicable provisions of this subtitle extend to which two other vehicle types (absent a provision that by its nature can't apply)?

- [x] A moped and an electric bicycle
- [ ] A golf cart and a neighborhood electric vehicle
- [ ] A motor-assisted scooter and a pocket bike
- [ ] An off-highway vehicle and a farm tractor

**Explanation:** 551.002 names exactly two extensions — moped and electric bicycle — distinct from the other low-powered-vehicle categories covered in later subchapters.

---

### Q499 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.101(b)

Under TC 551.101(b), a parent of a child or a guardian of a ward may not knowingly permit the child or ward to ______ this subtitle.

**Answer:** violate

**Explanation:** This creates a distinct parental/guardian liability for knowingly permitting a minor's bicycle-law violations.

---

### Q500 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.101(a)

Under TC 551.101(a), a person operating a bicycle generally has the same rights and duties as:

- [x] A driver operating a vehicle under this subtitle
- [ ] A pedestrian on a sidewalk
- [ ] An operator of a motor-assisted scooter
- [ ] An operator of a neighborhood electric vehicle

**Explanation:** The baseline rule treats a bicyclist as a vehicle driver, except where a Chapter 551 provision alters that duty or the duty cannot by its nature apply to a bicyclist.

---

### Q501 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.102(a)

Under TC 551.102(a), a person operating a bicycle shall ride only on or astride a permanent and regular ______ attached to the bicycle.

**Answer:** seat

**Explanation:** 551.102 also bars carrying more riders than the bicycle is designed for, and carrying an object that prevents the operator from keeping at least one hand on the handlebars.

---

### Q502 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.102(c)

Under TC 551.102(c), a person operating a bicycle may not carry an object that prevents the operator from keeping how many hands on the handlebars?

- [x] At least one hand
- [ ] Both hands
- [ ] At least two fingers
- [ ] No hands (voice-controlled steering permitted)

**Explanation:** The rule requires at least one hand remain on the handlebars at all times.

---

### Q503 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.103(a)

Under TC 551.103(a), a person operating a bicycle on a roadway who is moving slower than other traffic shall ride as near as practicable to the right curb or edge of the roadway, unless the person is preparing to turn left, is passing another vehicle, a roadway condition prevents safe riding there, or the person is operating in an outside lane that is less than ______ feet in width without an adjacent designated bicycle lane.

**Answer:** 14

**Explanation:** The 14-foot outside-lane-width exception lets cyclists take the full lane when it's too narrow to safely share side-by-side with a motor vehicle.

---

### Q504 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.103(c)

Under TC 551.103(c), how many bicyclists may ride abreast on a roadway that is not set aside for the exclusive operation of bicycles?

- [x] Two
- [ ] One
- [ ] Three
- [ ] Any number, without limit

**Explanation:** Riding more than two abreast is limited to parts of a roadway set aside exclusively for bicycles; on ordinary roadways, riders are capped at two abreast and must stay in a single lane if the roadway is laned.

---

### Q505 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.104(a)

Under TC 551.104(a), a person may not operate a bicycle unless it is equipped with a brake capable of making a braked wheel ______ on dry, level, clean pavement.

**Answer:** skid

**Explanation:** The "skid on dry, level, clean pavement" standard is the statutory test for adequate bicycle braking.

---

### Q506 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.104(a)

Under TC 551.104(a), a bicycle's required brake must be capable of:

- [x] Making a braked wheel skid on dry, level, clean pavement
- [ ] Stopping the bicycle within 15 feet at any speed
- [ ] Operating independently on the front and rear wheel
- [ ] Meeting federal motor vehicle brake standards

**Explanation:** The statute's test is skid-capability on a specific pavement condition, not a stopping-distance or federal-standard requirement.

---

### Q507 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.104(b)

Under TC 551.104(b), a person may not operate a bicycle at nighttime unless it is equipped with a front lamp emitting a white light visible from at least ______ feet, and, on the rear, either a red reflector visible from 50 to 300 feet or a lamp emitting a red light visible from ______ feet to the rear.

**Answer:** 500 | 500

**Explanation:** Both the front lamp and the rear lamp option share the same 500-foot visibility distance; the alternative rear reflector option instead uses a 50-300 foot range.

---

### Q508 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.104(b)(2)

Under TC 551.104(b)(2), a bicycle's rear-facing nighttime equipment may be either a red reflector visible from 50 to 300 feet, or:

- [x] A lamp that emits a red light visible from a distance of 500 feet to the rear
- [ ] A lamp that emits a white light visible from 500 feet to the rear
- [ ] A reflector visible from 100 to 600 feet to the rear
- [ ] No equipment, if riding only on well-lit streets

**Explanation:** The red-light-lamp alternative (500 feet) is the second lawful option alongside the red reflector (50-300 feet).

---

### Q509 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.107(b), (c)

Under TC 551.107(b)-(c), a person may not operate an electric bicycle unless its motor disengages or ceases to function when the operator stops ______ or when the brakes are applied, and a person may not operate a Class 3 electric bicycle unless the person is at least ______ years of age.

**Answer:** pedaling | 15

**Explanation:** The motor cutoff rule applies to all electric bicycles; the age-15 minimum is specific to Class 3 electric bicycles (the higher-speed category), though anyone may still ride as a passenger on one.

---

### Q510 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.107(c)

Under TC 551.107(c), a person under 15 years of age may still lawfully:

- [x] Ride as a passenger on a Class 3 electric bicycle
- [ ] Operate a Class 3 electric bicycle on a sidewalk only
- [ ] Operate a Class 3 electric bicycle if a parent consents in writing
- [ ] Operate a Class 3 electric bicycle at reduced speed

**Explanation:** The age-15 minimum only restricts operating a Class 3 electric bicycle — riding as a passenger is expressly still allowed.

---

### Q511 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.201

Under TC 551.201, an "electric personal assistive mobility device" is defined as a two ______ wheeled device designed for transporting one person that is ______.

**Answer:** non-tandem | self-balancing

**Explanation:** EPAMDs (Segway-type devices) are defined by two non-tandem wheels and a self-balancing design, propelled by an electric propulsion system.

---

### Q512 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.201

Under TC 551.201, which characteristic is part of the statutory definition of an "electric personal assistive mobility device"?

- [x] It is self-balancing
- [ ] It has three or more wheels
- [ ] It is designed to carry two people
- [ ] It is propelled solely by human power

**Explanation:** The device must be self-balancing with two non-tandem wheels, propelled by an electric propulsion system — not human power.

---

### Q513 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.201

Under TC 551.201, an electric personal assistive mobility device must be propelled by an electric propulsion system with an average power of ______ watts or one horsepower.

**Answer:** 750

**Explanation:** The statute sets the power ceiling at 750 watts (equivalent to one horsepower).

---

### Q514 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.201

Under TC 551.201, what is the maximum average power rating for the electric propulsion system of an electric personal assistive mobility device?

- [x] 750 watts or one horsepower
- [ ] 500 watts or half horsepower
- [ ] 1,000 watts or 1.5 horsepower
- [ ] 1,500 watts or two horsepower

**Explanation:** 750 watts (one horsepower) is the statutory ceiling.

---

### Q515 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.202(a)

Under TC 551.202(a), a person may operate an electric personal assistive mobility device on a residential street, roadway, or public highway with a speed limit of ______ miles per hour or less, and then only while crossing at a crosswalk, where no sidewalk is available, or when directed by a traffic control device or officer.

**Answer:** 30

**Explanation:** The roadway-speed ceiling for EPAMD operation is 30 mph.

---

### Q516 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.202(a)

Under TC 551.202(a), an electric personal assistive mobility device may be operated on a residential street, roadway, or public highway only if the posted speed limit is:

- [x] 30 miles per hour or less
- [ ] 25 miles per hour or less
- [ ] 35 miles per hour or less
- [ ] 45 miles per hour or less

**Explanation:** 30 mph is the statutory ceiling, and even then only under the specific conditions in 551.202(a)(1)-(3).

---

### Q517 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.301

Under TC 551.301, a "neighborhood electric vehicle" is defined as a vehicle that can attain a maximum speed of ______ miles per hour on a paved level surface and otherwise complies with Federal Motor Vehicle Safety Standard ______.

**Answer:** 35 | 500

**Explanation:** NEVs are capped at a 35 mph maximum attainable speed and must comply with FMVSS 500 (49 C.F.R. Sec. 571.500).

---

### Q518 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.301

Under TC 551.301, a "neighborhood electric vehicle" is one that can attain a maximum speed, on a paved level surface, of:

- [x] 35 miles per hour
- [ ] 25 miles per hour
- [ ] 30 miles per hour
- [ ] 45 miles per hour

**Explanation:** 35 mph is the maximum-attainable-speed threshold in the statutory definition.

---

### Q519 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.303(a)

Under TC 551.303(a), a neighborhood electric vehicle may be operated only on a street or highway with a posted speed limit of ______ miles per hour or less, and may not be operated at a speed exceeding the lesser of the posted speed limit or ______ miles per hour.

**Answer:** 45 | 35

**Explanation:** The road itself must be posted 45 mph or less, but the NEV's own operating speed is capped at the lesser of the posted limit or 35 mph.

---

### Q520 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.303(a)

Under TC 551.303(a), a neighborhood electric vehicle may be operated only on a street or highway for which the posted speed limit is:

- [x] 45 miles per hour or less
- [ ] 35 miles per hour or less
- [ ] 40 miles per hour or less
- [ ] 55 miles per hour or less

**Explanation:** 45 mph is the ceiling on the posted speed limit of the road itself (separate from the NEV's own 35 mph operating cap).

---

### Q521 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.304(a)(3)

Under TC 551.304(a)(3), an operator may operate a neighborhood electric vehicle on a public highway posted at not more than 35 mph if operated during the daytime and not more than ______ miles from the location where the vehicle is usually parked, for transportation to or from a golf course.

**Answer:** 2

**Explanation:** The NEV limited-operation distance for golf-course transportation is two miles — half the five-mile distance allowed for golf carts under 551.403(a)(3).

---

### Q522 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.304(a)(3)

Under TC 551.304(a)(3), a neighborhood electric vehicle operated for transportation to or from a golf course during the daytime may travel how far from where it is usually parked?

- [x] Not more than 2 miles
- [ ] Not more than 5 miles
- [ ] Not more than 10 miles
- [ ] Not more than 1 mile

**Explanation:** Two miles for NEVs — note this differs from the five-mile distance allowed for golf carts under a parallel provision.

---

### Q523 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.351(1)

Under TC 551.351(1), a "motor-assisted scooter" must have a gas or electric motor not exceeding ______ cubic centimeters and must have the ability to be propelled by ______ alone.

**Answer:** 40 | human power

**Explanation:** The 40cc motor ceiling and the human-power-alone capability both distinguish a motor-assisted scooter from a moped or motorcycle.

---

### Q524 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.351(1)

Under TC 551.351(1), a "motor-assisted scooter" may not have a gas or electric motor exceeding:

- [x] 40 cubic centimeters
- [ ] 50 cubic centimeters
- [ ] 125 cubic centimeters
- [ ] 250 cubic centimeters

**Explanation:** 40cc is the ceiling for a motor-assisted scooter; note the pocket bike/minimotorbike definition uses a different ceiling (less than 50cc).

---

### Q525 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.351(2)

Under TC 551.351(2), a "pocket bike or minimotorbike" has an electric motor or internal combustion engine with a piston displacement of less than ______ cubic centimeters and is ineligible for a certificate of title under Transportation Code Chapter ______.

**Answer:** 50 | 501

**Explanation:** Pocket bikes/minimotorbikes are capped at under 50cc displacement and cannot be titled under Chapter 501.

---

### Q526 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.351(2)

Under TC 551.351(2), a "pocket bike or minimotorbike" is defined as having a piston displacement of:

- [x] Less than 50 cubic centimeters
- [ ] Less than 40 cubic centimeters
- [ ] Less than 70 cubic centimeters
- [ ] Less than 125 cubic centimeters

**Explanation:** Less than 50cc is the statutory ceiling, distinct from the motor-assisted scooter's 40cc ceiling.

---

### Q527 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.352(a)

Under TC 551.352(a), a motor-assisted scooter may be operated only on a street or highway for which the posted speed limit is ______ miles per hour or less.

**Answer:** 35

**Explanation:** 35 mph is the operating-street speed ceiling for motor-assisted scooters.

---

### Q528 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.352(a)

Under TC 551.352(a), a motor-assisted scooter may be operated only on a street or highway posted at:

- [x] 35 miles per hour or less
- [ ] 25 miles per hour or less
- [ ] 30 miles per hour or less
- [ ] 40 miles per hour or less

**Explanation:** 35 mph is the ceiling; the scooter may still cross a road posted higher at an intersection.

---

### Q529 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.401

Under TC 551.401, a "golf cart" means a motor vehicle designed by the manufacturer primarily for use on a ______.

**Answer:** golf course

**Explanation:** The definition turns on the manufacturer's primary design purpose, not on how the vehicle is actually used.

---

### Q530 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.401

Under TC 551.401, a "golf cart" is defined as a motor vehicle:

- [x] Designed by the manufacturer primarily for use on a golf course
- [ ] Designed by the manufacturer primarily for off-highway recreational use
- [ ] Designed by the manufacturer primarily for farming or lawn care
- [ ] Registered by the state exclusively for use in master planned communities

**Explanation:** The statutory definition is about manufacturer design intent for golf-course use.

---

### Q531 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.402(a)-(b)

Under TC 551.402, the Texas Department of Motor Vehicles may not ______ a golf cart for operation on a highway regardless of any alteration, and a person may operate a golf cart on a highway in a manner authorized by this subchapter only if the vehicle displays a ______ issued under this section.

**Answer:** register | license plate

**Explanation:** Golf carts cannot be registered like ordinary vehicles; instead, a special golf cart license plate authorizes limited highway operation.

---

### Q532 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.402(a)

Under TC 551.402(a), the Texas Department of Motor Vehicles:

- [x] May not register a golf cart for highway operation, regardless of any alteration made to it
- [ ] Must register a golf cart for highway operation if it is equipped with headlamps and taillamps
- [ ] May register a golf cart for highway operation only in master planned communities
- [ ] May register a golf cart for highway operation if the owner pays an annual fee

**Explanation:** No alteration to a golf cart makes it eligible for standard highway registration.

---

### Q533 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.403(a)(3)

Under TC 551.403(a)(3), an operator may operate a golf cart on a highway posted at not more than 35 mph if operated during the daytime and not more than ______ miles from the location where the golf cart is usually parked, for transportation to or from a golf course.

**Answer:** 5

**Explanation:** Golf carts get a five-mile radius for golf-course transportation — more than double the two-mile radius allowed for NEVs and off-highway vehicles under parallel provisions.

---

### Q534 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.403(a)(3)

Under TC 551.403(a)(3), a golf cart operated for transportation to or from a golf course during the daytime may travel how far from where it is usually parked?

- [x] Not more than 5 miles
- [ ] Not more than 2 miles
- [ ] Not more than 3 miles
- [ ] Not more than 10 miles

**Explanation:** Five miles for golf carts — contrast with the two-mile figure used for NEVs (551.304) and off-highway vehicles (551A.055).

---

### Q535 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.403(b) (HB 1281)

Under TC 551.403(b), a golf cart operated in a qualifying master planned community may be operated on a highway posted at not more than 35 mph — including through an intersection with a highway posted at more than 35 mph — without displaying a golf cart ______.

**Answer:** license plate

**Explanation:** HB 1281 created this carve-out so master-planned-community golf carts need not display a golf cart license plate under 551.403(b).

---

### Q536 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.403(b) (HB 1281)

Under TC 551.403(b) (added by HB 1281), a golf cart operated in a qualifying master planned community on a highway posted at 35 mph or less:

- [x] Is not required to display a golf cart license plate
- [ ] Must display two golf cart license plates
- [ ] Must be registered as a neighborhood electric vehicle
- [ ] May only be operated by a licensed golf course employee

**Explanation:** This HB 1281 exception removes the license-plate-display requirement within a qualifying master planned community.

---

### Q537 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.404(b)-(c)

Under TC 551.404, the commissioners court of a county bordering the Gulf of Mexico may authorize golf cart operation on an unincorporated highway posted at not more than ______ miles per hour, if that county has a population of less than ______.

**Answer:** 35 | 500,000

**Explanation:** The county-authorization option under 551.404(c) applies to counties bordering/containing the Red River, or bordering the Gulf of Mexico with a population under 500,000, on highways posted 35 mph or less.

---

### Q538 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.404(c)

Under TC 551.404(c), a county bordering the Gulf of Mexico qualifies to authorize golf cart operation on its unincorporated highways only if the county's population is:

- [x] Less than 500,000
- [ ] Less than 100,000
- [ ] Less than 1,000,000
- [ ] Less than 250,000

**Explanation:** 500,000 is the population ceiling for Gulf-bordering counties under this provision (no population limit applies to counties bordering/containing the Red River).

---

### Q539 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 664.001(1)

Under TC 664.001(1), a "Class 1 electric bicycle" is equipped with a motor that assists the rider only when ______, with a top assisted speed of ______ miles per hour or less.

**Answer:** pedaling | 20

**Explanation:** Class 1 e-bikes are pedal-assist only, capped at a 20 mph top assisted speed.

---

### Q540 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 664.001(1)

Under TC 664.001(1), a "Class 1 electric bicycle" is equipped with a motor that:

- [x] Assists the rider only when the rider is pedaling
- [ ] May propel the bicycle without the rider pedaling
- [ ] Assists the rider only above 20 miles per hour
- [ ] Has no top-speed limitation

**Explanation:** The defining feature of Class 1 is pedal-assist-only operation, distinguishing it from Class 2.

---

### Q541 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 664.001(2)

Under TC 664.001(2), a "Class 2 electric bicycle" is equipped with a motor that may be used to propel the bicycle ______ the rider pedaling, with a top assisted speed of ______ miles per hour or less.

**Answer:** without | 20

**Explanation:** Class 2 e-bikes can be throttle-propelled without pedaling, still capped at 20 mph top assisted speed like Class 1.

---

### Q542 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 664.001(2)

Under TC 664.001(2), what distinguishes a "Class 2 electric bicycle" from a Class 1 electric bicycle?

- [x] Its motor may propel the bicycle without the rider pedaling
- [ ] Its top assisted speed is higher (up to 28 mph)
- [ ] It is not required to have operable pedals
- [ ] It has a motor of 750 watts or more

**Explanation:** Both Class 1 and Class 2 top out at 20 mph assisted speed; the difference is that Class 2 does not require pedaling to be propelled.

---

### Q543 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 664.001(3)

Under TC 664.001(3), a "Class 3 electric bicycle" is equipped with a motor that assists the rider only when pedaling, with a top assisted speed of more than ______ but less than ______ miles per hour.

**Answer:** 20 | 28

**Explanation:** Class 3 is pedal-assist only, like Class 1, but with a higher top assisted speed band of 20-28 mph.

---

### Q544 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 664.001(3)

Under TC 664.001(3), a "Class 3 electric bicycle" has a top assisted speed of:

- [x] More than 20 but less than 28 miles per hour
- [ ] 20 miles per hour or less
- [ ] 28 miles per hour or more
- [ ] Exactly 25 miles per hour

**Explanation:** Class 3's speed band (20-28 mph) sits above Class 1 and Class 2's 20 mph ceiling.

---

### Q545 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 664.001(4)

Under TC 664.001(4), an "electric bicycle" (the umbrella term) is equipped with fully operable pedals and an electric motor of fewer than ______ watts, with a top assisted speed of ______ miles per hour or less.

**Answer:** 750 | 28

**Explanation:** The general electric bicycle definition caps motor wattage under 750 watts and top assisted speed at 28 mph or less, encompassing Classes 1-3.

---

### Q546 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 664.001(4)

Under TC 664.001(4), the general definition of "electric bicycle" caps the electric motor at:

- [x] Fewer than 750 watts
- [ ] Fewer than 500 watts
- [ ] Fewer than 1,000 watts
- [ ] Fewer than 1 horsepower exactly

**Explanation:** Under 750 watts is the wattage ceiling for the general electric bicycle definition.

---

### Q547 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551A.001(1)

Under TC 551A.001(1), an "all-terrain vehicle" is designed to propel itself with ______ or more tires in contact with the ground and is not more than ______ inches wide.

**Answer:** three | 50

**Explanation:** ATVs are defined by three-plus tires and a 50-inch width ceiling, distinguishing them from four-plus-tire recreational off-highway vehicles and utility vehicles.

---

### Q548 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551A.001(1)

Under TC 551A.001(1), an "all-terrain vehicle" must not be more than how many inches wide?

- [x] 50 inches
- [ ] 40 inches
- [ ] 48 inches
- [ ] 60 inches

**Explanation:** 50 inches is the statutory width ceiling for an ATV.

---

### Q549 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551A.001(5)

Under TC 551A.001(5), a "recreational off-highway vehicle" is designed to propel itself with ______ or more tires in contact with the ground and is designed by the manufacturer for off-highway use by the ______ only.

**Answer:** four | operator

**Explanation:** ROVs have four-plus tires (vs. an ATV's three-plus) and are designed for operator-only off-highway use, though they may be built to carry a passenger.

---

### Q550 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551A.001(5)

Under TC 551A.001(5), a "recreational off-highway vehicle" is designed to propel itself with how many tires in contact with the ground?

- [x] Four or more
- [ ] Three or more
- [ ] Two or more
- [ ] Six or more

**Explanation:** Four-plus tires distinguish an ROV from a three-plus-tire ATV.

---

### Q551 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551A.001(6)

Under TC 551A.001(6), a "utility vehicle" is equipped with ______ seating for the operator and a passenger and is designed by the manufacturer primarily for ______ work, not recreational purposes.

**Answer:** side-by-side | utility

**Explanation:** The utility vehicle definition turns on side-by-side seating and a manufacturer purpose of utility work rather than recreation, distinguishing it from a golf cart or an ROV.

---

### Q552 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551A.001(6)

Under TC 551A.001(6), a "utility vehicle" is designed by the manufacturer primarily for:

- [x] Utility work, not recreational purposes
- [ ] Recreational purposes only
- [ ] Farming or lawn care exclusively
- [ ] Golf course use only

**Explanation:** Utility work — not recreation — is the defining manufacturer purpose for a utility vehicle.

---

### Q553 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551A.031(b)

Under TC 551A.031(b), a person may not operate an off-highway vehicle on public off-highway vehicle land or a beach unless the person holds a ______ issued under Chapter 551A or under the authority of another state, is taking a supervised safety training course, or is under the direct supervision of an adult who holds one.

**Answer:** safety certificate

**Explanation:** A safety certificate (or qualifying supervision) is required to operate an OHV on public OHV land or a beach.

---

### Q554 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551A.031(b)

Under TC 551A.031(b), which of the following satisfies the requirement to operate an off-highway vehicle on public off-highway vehicle land or a beach?

- [x] Holding a safety certificate issued under Chapter 551A or another state's authority
- [ ] Holding a standard Class C driver's license
- [ ] Being at least 18 years of age
- [ ] Carrying proof of vehicle registration

**Explanation:** A safety certificate, supervised training, or supervision by a certificate-holding adult satisfies this requirement.

---

### Q555 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551A.032

Under TC 551A.032, a person younger than ______ years of age operating an off-highway vehicle on public off-highway vehicle land must be accompanied by and under the direct supervision of a parent, guardian, or an adult authorized by the parent or guardian.

**Answer:** 14

**Explanation:** 14 is the age threshold requiring direct parental/guardian (or authorized adult) supervision on public OHV land.

---

### Q556 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551A.032

Under TC 551A.032, direct supervision by a parent, guardian, or authorized adult is required for an off-highway vehicle operator on public off-highway vehicle land who is younger than:

- [x] 14 years of age
- [ ] 12 years of age
- [ ] 16 years of age
- [ ] 18 years of age

**Explanation:** Under 14 requires direct supervision on public OHV land under 551A.032.

---

### Q557 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551A.033(b)

Under TC 551A.033(b), a person operating an off-highway vehicle on a beach must hold and have in the person's possession a ______.

**Answer:** driver's license

**Explanation:** Unlike public OHV land, beach operation requires the operator to hold and carry a driver's license.

---

### Q558 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551A.033(b)

Under TC 551A.033(b), a person operating an off-highway vehicle on a beach must hold and have in the person's possession:

- [x] A driver's license
- [ ] A safety certificate only
- [ ] Proof of vehicle insurance
- [ ] A boating safety certificate

**Explanation:** A driver's license, specifically, is required for beach operation of an OHV.

---

### Q559 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551A.052(c)-(d)

Under TC 551A.052, the Texas Department of Motor Vehicles may charge a fee not to exceed $______ for an off-highway vehicle license plate, and the license plate does ______ expire.

**Answer:** 10 | not

**Explanation:** Like the golf cart license plate under 551.402, the OHV license plate has a $10 fee cap and never expires.

---

### Q560 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551A.052(c)

Under TC 551A.052(c), the Texas Department of Motor Vehicles may charge a fee for an off-highway vehicle license plate not to exceed:

- [x] $10
- [ ] $5
- [ ] $25
- [ ] $50

**Explanation:** $10 is the fee cap, matching the golf cart license plate fee cap under 551.402(c)(2).

---

### Q561 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551A.055(2)

Under TC 551A.055(2), an operator may operate an unregistered off-highway vehicle on a highway posted at not more than 35 mph if operated during the daytime and not more than ______ miles from the location where the vehicle is usually parked, for transportation to or from a golf course.

**Answer:** 2

**Explanation:** The OHV golf-course-transportation distance is two miles, matching the NEV distance under 551.304 but less than the golf cart's five miles under 551.403.

---

### Q562 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551A.055(2)

Under TC 551A.055(2), an off-highway vehicle operated for transportation to or from a golf course during the daytime may travel how far from where it is usually parked?

- [x] Not more than 2 miles
- [ ] Not more than 5 miles
- [ ] Not more than 10 miles
- [ ] Not more than 3 miles

**Explanation:** Two miles for off-highway vehicles — the same figure used for neighborhood electric vehicles, but less than the golf cart's five-mile allowance.

---

### Q563 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551A.057

Under TC 551A.057, an unregistered off-highway vehicle used for agricultural or utility work on a highway must display a triangular orange flag at least ______ feet above ground level, and the operation may not exceed a distance of ______ miles from point of origin to destination.

**Answer:** 6 | 25

**Explanation:** The agricultural/utility exception requires a six-foot flag and caps the trip distance at 25 miles.

---

### Q564 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551A.057(a)(5)

Under TC 551A.057, an off-highway vehicle operated for agricultural or utility purposes on a highway may not exceed a distance from point of origin to destination of:

- [x] 25 miles
- [ ] 10 miles
- [ ] 15 miles
- [ ] 50 miles

**Explanation:** 25 miles is the agricultural/utility distance cap — contrast with the shorter 10-mile cap for law enforcement operation under 551A.058.

---

### Q565 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551A.058

Under TC 551A.058, a peace officer or other emergency services provider operating an unregistered off-highway vehicle on a highway in connection with official duties must display the required orange flag, and the operation may not exceed a distance of ______ miles from point of origin to destination.

**Answer:** 10

**Explanation:** Law enforcement/emergency operation is capped at 10 miles — less than the 25-mile cap for agricultural/utility operation.

---

### Q566 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551A.058(a)(4)

Under TC 551A.058, a peace officer operating an unregistered off-highway vehicle on a highway while performing official duties may not exceed a distance from point of origin to destination of:

- [x] 10 miles
- [ ] 25 miles
- [ ] 5 miles
- [ ] 15 miles

**Explanation:** 10 miles for law enforcement operation — less than the agricultural/utility operation's 25-mile allowance.

---

### Q567 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551A.072(a)

Under TC 551A.072(a), a person operating, riding, or being carried on an off-highway vehicle on public off-highway vehicle land, a beach, or a highway must wear a safety helmet that complies with United States ______ standards, as well as ______.

**Answer:** Department of Transportation | eye protection

**Explanation:** The required safety apparel includes a DOT-compliant helmet and eye protection (plus seat belts, if the vehicle is so equipped).

---

### Q568 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551A.072(a)

Under TC 551A.072(a), in addition to a safety helmet, what other safety apparel must a person operating, riding, or being carried on an off-highway vehicle wear?

- [x] Eye protection
- [ ] A reflective vest
- [ ] Steel-toed boots
- [ ] Long sleeves

**Explanation:** Eye protection is explicitly required alongside a DOT-compliant helmet (and seat belts, if equipped).

---

### Q569 — Pedestrians
**LO:** 22.19 · **Source:** TC 552.001(b)-(c)

Under TC 552.001, a pedestrian facing a green signal may proceed across a roadway within a crosswalk unless the sole green signal is a ______ arrow, and a pedestrian facing a steady red signal alone or a steady yellow signal may not ______ a roadway.

**Answer:** turn | enter

**Explanation:** A green turn arrow alone does not authorize pedestrian crossing, and steady red or yellow signals prohibit pedestrians from entering the roadway.

---

### Q570 — Pedestrians
**LO:** 22.19 · **Source:** TC 552.001(b)

Under TC 552.001(b), a pedestrian facing a green signal may NOT proceed across the roadway if:

- [x] The sole green signal displayed is a turn arrow
- [ ] The signal has been green for less than 10 seconds
- [ ] There is no marked crosswalk
- [ ] The pedestrian is carrying a package

**Explanation:** A sole green turn arrow does not authorize a pedestrian to cross.

---

### Q571 — Pedestrians
**LO:** 22.19 · **Source:** TC 552.002(b)

Under TC 552.002(b), a pedestrian facing a "Walk" signal may proceed across a roadway in the direction of the signal, and the operator of a vehicle shall ______ and yield the right-of-way to the pedestrian.

**Answer:** stop

**Explanation:** A "Walk" signal obligates vehicle operators to stop and yield to the pedestrian.

---

### Q572 — Pedestrians
**LO:** 22.19 · **Source:** TC 552.002(b)

Under TC 552.002(b), when a pedestrian is proceeding across a roadway in the direction of a "Walk" signal, the operator of a vehicle must:

- [x] Stop and yield the right-of-way to the pedestrian
- [ ] Sound the horn and proceed with caution
- [ ] Yield only if the pedestrian is a child
- [ ] Proceed if no pedestrian is within 10 feet

**Explanation:** The vehicle operator's duty is to stop and yield to the "Walk"-signal pedestrian.

---

### Q573 — Pedestrians
**LO:** 22.19 · **Source:** TC 552.003(a)

Under TC 552.003(a), if no traffic control signal is in place, the operator of a vehicle shall stop and yield the right-of-way to a pedestrian crossing in a crosswalk who is on the half of the roadway in which the vehicle is traveling, or is approaching so closely from the opposite half as to be in ______.

**Answer:** danger

**Explanation:** The "in danger" standard triggers the stop-and-yield duty even for a pedestrian on the opposite half of the roadway.

---

### Q574 — Pedestrians
**LO:** 22.19 · **Source:** TC 552.003(a)

Under TC 552.003(a), at an uncontrolled crosswalk (no traffic control signal), a vehicle operator must stop and yield to a pedestrian who is:

- [x] On the half of the roadway the vehicle is traveling in, or approaching closely enough from the opposite half to be in danger
- [ ] Only on the exact half of the roadway the vehicle is traveling in
- [ ] Only if the pedestrian has already reached the vehicle's lane
- [ ] Only during daylight hours

**Explanation:** The duty extends to a pedestrian approaching closely from the opposite half, not just the vehicle's own half.

---

### Q575 — Pedestrians
**LO:** 22.19 · **Source:** TC 552.004

Under TC 552.004, a pedestrian shall proceed on the ______ half of a crosswalk if possible.

**Answer:** right

**Explanation:** Pedestrians should keep to the right half of a crosswalk, mirroring vehicle traffic's keep-right convention.

---

### Q576 — Pedestrians
**LO:** 22.19 · **Source:** TC 552.004

Under TC 552.004, a pedestrian shall proceed on which half of a crosswalk, if possible?

- [x] The right half
- [ ] The left half
- [ ] Whichever half is less crowded
- [ ] The center of the crosswalk

**Explanation:** The right half — the pedestrian equivalent of vehicles keeping to the right.

---

### Q577 — Pedestrians
**LO:** 22.19 · **Source:** TC 552.006(a)-(b)

Under TC 552.006, a pedestrian may not walk along and on a roadway if an adjacent sidewalk is provided and accessible; if no sidewalk is provided, the pedestrian shall walk on the ______ side of the roadway or shoulder, facing ______ traffic.

**Answer:** left | oncoming

**Explanation:** Absent a sidewalk, a pedestrian must walk facing oncoming traffic on the left side or shoulder — the opposite convention from vehicle traffic.

---

### Q578 — Pedestrians
**LO:** 22.19 · **Source:** TC 552.006(b)

Under TC 552.006(b), if no sidewalk is provided, a pedestrian walking along a highway shall walk:

- [x] On the left side of the roadway or shoulder, facing oncoming traffic
- [ ] On the right side of the roadway or shoulder, with the flow of traffic
- [ ] In the center of the roadway
- [ ] Only on paved shoulders, never on the roadway itself

**Explanation:** Facing oncoming traffic on the left side/shoulder is the rule when no sidewalk exists.

---

### Q579 — Pedestrians
**LO:** 22.19 · **Source:** TC 552.007(a)

Under TC 552.007(a), a person may not stand in a roadway to solicit a ride, contribution, employment, or business from an occupant of a vehicle, except that a person may solicit a ______ contribution if authorized by the local authority having jurisdiction over the roadway.

**Answer:** charitable

**Explanation:** The only roadway-solicitation exception is for authorized charitable contributions.

---

### Q580 — Pedestrians
**LO:** 22.19 · **Source:** TC 552.007(a)

Under TC 552.007(a), a person may stand in a roadway to solicit which of the following, if authorized by the local authority having jurisdiction?

- [x] A charitable contribution
- [ ] Employment
- [ ] A ride
- [ ] Business for a private company

**Explanation:** Only charitable-contribution solicitation has a statutory authorization carve-out; solicitation of rides, employment, or business from vehicle occupants is otherwise prohibited.

---

### Q581 — Pedestrians
**LO:** 22.19 · **Source:** TC 552.010(a)-(b)

Under TC 552.010, a person may not carry a white cane on a public street or highway unless the person is totally or partially ______, and the driver of a vehicle approaching a pedestrian guided by an assistance animal or carrying a white cane shall bring the vehicle to a full ______ if injury or danger can be avoided only by that action.

**Answer:** blind | stop

**Explanation:** White-cane use is legally restricted to blind pedestrians, and drivers must fully stop when that is the only way to avoid injuring them.

---

### Q582 — Pedestrians
**LO:** 22.19 · **Source:** TC 552.010(b)

Under TC 552.010(b), when approaching a pedestrian guided by an assistance animal or carrying a white cane, a vehicle driver must:

- [x] Bring the vehicle to a full stop if injury or danger can be avoided only by that action
- [ ] Sound the horn twice and proceed
- [ ] Yield only if the pedestrian is in the crosswalk
- [ ] Reduce speed by half

**Explanation:** A full stop is required specifically when it's the only way to avoid injuring or endangering the pedestrian.

---

### Q583 — Pedestrians
**LO:** 22.19 · **Source:** TC 552A.0101

Under TC 552A.0101, a "motorized mobility device" is not capable of speeds exceeding ______ miles per hour, and a person operating a nonmotorized wheelchair or a motorized mobility device is considered to be a ______ for purposes of this subtitle.

**Answer:** 8 | pedestrian

**Explanation:** The 8 mph speed ceiling defines a motorized mobility device, and its operator (like a wheelchair user) is legally treated as a pedestrian.

---

### Q584 — Pedestrians
**LO:** 22.19 · **Source:** TC 552A.0101

Under TC 552A.0101, a person operating a nonmotorized wheelchair or a motorized mobility device is considered, for purposes of this subtitle, to be a:

- [x] Pedestrian
- [ ] Bicycle operator
- [ ] Motor vehicle operator
- [ ] Moped operator

**Explanation:** Wheelchair and motorized-mobility-device users are legally classified as pedestrians.

---

### Q585 — Arrest, Charging & Promise to Appear
**LO:** 22.20 · **Source:** TC 543.001

Under TC 543.001, any peace officer may arrest ______ a warrant a person found committing a violation of this subtitle.

**Answer:** without

**Explanation:** TC 543.001 authorizes warrantless arrest for a Transportation Code violation committed in the officer's presence.

---

### Q586 — Arrest, Charging & Promise to Appear
**LO:** 22.20 · **Source:** TC 543.001

Under TC 543.001, a peace officer may arrest a person found committing a violation of the Rules of the Road subtitle:

- [x] Without a warrant
- [ ] Only with a warrant
- [ ] Only with supervisor approval
- [ ] Only if the violation is a felony

**Explanation:** Warrantless arrest is authorized for a subtitle violation found being committed.

---

### Q587 — Arrest, Charging & Promise to Appear
**LO:** 22.20 · **Source:** TC 543.002(a)

Under TC 543.002(a), a person arrested for a misdemeanor violation of this subtitle must be immediately taken before a magistrate if the person is arrested on a charge of failure to stop after a collision causing damage to ______, or if the person demands an immediate appearance or refuses to make a written ______ to appear.

**Answer:** property | promise

**Explanation:** These are the two triggers requiring immediate presentation to a magistrate rather than release on a written promise to appear.

---

### Q588 — Arrest, Charging & Promise to Appear
**LO:** 22.20 · **Source:** TC 543.002(a)

Under TC 543.002(a), a person arrested for a misdemeanor Transportation Code violation must be immediately taken before a magistrate if the person is arrested on a charge of:

- [x] Failure to stop in the event of a collision causing property damage
- [ ] Speeding more than 20 mph over the limit
- [ ] Driving with an expired registration
- [ ] Failing to signal a lane change

**Explanation:** Failure to stop after a property-damage collision is one of the two statutory triggers for mandatory presentation to a magistrate.

---

### Q589 — Arrest, Charging & Promise to Appear
**LO:** 22.20 · **Source:** TC 543.003

Under TC 543.003, an officer who arrests a person for a misdemeanor violation and does not take the person before a magistrate shall issue a written notice to appear showing the time and place to appear, the offense charged, the name and address of the person charged, and, if applicable, the ______ of the person's vehicle.

**Answer:** license number

**Explanation:** The vehicle's license number is included "if applicable" alongside the mandatory time, place, offense, and identity information.

---

### Q590 — Arrest, Charging & Promise to Appear
**LO:** 22.20 · **Source:** TC 543.003

Under TC 543.003, which piece of information is included on a written notice to appear only "if applicable"?

- [x] The license number of the person's vehicle
- [ ] The time the person is to appear
- [ ] The offense charged
- [ ] The name of the person charged

**Explanation:** Time, place, offense, and name/address are always required; the vehicle's license number is included only if applicable.

---

### Q591 — Arrest, Charging & Promise to Appear
**LO:** 22.20 · **Source:** TC 543.004(a)

Under TC 543.004(a), an officer shall issue a written notice to appear (given a written promise to appear) for the offenses of speeding, use of a wireless communication device under Section 545.4251, and violation of the open container law under Penal Code Section ______.

**Answer:** 49.031

**Explanation:** These three offenses (speeding, wireless communication device use, open container) are the only ones for which issuing a notice to appear is mandatory.

---

### Q592 — Arrest, Charging & Promise to Appear
**LO:** 22.20 · **Source:** TC 543.004(a)

Under TC 543.004, which of the following is one of the three offenses for which an officer must issue a written notice to appear (assuming the person promises to appear)?

- [x] Speeding
- [ ] Reckless driving
- [ ] Driving while intoxicated
- [ ] Failure to maintain financial responsibility

**Explanation:** Speeding, wireless communication device use, and open container are the only offenses for which issuance of a notice to appear is mandatory.

---

### Q593 — Arrest, Charging & Promise to Appear
**LO:** 22.20 · **Source:** TC 543.005

Under TC 543.005, to secure release, a person arrested must make a written ______ to appear in court, and the arresting officer shall retain the original of the notice and deliver a ______ of the notice to the person arrested.

**Answer:** promise | copy

**Explanation:** The arresting officer keeps the original and gives the arrestee a copy after the written promise to appear is signed.

---

### Q594 — Arrest, Charging & Promise to Appear
**LO:** 22.20 · **Source:** TC 543.005

Under TC 543.005, after a person signs a written promise to appear, the arresting officer:

- [x] Retains the original notice and delivers a copy to the person arrested
- [ ] Retains a copy and delivers the original to the person arrested
- [ ] Mails the original to the court and gives the person nothing
- [ ] Destroys the notice once the person is released

**Explanation:** The officer keeps the original and gives the arrestee the copy, then promptly releases the person.

---

### Q595 — Arrest, Charging & Promise to Appear
**LO:** 22.20 · **Source:** TC 543.006(a)

Under TC 543.006(a), the time specified in a notice to appear must be at least ______ days after the date of arrest, unless the person arrested demands an earlier hearing.

**Answer:** 10

**Explanation:** 10 days is the minimum lead time for the appearance date, absent a demand for an earlier hearing.

---

### Q596 — Arrest, Charging & Promise to Appear
**LO:** 22.20 · **Source:** TC 543.006(a)

Under TC 543.006(a), unless the person arrested demands an earlier hearing, the time specified in a notice to appear must be at least how many days after the date of arrest?

- [x] 10 days
- [ ] 5 days
- [ ] 7 days
- [ ] 14 days

**Explanation:** 10 days is the statutory minimum.

---

### Q597 — Arrest, Charging & Promise to Appear
**LO:** 22.20 · **Source:** TC 543.007

Under TC 543.007, a notice to appear issued to the operator of a commercial motor vehicle or holder of a commercial driver's license must contain the information required by department rule to comply with Transportation Code Chapter 522 and the federal Commercial Motor Vehicle Safety Act of ______.

**Answer:** 1986

**Explanation:** The federal Commercial Motor Vehicle Safety Act of 1986 sets additional content requirements for CMV/CDL notices to appear.

---

### Q598 — Arrest, Charging & Promise to Appear
**LO:** 22.20 · **Source:** TC 543.007

Under TC 543.007, a notice to appear issued to a commercial motor vehicle operator or CDL holder must comply with Chapter 522 and which federal act?

- [x] The Commercial Motor Vehicle Safety Act of 1986
- [ ] The Motor Carrier Safety Improvement Act of 1999
- [ ] The Federal Highway Act of 1956
- [ ] The Commercial Driver's License Act of 1990

**Explanation:** The Commercial Motor Vehicle Safety Act of 1986 is the specific federal act referenced in 543.007.

---

### Q599 — Arrest, Charging & Promise to Appear
**LO:** 22.20 · **Source:** TC 543.008

Under TC 543.008, a violation by an officer of Sections 543.003-543.007 is ______ in office, and the officer is subject to removal from the officer's position.

**Answer:** misconduct

**Explanation:** Officer noncompliance with the notice-to-appear provisions is treated as misconduct in office.

---

### Q600 — Arrest, Charging & Promise to Appear
**LO:** 22.20 · **Source:** TC 543.008

Under TC 543.008, an officer's violation of Sections 543.003-543.007 constitutes:

- [x] Misconduct in office, subject to removal from position
- [ ] A Class C misdemeanor only
- [ ] Grounds for a written reprimand only
- [ ] No specified consequence

**Explanation:** The statute treats this as misconduct in office with removal as the consequence.

---

### Q601 — Arrest, Charging & Promise to Appear
**LO:** 22.20 · **Source:** TC 543.009

Under TC 543.009, a person may comply with a written promise to appear by an appearance by ______, and a person who wilfully violates a written promise to appear commits a misdemeanor regardless of the ______ of the charge on which the person was arrested.

**Answer:** counsel | disposition

**Explanation:** Compliance can be satisfied by appearance through counsel, and willfully breaking the promise is its own separate misdemeanor no matter how the original charge turned out.

---

### Q602 — Arrest, Charging & Promise to Appear
**LO:** 22.20 · **Source:** TC 543.009(b)

Under TC 543.009(b), a person who wilfully violates a written promise to appear in court commits a misdemeanor:

- [x] Regardless of the disposition of the charge on which the person was arrested
- [ ] Only if the original charge resulted in a conviction
- [ ] Only if the original charge was dismissed
- [ ] Only if the person was arrested for a felony

**Explanation:** The promise-to-appear violation is its own offense, independent of what happened with the underlying charge.

---

### Q603 — Arrest, Charging & Promise to Appear
**LO:** 22.20 · **Source:** TC 543.010

Under TC 543.010, a complaint and notice to appear on a speeding charge must specify the maximum or minimum speed limit applicable at the location, and the ______ at which the defendant is alleged to have ______.

**Answer:** speed | driven

**Explanation:** Both the applicable limit and the specific alleged speed must be stated in a speeding complaint.

---

### Q604 — Arrest, Charging & Promise to Appear
**LO:** 22.20 · **Source:** TC 543.010

Under TC 543.010, a complaint or notice to appear on a speeding charge must specify:

- [x] The applicable speed limit and the speed the defendant is alleged to have driven
- [ ] Only the applicable speed limit
- [ ] Only the speed the defendant is alleged to have driven
- [ ] The make and model of the defendant's vehicle

**Explanation:** Both pieces of information — the limit and the alleged speed — are required.

---

### Q605 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.21 · **Source:** BPOC 22.21

The Driver Responsibility Program (DRP), formerly found in Transportation Code Chapter 708, was repealed effective September ______, 2019.

**Answer:** 1

**Explanation:** DRP repeal took effect September 1, 2019.

---

### Q606 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.21 · **Source:** BPOC 22.21

Under BPOC 22.21, what happened to Driver Responsibility Program surcharge assessments when the program was repealed on September 1, 2019?

- [x] All DRP surcharge assessments were waived, with no future charges to be assessed
- [ ] Surcharges were reduced by half but remained collectible
- [ ] Surcharges converted to a one-time flat reinstatement fee
- [ ] Surcharges were transferred to the county tax assessor-collector for collection

**Explanation:** The repeal waived all DRP surcharge assessments and stopped any future assessment.

---

### Q607 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.21 · **Source:** BPOC 22.21

The Driver Responsibility Program was originally enacted in 2003 and was found in Chapter ______ of the Texas Transportation Code.

**Answer:** 708

**Explanation:** DRP lived in the now-repealed Transportation Code Chapter 708.

---

### Q608 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.21 · **Source:** BPOC 22.21

Under BPOC 22.21, even though DRP surcharges were repealed and waived as of September 1, 2019, what remains an individual's responsibility?

- [x] Any other suspensions, fines, or fees on the person's driving record
- [ ] A one-time reinstatement fee of $250
- [ ] Any DRP surcharge assessed before 2010
- [ ] Nothing — all driving record consequences were also waived

**Explanation:** The repeal only waived DRP surcharges; other suspensions, fines, or fees on the driving record are unaffected.

---

### Q609 — Motorcycles
**LO:** 22.22 · **Source:** TC 661.001(1)

Under TC 661.001(1), a "motorcycle" is a motor vehicle designed to propel itself with not more than ______ wheels in contact with the ground and having a ______ for the use of the rider.

**Answer:** three | saddle

**Explanation:** The definition caps the wheel count at three and requires a saddle, which is why a three-wheeled vehicle with a cab, seat, and seat belt is excluded.

---

### Q610 — Motorcycles
**LO:** 22.22 · **Source:** TC 661.001(1)

Under TC 661.001(1), a "motorcycle" is a motor vehicle designed to propel itself with not more than how many wheels in contact with the ground?

- [x] Three
- [ ] Two
- [ ] Four
- [ ] Two or three, but not one

**Explanation:** Not more than three wheels, plus a saddle for the rider, defines a motorcycle under this chapter.

---

### Q611 — Motorcycles
**LO:** 22.22 · **Source:** TC 661.0015(b)

Under TC 661.0015(b), for purposes of Chapter 661, an autocycle is considered to be a motorcycle, except that the chapter does not apply to an autocycle that has a partially or fully enclosed ______.

**Answer:** cab

**Explanation:** An enclosed-cab autocycle falls outside Chapter 661's headgear requirements entirely.

---

### Q612 — Motorcycles
**LO:** 22.22 · **Source:** TC 661.0015(b)

Under TC 661.0015(b), Chapter 661 (protective headgear) does NOT apply to an autocycle that:

- [x] Has a partially or fully enclosed cab
- [ ] Has fewer than four wheels
- [ ] Was manufactured before 2015
- [ ] Is operated by a person under 21

**Explanation:** The enclosed-cab exception removes an autocycle from Chapter 661's coverage.

---

### Q613 — Motorcycles
**LO:** 22.22 · **Source:** TC 661.003(a)-(b)

Under TC 661.003, a person commits an offense if the person operates or rides as a passenger on a motorcycle on a public street or highway and is not wearing protective headgear that meets safety standards adopted by the ______; it is also an offense for an operator to carry a ______ who is not wearing such headgear.

**Answer:** department | passenger

**Explanation:** Both the operator's own headgear and any passenger's headgear must meet DPS-adopted safety standards.

---

### Q614 — Motorcycles
**LO:** 22.22 · **Source:** TC 661.003(a)

Under TC 661.003(a), the protective headgear worn by a motorcycle operator or passenger must meet safety standards adopted by:

- [x] The Department of Public Safety
- [ ] The National Highway Traffic Safety Administration
- [ ] The Texas Department of Motor Vehicles
- [ ] The Texas Department of Licensing and Regulation

**Explanation:** TC 661.001(2) defines "department" (for this chapter) as the Department of Public Safety, which sets the headgear safety standards.

---

### Q615 — Motorcycles
**LO:** 22.22 · **Source:** TC 661.003(c)
