- [ ] A single lane only, with no exceptions
- [ ] The entire roadway until a peace officer arrives to reopen it

**Explanation:** 546.007(c) limits a firefighter's lane closure to the affected lane(s) plus one additional lane, unless the safety of emergency personnel operating on the road requires more lanes closed; the firefighter must also deploy an authorized emergency vehicle with signals meeting 547.305/547.702 (546.007(d)).

---

### Q766 — Crash Investigation Procedures
**LO:** 22.31 · **Source:** BPOC 22.31

Per BPOC crash-scene management guidance, a seriously injured person should generally not be removed from a vehicle except in which circumstance?

- [x] When there is a fire
- [ ] Whenever an ambulance is more than 10 minutes away
- [ ] Whenever the vehicle is blocking a travel lane
- [ ] Whenever the injured person requests to be moved

**Explanation:** BPOC training instructs officers to administer first aid and summon additional medical aid without removing a seriously injured person from the vehicle, except in the case of fire — where the immediate danger outweighs the risk of movement.

---

### Q767 — Crash Investigation Procedures
**LO:** 22.31 · **Source:** BPOC 22.31

Per BPOC's procedures for property collection at a crash scene, once property is identified as evidence to the case, an officer must secure and mark the evidence and maintain the chain of ______.

**Answer:** custody

**Explanation:** Maintaining chain of custody is the core requirement once crash-scene property is identified as evidence, distinct from ordinary property inventory/release procedures for non-evidentiary items.

---

### Q768 — Crash Investigation Procedures
**LO:** 22.32 · **Source:** BPOC 22.32

Per BPOC interviewing procedures, when a crash involves multiple drivers and witnesses, an officer should generally:

- [x] Separate the parties and interview each one separately
- [ ] Interview all parties together so accounts can be cross-checked in real time
- [ ] Interview only the driver believed to be at fault
- [ ] Wait until all parties have discussed the crash among themselves before interviewing anyone

**Explanation:** Separating parties and interviewing each individually prevents accounts from being influenced by what others say, and is standard BPOC interviewing procedure at a crash scene.

---

### Q769 — Crash Investigation Procedures
**LO:** 22.32 · **Source:** BPOC 22.32

Which of the following is listed among the physical appearance factors an officer should note about a driver during a crash investigation?

- [x] Stance indicative of injury or possible intoxication (balanced, off-balanced, needing support)
- [ ] The make and model of the driver's cell phone
- [ ] The driver's political affiliation
- [ ] The driver's marital status

**Explanation:** BPOC lists restrictions noted on the driver's license, dress, stance, and odor as physical appearance factors relevant to a crash investigation — cues that can point toward injury or intoxication.

---

### Q770 — Crash Investigation Procedures
**LO:** 22.32 · **Source:** BPOC 22.32

When examining a vehicle involved in a crash for equipment failures that may warrant expert examination, BPOC guidance specifically lists braking failures, ______ failure, and other mechanical failures such as headlights being out.

**Answer:** steering

**Explanation:** Braking and steering failures — along with other mechanical defects like inoperative headlights — are the equipment-failure categories BPOC flags as worth an expert follow-up examination.

---

### Q771 — Crash Investigation Procedures
**LO:** 22.32 · **Source:** BPOC 22.32

Which of the following is listed as a physical obstruction or environmental condition that may contribute to a crash?

- [x] Debris on the roadway
- [ ] The color of the vehicles involved
- [ ] The drivers' insurance carriers
- [ ] The time the officer arrives on scene

**Explanation:** BPOC lists physical roadway conditions, debris on the roadway, weather, and nighttime driving as environmental/obstruction factors that may have contributed to a crash.

---

### Q772 — Crash Investigation Procedures
**LO:** 22.33 · **Source:** BPOC 22.33

**Answer:** Nomograph

"______" is a graph on which three or more scales are arranged so that a straight line drawn through values on any two will cross the third at a corresponding value.

**Explanation:** A nomograph is the specific graphing tool BPOC identifies for crash-scene calculations (e.g., relating speed, distance, and time) using three or more aligned scales.

---

### Q773 — Crash Investigation Procedures
**LO:** 22.33 · **Source:** BPOC 22.33

When collecting physical evidence at a crash scene, an officer inspects a vehicle's VIN plate for what purpose?

- [x] To confirm the plate is properly secured and that the number corresponds to the vehicle by comparing it with the registration certificate
- [ ] To determine the vehicle's original sale price
- [ ] To identify the vehicle's insurance carrier
- [ ] To confirm the vehicle passed its most recent safety inspection

**Explanation:** BPOC directs officers to check that a vehicle's VIN corresponds to the vehicle (by comparing it to the registration certificate) and that the VIN plate itself is properly and securely affixed — a basic check against a swapped or altered VIN.

---

### Q774 — Crash Investigation Procedures
**LO:** 22.33 · **Source:** BPOC 22.33

In a hit-and-run investigation, which of the following pieces of physical evidence can help show the fleeing vehicle's direction of travel from the scene?

- [x] Vehicle fluids (e.g., a leaking-fluid trail)
- [ ] The victim's driver's license
- [ ] The posted speed limit sign
- [ ] The time of day the crash occurred

**Explanation:** BPOC notes that vehicle fluids can reveal the suspect vehicle's direction of travel away from a hit-and-run scene, alongside paint flakes/transfer and other trace evidence.

---

### Q775 — Crash Investigation Procedures
**LO:** 22.33 · **Source:** BPOC 22.33

Per BPOC guidance, an officer should determine whether a crash scene is serious enough to warrant photographs, which may also be necessary when statements ______ or when physical evidence does not support the statements given.

**Answer:** conflict

**Explanation:** Conflicting statements or physical evidence that contradicts what witnesses/drivers report are two specific triggers BPOC identifies for photographing a crash scene beyond the standard sketch and measurements.

---

### Q776 — Crash Investigation Procedures
**LO:** 22.33 · **Source:** BPOC 22.33

Which of the following is listed among the factors that may contribute to a vehicular crash?

- [x] Failure to yield right-of-way
- [ ] The vehicle's paint color
- [ ] The number of passengers wearing seatbelts
- [ ] The manufacturer's warranty status

**Explanation:** BPOC lists speed, failure to yield right-of-way, improper turning movement, faulty equipment, weather conditions, and driver conditions as contributing factors to a vehicular crash.

---

### Q777 — Crash Investigation Procedures
**LO:** 22.34 · **Source:** BPOC 22.34

The standard written collision report an officer files under TC 550.062 is known by its form number as the "Texas Peace Officer's Crash Report," Form ______.

**Answer:** CR-3

**Explanation:** Form CR-3 is the Texas Peace Officer's Crash Report — the form officers use to satisfy the written-report requirement of TC 550.062.

---

### Q778 — Crash Investigation Procedures
**LO:** 22.34 · **Source:** BPOC 22.34

Which crash-related form is described as a "vehicle damage guide for traffic crash investigators"?

- [x] CR-80
- [ ] CR-3
- [ ] CR-100
- [ ] CR-42

**Explanation:** CR-80 is the vehicle damage guide that helps investigators code the location and severity of damage on the CR-3; CR-100 instead provides instructions to police for reporting crashes.

---

### Q779 — Traffic Incident Management & Control
**LO:** 22.36 · **Source:** BPOC 22.36

To meet this learning objective, BPOC students must complete the National Traffic Incident Management (TIM) Responder Training Program, a course of at least ______ hours.

**Answer:** 4

**Explanation:** BPOC requires completion of the 4-hour National TIM Responder Training Program (a free online course is available) to satisfy LO 22.36.

---

### Q780 — Traffic Incident Management & Control
**LO:** 22.37 · **Source:** TC 541.002(3)

Under TC 541.002(3), a "local authority" means a county, municipality, or other local entity authorized to enact traffic laws, or a ______ district — but only when designating school crossing guards for the schools it operates.

**Answer:** school

**Explanation:** 541.002(3)(B) gives a school district "local authority" status solely for the limited purpose of designating school crossing guards — it isn't a general traffic-law-enacting authority the way a county or municipality is.

---

### Q781 — Traffic Incident Management & Control
**LO:** 22.37 · **Source:** BPOC 22.37

Per the Traffic Incident Management Handbook definition used in BPOC training, an "incident" is:

- [x] Any non-recurring event that causes a reduction of roadway capacity or an abnormal increase in demand and requires a law enforcement response
- [ ] Any crash involving a fatality
- [ ] Any traffic stop initiated by a peace officer
- [ ] Any event lasting longer than one hour

**Explanation:** The Federal Highway Administration's Traffic Incident Management Handbook defines "incident" broadly — a non-recurring capacity-reducing or demand-spiking event needing a law enforcement response — which covers crashes but also disabled vehicles, spilled cargo, and special events.

---

### Q782 — Traffic Incident Management & Control
**LO:** 22.37 · **Source:** BPOC 22.37

Which of the following is classified as an "uncommon" traffic incident (a special event) rather than a "common" traffic incident under BPOC's traffic-flow-management material?

- [x] A parade
- [ ] A traffic crash
- [ ] A disabled vehicle
- [ ] Spilled cargo

**Explanation:** BPOC lists traffic crashes, disabled vehicles, spilled cargo, and highway maintenance as common incidents, while sporting events, concerts, parades, and funerals are grouped as uncommon "special event" incidents.

---

### Q783 — Traffic Incident Management & Control
**LO:** 22.37 · **Source:** BPOC 22.37

Per BPOC's roadway-space-management guidance, an officer managing an incident scene should close only the lanes necessary for the protection of victims and responders, and should ______ the time those lanes are closed.

**Answer:** minimize

**Explanation:** Minimizing lane-closure time (along with expediting the closure itself) is one of BPOC's core roadway-space-management principles for restoring traffic flow as quickly as safely possible.

---

### Q784 — Traffic Incident Management & Control
**LO:** 22.37 · **Source:** TC 545.156(a-1)

Under TC 545.156, when an authorized emergency vehicle lawfully using its audible and visual signals approaches, an ordinary driver — unless otherwise directed by a police officer — must:

- [x] Yield the right-of-way, pull as close as possible to the right-hand edge or curb clear of any intersection, and stop and remain standing until the emergency vehicle has passed
- [ ] Immediately stop wherever the vehicle happens to be, even in an active intersection
- [ ] Accelerate to clear the area ahead of the emergency vehicle as quickly as possible
- [ ] Continue driving normally as long as the driver maintains a safe following distance

**Explanation:** 545.156(a-1) requires yielding, pulling to the right-hand edge clear of intersections, and stopping until the emergency vehicle passes — the baseline duty every other motorist owes when an officer is directing an emergency response through traffic.

---

### Q785 — Traffic Incident Management & Control
**LO:** 22.38 · **Source:** BPOC 22.38

Per BPOC guidance on vehicle lighting during traffic control, officers should limit the number of flashing lights displayed because:

- [x] Drivers who are fatigued, impaired, or aged have more difficulty with glare, and people tend to drive toward where they look
- [ ] Flashing lights drain the patrol vehicle's battery too quickly
- [ ] State law caps the number of emergency lights a vehicle may display
- [ ] Flashing lights interfere with the officer's in-car camera system

**Explanation:** BPOC's rationale is driver behavior, not equipment limits: excess flashing lights make gaze more difficult for fatigued, impaired, or aged drivers, and since people tend to steer toward what they're looking at, too many lights can actually draw traffic toward the scene instead of away from it.

---

### Q786 — Traffic Incident Management & Control
**LO:** 22.38 · **Source:** BPOC 22.38

To signal oncoming traffic to stop by hand, BPOC instructs an officer to look directly at the driver, extend the arm and point at the driver, then raise the ______ and hold that position until traffic has stopped.

**Answer:** palm

**Explanation:** The stop signal is: point at the driver with the arm extended, then raise the palm and hold it until traffic has come to a stop.

---

### Q787 — Traffic Incident Management & Control
**LO:** 22.38 · **Source:** BPOC 22.38

Under BPOC's whistle-command guidance for directing traffic, what does one long blast of a police whistle (with an accompanying hand signal) indicate?

- [x] Attracting drivers' attention to stop
- [ ] Attracting drivers' attention to start moving
- [ ] A general warning signal
- [ ] An all-clear signal to disperse a crowd

**Explanation:** BPOC's whistle commands are: one long blast to signal stop, two short blasts to signal start, and short rapid blasts (normally three) as a general warning.

---

### Q788 — Traffic Incident Management & Control
**LO:** 22.38 · **Source:** BPOC 22.38

BPOC identifies five areas of a work zone: the advanced warning area, the transition area, the buffer area, the work area, and the ______ area, which allows traffic to return to normal operation.

**Answer:** termination

**Explanation:** The termination area is the last of the five standard work-zone areas — it's where traffic control ends and normal traffic flow resumes after passing through the advanced warning, transition, buffer, and work areas.

---

### Q789 — Traffic Incident Management & Control
**LO:** 22.38 · **Source:** BPOC 22.38

Per BPOC guidance on pedestrian control while directing traffic at an incident, an officer should:

- [x] Hold pedestrians back behind the curb, off the roadway, until traffic movement has stopped, and start/stop their flow the same way as vehicle traffic
- [ ] Allow pedestrians to cross whenever there is a gap in traffic, regardless of the officer's signals
- [ ] Direct pedestrians to remain in the roadway near the curb line at all times
- [ ] Treat pedestrian control as outside the officer's responsibility at a traffic-control scene

**Explanation:** BPOC treats pedestrian control as parallel to vehicle traffic control: hold pedestrians behind the curb until vehicle movement has stopped, then start and stop their flow the same way vehicle traffic is started and stopped, while preventing jaywalking and other illegal crossings.

---

## Additional Questions — 2026 Reference-Material Expansion (Task 12)

*The questions below (Q790-Q932) were added from the newly researched 2026 direct-citation material: House Bills 291 (occupational driver's license overhaul), 1281 (golf carts), 1442 (racing/reckless-driving forfeiture and organized-crime predicate offenses), 2190 (statutory "accident" to "collision" terminology change across Chapter 550 and related sections), Senate Bill 792 (disabled veteran plates and parking placards), House Bill 558 (mandatory blood-specimen expansion under the Implied Consent law), and DPS/DMV Administrative Rules 37 TAC §§21.2, 21.4, 21.5 and 43 TAC §217.27 (vehicle equipment, safety-guard, safety-chain, and registration-display standards) — plus the strongest already-researched one-hop tertiary citations reached directly from Chapter 22's own text (the Move Over/Slow Down law, tow-away and storage-company rules, child passenger safety seats, the racing and open-container statutes, distracted driving, automated vehicles, vehicle equipment/inspection definitions, registration and dealer-licensing terminology, custom/antique/collector vehicle registration, disabled-pedestrian right-of-way, freeway ramp-control and pedestrian-hybrid-beacon signals, and escort flag vehicle requirements). House Bill 1163 (boating while intoxicated with a child passenger) was researched but excluded here as out of scope: it amends Penal Code Chapter 49 provisions specific to watercraft, not ground-vehicle traffic law within BPOC's Chapter 22.*

### Q790 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 521.2421 (HB 291)

Under the 2023 rewrite of the occupational driver's license statute, what replaced former Section 521.242 as the way a person establishes eligibility for an occupational license?

- [x] A verified petition filed under new Section 521.2421
- [ ] An affidavit signed by the person's employer only
- [ ] A finding by DPS made without any court filing
- [ ] A plea agreement entered in the underlying criminal case

**Explanation:** HB 291 repealed the old eligibility scheme and consolidated it into new Section 521.2421, which requires a verified petition process rather than the prior mechanism.

---

### Q791 — Driver's License & CDL
**LO:** 22.7 · **Source:** TC 521.001 (HB 291)

HB 291 expanded the definition of "essential need" that justifies an occupational driver's license to specifically include travel to and from:

- [x] An educational facility
- [ ] A place of worship
- [ ] A family member's residence in another state
- [ ] Any retail establishment

**Explanation:** The amended "essential need" definition added travel to and from an educational facility alongside the existing essential-need categories (such as employment and household duties).

---

- [x] $10

- [ ] $50
- [ ] No fee is required

**Explanation:** New Section 521.2465 sets a $10 fee for the restricted license it creates.

---

### Q793 — Driver's License & CDL

- [x] 30 days
- [ ] 10 days
- [ ] 60 days
- [ ] 90 days

**Explanation:** Section 521.2465 requires 30 days' notice under the new restricted-license framework.

- [ ] Automatic license reinstatement after one year
- [ ] Waiver of all occupational license fees

### Q797 — Driver's License & CDL

### Q799 — Driver's License & CDL

What did HB 291 do to former Section 521.251, which had graduated an occupational license's effective date based on the person's prior suspension history?

- [x] Repealed it entirely
- [ ] Left it unchanged
- [ ] Doubled the graduated waiting periods it imposed
- [ ] Moved it, unchanged, to a new section number

### Q800 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.403(a)(3) (HB 1281)

HB 1281 increased how far from its usual parking spot a golf cart may be operated on a highway with a posted speed limit of 35 mph or less (during daytime, for transportation to or from a golf course)?

- [x] From two miles to five miles
- [ ] From five miles to ten miles
- [ ] From one mile to three miles
- [ ] The distance limit was removed entirely

**Explanation:** Section 551.403(a)(3) previously capped this at two miles; HB 1281 raised it to five miles.

### Q801 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.403(a)(1) (HB 1281)

HB 1281 changed the master-planned-community platting requirement for golf cart operation from requiring "a plat" to requiring:

- [x] One or more plats approved by a county or municipality
- [ ] No plat at all
- [ ] A plat approved by the Texas Department of Transportation only
- [ ] A plat recorded within the preceding 12 months

### Q802 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.4031 (HB 1281)

Under Section 551.4031, as renumbered/amended by HB 1281, which two entities may prohibit golf cart operation on a highway in the interest of safety?

- [x] A county or municipality, and the Texas Department of Transportation
- [ ] Only the Texas Department of Public Safety
- [ ] Only the Texas Department of Transportation
- [ ] Only a homeowners' association

### Q803 — Bicycles, Scooters & Low-Speed Vehicles
**LO:** 22.18 · **Source:** TC 551.403(b) (HB 1281)

In a master planned community, a golf cart may be operated on a highway posted 35 mph or less without a golf cart license plate, including:

- [x] Through an intersection of a highway posted above 35 mph
- [ ] On any interstate frontage road regardless of speed limit
- [ ] At night without headlamps
- [ ] By a driver without any form of identification

**Explanation:** Section 551.403(b) specifically allows crossing through an intersection of a higher-speed highway without a golf cart license plate.

---

### Q804 — Implied Consent
**LO:** 22.29 · **Source:** TC 724.012(a-1) (HB 558)

HB 558 added new Subsection (a-1) to Section 724.012, making blood specimen collection mandatory (rather than merely authorized after refusal) when the arrest is for an intoxication offense involving a vehicle or watercraft AND the officer reasonably believes the accident caused:

- [x] Death or serious bodily injury to any individual
- [ ] Only property damage over $1,000
- [ ] A traffic violation unrelated to intoxication
- [ ] Injury solely to the arrested person

**Explanation:** New Subsection (a-1) makes the blood draw mandatory (not contingent on refusal in the same way) when the officer reasonably believes the accident caused death or will cause death, or serious bodily injury, to any individual.

---

### Q805 — Implied Consent
**LO:** 22.29 · **Source:** TC 724.012(a-1) (HB 558)

Under new Section 724.012(a-1), a peace officer SHALL require a blood specimen even if the person does NOT refuse voluntary testing, so long as the other (a-1) conditions (arrest under Penal Code Chapter 49 involving a vehicle/watercraft, an accident, and reasonably believed death or serious bodily injury) are met. True or False?

- [x] False — refusal of the officer's request to submit voluntarily is still a required element of (a-1)
- [ ] True — refusal is no longer relevant under (a-1)
- [ ] True, but only for repeat offenders
- [ ] False — (a-1) applies only to breath specimens

**Explanation:** Section 724.012(a-1)(2) still requires that "the person refuses the officer's request to submit to the taking of a specimen voluntarily" as one of its four conditions — refusal remains an element even under the new mandatory-draw provision.

---

### Q806 — Implied Consent
**LO:** 22.29 · **Source:** TC 724.012(e) (HB 558)

HB 558 added Section 724.012(e), which conditions an officer's authority to require a specimen under the section on the officer:

- [x] Obtaining a warrant, or having probable cause that exigent circumstances exist
- [ ] Having a supervisor's written approval
- [ ] Completing a breath test first regardless of refusal
- [ ] Waiting a minimum of two hours after the arrest

**Explanation:** New Subsection (e) requires either a warrant directing the specimen be taken, or probable cause that exigent circumstances exist.

---

### Q807 — Implied Consent
**LO:** 22.29 · **Source:** TC 724.013 (HB 558)

Section 724.013 generally prohibits taking a specimen if the person refuses, but HB 558 added which exception to that prohibition?

- [x] Section 724.012(a-1)
- [ ] Section 724.012(d)
- [ ] Section 724.011
- [ ] There is no exception — refusal always bars the specimen

**Explanation:** Section 724.013 now reads "Except as provided by Section 724.012(a-1) or (b)" — HB 558 added the (a-1) exception alongside the pre-existing (b) exception.

---

### Q808 — Implied Consent
**LO:** 22.29 · **Source:** TC 724.012(a-1) (HB 558)

HB 558 added Subsection (a-1) to Section 724.012 of the Transportation Code, expanding mandatory blood-specimen collection for intoxication arrests involving a vehicle or ______.

**Answer:** watercraft

**Explanation:** The new mandatory-draw provision applies to intoxication arrests involving the operation of a motor vehicle or a watercraft.

---

### Q809 — Implied Consent
**LO:** 22.29 · **Source:** TC 724.012(b) (HB 558)

Prior to HB 558, Section 724.012(b) already required a specimen after refusal in certain accident-injury scenarios. HB 558 restructured this by making Subsection (b) expressly "subject to":

- [x] Subsection (a-1)
- [ ] Subsection (c)
- [ ] A different chapter of the Penal Code
- [ ] Nothing — Subsection (b) was left completely untouched

**Explanation:** The amended Subsection (b) opens with "Subject to Subsection (a-1), a peace officer shall require..." tying the two provisions together.

---

### Q810 — Implied Consent
**LO:** 22.29 · **Source:** TC 724.012(c) (HB 558)

HB 558 revised Section 724.012(c), which addresses who designates the type of specimen to be taken. As amended, this authority belongs to the peace officer:

- [x] Except as provided by Subsection (a-1)
- [ ] Only with the arrested person's consent
- [ ] Only after a magistrate's order
- [ ] Only for breath specimens, never blood

### Q811 — Implied Consent
**LO:** 22.29 · **Source:** TC 724.012(a-1)(1) (HB 558)

The mandatory blood-draw provision added by HB 558 applies to an arrest for an offense under which Penal Code chapter?

- [x] Chapter 49 (Intoxication and Alcoholic Beverage Offenses)
- [ ] Chapter 46 (Weapons)
- [ ] Chapter 71 (Organized Crime)
- [ ] Chapter 42 (Disorderly Conduct)

### Q812 — Crash Investigation Procedures
**LO:** 22.30 · **Source:** TC 550.021 (HB 2190)

HB 2190 replaced the term "accident" with "collision" throughout Chapter 550. Under the renamed Section 550.021, an operator involved in a collision that results or is reasonably likely to result in injury or death must do all of the following EXCEPT:

- [x] Notify their insurance carrier before doing anything else
- [ ] Immediately stop the vehicle at or as close as possible to the scene
- [ ] Immediately return to the scene if not stopped there
- [ ] Remain at the scene until Section 550.023 duties are met

**Explanation:** Section 550.021(a) requires stopping at (or returning to) the scene, determining if aid is needed, and remaining until Section 550.023 is satisfied — it does not require notifying an insurer as a first step.

---

### Q813 — Crash Investigation Procedures
**LO:** 22.30 · **Source:** TC 550.021(c) (HB 2190)

Under Section 550.021(c), failing to stop or comply after a collision resulting in death of a person is punished as:

- [x] A felony of the second degree
- [ ] A felony of the third degree
- [ ] A Class A misdemeanor
- [ ] A Class B misdemeanor

**Explanation:** Section 550.021(c)(1)(A) makes failure to stop and comply, when the collision results in death, a second-degree felony.

---

### Q814 — Crash Investigation Procedures
**LO:** 22.30 · **Source:** TC 550.021(c) (HB 2190)

Under Section 550.021(c), failing to stop or comply after a collision resulting in serious bodily injury (as defined by Penal Code Section 1.07) is punished as:

- [x] A felony of the third degree
- [ ] A felony of the second degree
- [ ] A state jail felony
- [ ] A Class A misdemeanor

**Explanation:** Section 550.021(c)(1)(B) makes this a third-degree felony.

---

### Q815 — Crash Investigation Procedures
**LO:** 22.31 · **Source:** TC 550.022 (HB 2190)

- [x] "Collision Involving Damage to Vehicle"
- [ ] "Property Damage Collision Report"
- [ ] "Minor Collision Notification"
- [ ] The section heading was left unchanged

**Explanation:** HB 2190 amended the heading of Section 550.022 to substitute "Collision" for "Accident."

---

### Q816 — Crash Investigation Procedures
**LO:** 22.31 · **Source:** TC 550.022(b) (HB 2190)

Under Section 550.022(b), when a damage-only collision occurs on a freeway main lane, ramp, shoulder, or median in a metropolitan area and each vehicle can be safely driven, the operators should move to:

- [x] A designated collision investigation site, if available, or another suitable location off the traveled way
- [ ] The nearest police station
- [ ] Any location, since moving is optional
- [ ] The center median only

**Explanation:** Section 550.022(b) directs operators to a designated collision investigation site if available, or the frontage road, nearest suitable cross street, or other suitable location.

---

### Q817 — Crash Investigation Procedures
**LO:** 22.30 · **Source:** TC 550.023 (HB 2190)

Section 550.023, "Duty to Give Information and Render Aid," requires the operator to provide all of the following to a person injured in the collision EXCEPT:

- [x] A copy of the operator's vehicle registration certificate
- [ ] The operator's name and address
- [ ] The registration number of the vehicle being driven
- [ ] The name of the operator's motor vehicle liability insurer

**Explanation:** Section 550.023 requires name, address, vehicle registration number, and liability insurer name — not a physical copy of the registration certificate.

### Q818 — Crash Investigation Procedures
**LO:** 22.30 · **Source:** TC 550.026 (HB 2190)

Under the renamed Section 550.026, "Immediate Report of Collision," if a collision occurs not more than 100 feet outside the limits of a municipality, the operator must immediately notify:

- [x] The local police department or the sheriff's office
- [ ] Only the Texas Department of Transportation
- [ ] Only the nearest hospital
- [ ] Only the Department of Public Safety headquarters in Austin

**Explanation:** Section 550.026(a)(2) requires notice to the local police department or the sheriff's office for a collision occurring not more than 100 feet outside a municipality's limits.

### Q819 — Crash Investigation Procedures
**LO:** 22.31 · **Source:** TC 550.041(a) (HB 2190)

HB 2190 retitled Subchapter C of Chapter 550 as "Investigation of Collision." Under Section 550.041(a), a peace officer notified of a motor vehicle collision resulting in injury, death, or property damage of at least what amount may investigate and file justifiable charges?

- [x] $1,000
- [ ] $500
- [ ] $2,500
- [ ] $5,000

- [x] $1,000 to the property of any one person
- [ ] $500 to the property of any one person
- [ ] $2,000 to the property of any one person
- [ ] Any amount, however small

### Q841 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting

- [ ] Stricter than the federal standard by design
- [ ] Left to each municipality to set independently

### Q852 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** 37 TAC §21.4(d)

37 TAC Section 21.4(d) requires a safety guard or flap to be at least as wide as:

- [x] The tires it is protecting
- [ ] The full width of the vehicle
- [ ] 24 inches, regardless of tire size
- [ ] The axle housing

**Explanation:** Section 21.4(d) requires the guard/flap width to match or exceed the width of the tires it protects.

---

### Q853 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** 37 TAC §21.4(a)

37 TAC Section 21.4(a) defines a "super single tire" as:

- [x] A wide-base, single tire that may be used in place of two standard tires on the same axle
- [ ] Any tire rated for speeds above 80 mph
- [ ] A dual-mounted tire pair on one hub
- [ ] A tire specifically designed for golf carts

**Explanation:** Section 21.4(a)'s own definition describes a super single tire as a wide-base single tire substituting for two standard tires on an axle.

---

### Q854 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** 37 TAC §21.5(a)

Under 37 TAC Section 21.5(a), a person towing a trailer, semitrailer, house trailer, or another motor vehicle on a public highway with a passenger car or light truck must use safety chains that are:

- [x] Of a type and attachment manner approved by the department
- [ ] Any chains at all, regardless of rating
- [ ] Made of galvanized steel specifically
- [ ] Optional if the towed vehicle has working brakes

**Explanation:** Section 21.5(a) requires safety chains of a department-approved type, attached in a department-approved manner.

---

### Q855 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** 37 TAC §21.5(b)

37 TAC Section 21.5(b) lists exceptions to the safety chain requirement. Which of the following is NOT one of the listed exceptions?

- [x] Trailers used to transport hazardous materials
- [ ] Trailers used for agricultural purposes
- [ ] Trailers/vehicles operated under Federal Motor Carrier Safety Regulations
- [ ] Fifth wheel or gooseneck semitrailers

**Explanation:** Section 21.5(b) exempts agricultural trailers, FMCSR-compliant combinations, pre-1994 manufacturer-installed chains, and fifth wheel/gooseneck semitrailers — not hazmat trailers generically.

---

### Q856 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** 37 TAC §21.5(d)(1)

Under 37 TAC Section 21.5(d)(1), how many separate safety chains must be used simultaneously whenever safety chains are required?

- [x] Two
- [ ] One
- [ ] Three
- [ ] Four

**Explanation:** Section 21.5(d)(1) requires two separate and individual safety chains used simultaneously.

---

### Q857 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** 37 TAC §21.5(c)(2)

37 TAC Section 21.5(c)(2) defines "light truck" as any truck with a manufacturer's rated carrying capacity not to exceed:

- [x] 2,000 pounds
- [ ] 1,000 pounds
- [ ] 3,500 pounds
- [ ] 5,000 pounds

**Explanation:** Section 21.5(c)(2) caps the "light truck" carrying capacity at 2,000 pounds, covering pickups, panel delivery trucks, and carryall trucks.

---

### Q858 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** 37 TAC §21.5(d)(2)

37 TAC Section 21.5(d)(2) requires safety chains to be connected in a manner that prevents:

- [x] The tow-bar from dropping to the ground if it fails or disconnects
- [ ] The towed vehicle from being repainted a different color
- [ ] The chains from being visible to other drivers
- [ ] The towing vehicle from exceeding 55 mph

**Explanation:** Section 21.5(d)(2) requires the connection to prevent the tow-bar from dropping to the ground upon failure or disconnection, and bars the chains from contacting the road surface during movement.

---

### Q859 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** 37 TAC §21.5(b)(3)

Under 37 TAC Section 21.5(b)(3), the safety chain requirement does not apply to trailers/semitrailers/house trailers equipped with manufacturer-installed safety chains before the effective date of the section, which is:

- [x] August 18, 1994
- [ ] September 1, 1997
- [ ] January 1, 1994
- [ ] April 12, 2012

**Explanation:** Section 21.5(b)(3) grandfathers in manufacturer-installed chains predating the rule's August 18, 1994 effective date.

---

### Q860 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** 37 TAC §21.5(e)

Under 37 TAC Section 21.5(e), enforcement action for safety chain violations should be initiated when chains are improperly attached to the degree that:

- [x] One or both chains are in contact with the surface of the road
- [ ] The chains are a different color than the vehicle
- [ ] The chains were purchased from an unlicensed vendor
- [ ] The driver has not registered the trailer in the current calendar year

**Explanation:** Section 21.5(e)(2) specifically flags road-surface contact by improperly attached chains as an enforcement trigger.

---

### Q861 — Vehicle Registration & Titling
**LO:** 22.25 · **Source:** 43 TAC §217.27(b)(1)

Under 43 TAC Section 217.27(b)(1), unless otherwise prescribed by law, a registered vehicle must display two license plates positioned no less than how far from the ground, measured from the bottom?

- [x] 12 inches
- [ ] 6 inches
- [ ] 18 inches
- [ ] 24 inches

**Explanation:** Section 217.27(b)(1) sets the minimum plate height at 12 inches from the ground, measured from the bottom.

---

### Q862 — Vehicle Registration & Titling
**LO:** 22.25 · **Source:** 43 TAC §217.27(b)(2)

Under 43 TAC Section 217.27(b)(2), which vehicle types display only ONE rear license plate rather than front and rear plates?

- [x] Road tractor, motorcycle, trailer, or semitrailer
- [ ] Passenger car and light truck
- [ ] Any vehicle registered for personal use
- [ ] Any vehicle over 10 years old

**Explanation:** Section 217.27(b)(2) lists road tractors, motorcycles, trailers, and semitrailers as displaying a single rear plate.

---

### Q863 — Vehicle Registration & Titling
**LO:** 22.25 · **Source:** 43 TAC §217.27(a)(1)

Under 43 TAC Section 217.27(a)(1), if a vehicle has a windshield, the vehicle registration insignia must be attached to:

- [x] The inside lower left corner of the front windshield, without obstructing the driver's vision
- [ ] The outside of the rear windshield
- [ ] The dashboard, visible from outside the vehicle
- [ ] The driver's side window

**Explanation:** Section 217.27(a)(1) requires attachment to the inside lower left corner of the front windshield in a manner that does not obstruct the driver's vision.

---

### Q864 — Vehicle Registration & Titling
**LO:** 22.25 · **Source:** 43 TAC §217.27(a)(4)

Under 43 TAC Section 217.27(a)(4), a vehicle registered as a former military vehicle under Transportation Code Section 504.502 displays, instead of a symbol/tab/plate, the vehicle's:

- [x] Registration number, in letters/numbers at least two inches tall
- [ ] Original military unit designation only
- [ ] VIN etched into the windshield
- [ ] A blank placeholder plate

**Explanation:** Section 217.27(a)(4) requires the registration number to be displayed prominently in characters at least two inches in height.

---

### Q865 — Vehicle Registration & Titling
**LO:** 22.25 · **Source:** 43 TAC §217.27(d)

Under 43 TAC Section 217.27(d), when reviewing a personalized license plate application, the department:

- [x] Need not consider the applicant's subjective intent or declared meaning
- [ ] Must accept the applicant's stated meaning as conclusive
- [ ] Must hold a public hearing before any denial
- [ ] May only deny plates that use profanity in English

**Explanation:** Section 217.27(d) expressly states the department need not consider the applicant's subjective intent or declared meaning when evaluating objectionable content.

---

### Q866 — Vehicle Registration & Titling
**LO:** 22.25 · **Source:** 43 TAC §217.27(d)(2)(A)

Under 43 TAC Section 217.27(d)(2)(A), a personalized plate number "69" is prohibited as indecent UNLESS it is used:

- [x] With the full year (1969) or in combination with a reference to a vehicle
- [ ] On a plate issued before 2015
- [ ] On a plate for a vehicle owned by a corporation
- [ ] On a motorcycle plate only

**Explanation:** Section 217.27(d)(2)(A) carves out an exception for "69" when used with the full year 1969 or with a vehicle reference.

### Q867 — Vehicle Registration & Titling
**LO:** 22.25 · **Source:** 43 TAC §217.27(f)

Under 43 TAC Section 217.27(f), an appeal of a personalized-plate cancellation or non-issuance decision must be filed with the executive director (or designee) within how many days of notification?

- [x] 20 days
- [ ] 10 days
- [ ] 30 days
- [ ] 60 days

**Explanation:** Section 217.27(f) sets a 20-day window to appeal a cancellation or non-issuance decision.

---

### Q868 — Vehicle Registration & Titling
**LO:** 22.25 · **Source:** 43 TAC §217.27(c)(2)(A)-(B)

Under 43 TAC Section 217.27(c)(2), vehicles described by Transportation Code Section 502.0024 may obtain a registration insignia valid for how many possible consecutive-month periods?

- [x] 12, 24, 36, 48, or 60 months
- [ ] Only 12 months
- [ ] Only 24 or 36 months
- [ ] Only a single lifetime registration

**Explanation:** Section 217.27(c)(2)(A) lists 12, 24, 36, 48, or 60 consecutive months as available registration periods for these vehicles, on payment of fees for each full year.

### Q869 — Right-of-Way
**LO:** 22.11 · **Source:** TC 545.157

Texas's "Move Over/Slow Down" law under Section 545.157 requires a driver approaching certain stopped vehicles with emergency or specified lights activated to either vacate the lane next to that vehicle if it can be done safely, or:

- [x] Slow to a speed not to exceed 20 mph less than the posted speed limit
- [ ] Come to a complete stop regardless of traffic conditions
- [ ] Sound the horn continuously while passing
- [ ] Pull over and wait until the vehicle departs

**Explanation:** Section 545.157 requires either vacating the adjacent lane when safe, or reducing speed by up to 20 mph below the posted limit if the lane cannot be safely vacated (with additional floors for certain low speed limits).

---

### Q870 — Right-of-Way
**LO:** 22.11 · **Source:** TC 545.157(a)

Which of the following is NOT one of the categories of stopped vehicles that trigger the Move Over/Slow Down duty under Section 545.157?

- [x] Any personal vehicle stopped on the shoulder for a phone call
- [ ] An authorized emergency vehicle using visual signals
- [ ] A Texas Department of Transportation vehicle using visual signals
- [ ] A tow truck stopped with equipment activated, providing service

**Explanation:** Section 545.157 covers emergency vehicles, TxDOT vehicles, utility service vehicles, and tow trucks providing service with lights activated — not an ordinary personal vehicle stopped for an unrelated reason.

---

### Q871 — Right-of-Way
**LO:** 22.11 · **Source:** TC 545.157(e)

Section 545.157(e) defines "traffic control channelizing device" for purposes of the Move Over/Slow Down law. This term generally refers to devices used to:

- [x] Warn or guide traffic around a temporary hazard or condition, such as cones, barrels, or vertical panels
- [ ] Permanently mark municipal boundary lines
- [ ] Regulate parking meters in a downtown district
- [ ] Identify school bus routes only

**Explanation:** The channelizing-device definition in 545.157(e) covers devices such as cones, drums, vertical panels, and similar warning/guide devices used around temporary highway conditions.

---

### Q872 — Right-of-Way
**LO:** 22.11 · **Source:** TC 372.001

Under Section 372.001, a "toll project entity" is best described as:

- [x] A public or private entity authorized to develop, finance, or operate a toll project
- [ ] Any private towing company operating on a public highway
- [ ] A county sheriff's department
- [ ] A federal highway administration office

**Explanation:** Section 372.001(2) defines toll project entity broadly to cover public and private entities authorized to develop, finance, construct, or operate a toll project.

---

### Q873 — Right-of-Way
**LO:** 22.11 · **Source:** TC 545.157

The Move Over/Slow Down law applies to a driver on a highway with how many or more lanes carrying traffic in the same direction as the driver, in the relevant subsections addressing lane changes?

- [x] Two or more lanes
- [ ] Three or more lanes
- [ ] Four or more lanes
- [ ] The lane count is irrelevant

**Explanation:** Section 545.157 conditions the lane-change duty on the highway having two or more lanes carrying traffic in the driver's direction.

---

### Q874 — Right-of-Way
**LO:** 22.11 · **Source:** TC 545.157

Failing to comply with the Move Over/Slow Down requirements of Section 545.157 is classified as:

- [x] A misdemeanor traffic offense, enforceable like other moving violations under the Transportation Code
- [ ] A felony regardless of circumstances
- [ ] A civil infraction with no criminal penalty available
- [ ] Not an enforceable offense, only a recommended practice

**Explanation:** Move Over/Slow Down violations are enforced as Transportation Code misdemeanor offenses, consistent with the general penalty scheme for moving violations.

---

### Q875 — Stopping, Standing & Parking
**LO:** 22.13 · **Source:** TC 545.305

Under Section 545.305, a peace officer may authorize the removal (tow-away) of a vehicle that is unlawfully stopped, standing, or parked when:

- [x] The vehicle is left unattended in a position that constitutes a hazard or obstruction, per the statute's authorized circumstances
- [ ] Any vehicle is parked for more than five minutes without the owner present
- [ ] The vehicle's registration expired more than one day ago
- [ ] The officer personally owns a competing parking facility

**Explanation:** Section 545.305 authorizes removal of vehicles unlawfully stopped/parked under the statute's specified hazard/obstruction circumstances.

---

### Q876 — Stopping, Standing & Parking
**LO:** 22.13 · **Source:** TC 545.305(e)

Under Section 545.305(e), a "towing company" is generally defined as a person who, for compensation, tows or removes:

- [x] A vehicle from public or private property using a tow truck
- [ ] Only abandoned motor vehicles from junkyards
- [ ] Only vehicles involved in a fatal collision
- [ ] Only vehicles registered outside of Texas

**Explanation:** Section 545.305(e) defines a towing company by reference to towing/removing vehicles for compensation using a tow truck.

---

### Q877 — Stopping, Standing & Parking
**LO:** 22.13 · **Source:** TC 545.305(e)

Under Section 545.305(e), a "storage company" refers to a person who, for compensation, is engaged in:

- [x] The business of storing or parking vehicles that have been towed
- [ ] The retail sale of new and used vehicles
- [ ] Manufacturing tow trucks
- [ ] Only long-term airport parking operations

**Explanation:** Section 545.305(e) defines storage company by reference to the compensated business of storing towed vehicles.

---

### Q878 — Stopping, Standing & Parking
**LO:** 22.13 · **Source:** TC 545.305

A vehicle removed under Section 545.305's tow-away authority is typically taken to:

- [x] The nearest available storage facility designated for that purpose
- [ ] A police impound lot located out of state
- [ ] Any private residence chosen by the towing company
- [ ] A junkyard for immediate disposal

**Explanation:** Vehicles removed under Section 545.305 are taken to a designated nearby storage facility, consistent with the towing/storage-company framework the section establishes.

---

### Q879 — Stopping, Standing & Parking
**LO:** 22.13 · **Source:** TC 545.305

Under Section 545.305, the vehicle owner generally becomes responsible for which costs after an authorized tow-away?

- [x] Reasonable towing and storage fees
- [ ] No costs — removal is always at the municipality's expense
- [ ] Only storage fees, never towing fees
- [ ] A statutory fine payable to the arresting officer personally

**Explanation:** Section 545.305's tow-away scheme places reasonable towing and storage costs on the vehicle's owner/operator.

### Q880 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.412

Under Section 545.412, a child younger than 8 years of age must be secured in a child passenger safety seat system unless the child is:

- [x] Taller than 4 feet 9 inches
- [ ] Older than 5 years of age
- [ ] Riding in the front seat with adult supervision
- [ ] Wearing an adult-sized jacket

**Explanation:** Section 545.412 exempts a child from the safety-seat requirement once the child is taller than 4 feet 9 inches, even if younger than 8.

---

### Q881 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.412(f)

Under Section 545.412(f), "safety belt" is defined to include which combination of restraint types?

- [x] A lap belt, a shoulder strap, or a combination of both
- [ ] Only a five-point racing harness
- [ ] Only an airbag system
- [ ] Only a lap belt, never a shoulder strap

**Explanation:** Section 545.412(f) defines safety belt broadly to include a lap belt, a shoulder strap, or a combination of the two.

---

### Q882 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.412

A violation of the child passenger safety seat requirement under Section 545.412 is classified as:

- [x] A misdemeanor offense punishable by fine
- [ ] A felony of the third degree
- [ ] A civil infraction only, with no fine
- [ ] Not enforceable without a separate collision occurring

**Explanation:** Section 545.412 violations are misdemeanor offenses punishable by fine, consistent with other Chapter 545 safety-restraint requirements.

---

### Q883 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.412

The child passenger safety seat requirement under Section 545.412 generally applies to a child riding in a passenger vehicle, moped, or:

- [x] Light truck operated in Texas
- [ ] Farm tractor being used solely for agricultural work
- [ ] Golf cart operated in a master planned community
- [ ] School bus

**Explanation:** Section 545.412 applies to passenger vehicles and light trucks (mopeds are separately addressed); school buses and certain other vehicle types are treated differently under the broader safety-restraint scheme.

---

### Q884 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.412

Under Section 545.412, responsibility for securing a child in a required child passenger safety seat system generally falls on:

- [x] The operator of the vehicle
- [ ] The registered owner only, regardless of who is driving
- [ ] The child's school
- [ ] The vehicle's insurer

**Explanation:** Section 545.412 places the duty to secure the child in the required safety seat system on the vehicle's operator.

---

### Q885 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.420(b)

Under Section 545.420(b), a person commits the offense of participating in a race on a highway if the person, among other things:

- [x] Participates as a driver or spectator in a race, or in an exhibition of vehicle acceleration/speed on a highway
- [ ] Merely drives a vehicle capable of exceeding 100 mph, regardless of speed actually driven
- [ ] Owns a vehicle that has previously been used in a race
- [ ] Purchases racing equipment for personal use

**Explanation:** Section 545.420(b) covers driving or acting as a starter/timer/judge/spectator in a race, or an exhibition of acceleration, speed, or making a vehicle skid.

---

### Q886 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** PC 49.031(a)

Under Penal Code Section 49.031(a), the "passenger area of a motor vehicle" for open-container purposes generally excludes:

- [x] A locked glove compartment and the trunk
- [ ] The driver's seat only
- [ ] The rear passenger seats only
- [ ] Any area accessible without a key

**Explanation:** Section 49.031(a)'s passenger-area definition excludes glove compartments (if locked) and trunks from the open-container prohibition's reach.

---

### Q887 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** PC 46.01(17)

A "tire deflation device," as defined in Penal Code Section 46.01(17), is best described as a device that is:

- [x] Designed to be placed on a roadway to puncture or deflate the tires of a moving vehicle
- [ ] A permanently mounted vehicle safety feature
- [ ] Used exclusively to inflate, not deflate, tires
- [ ] Only usable by licensed tire shops

**Explanation:** Section 46.01(17) defines a tire deflation device by its function: puncturing or deflating a moving vehicle's tires, commonly a "spike strip."

---

### Q888 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.420

A violation of the racing statute, Section 545.420, that causes serious bodily injury to another person is generally punished as:

- [x] A felony, with the degree depending on the resulting harm
- [ ] Always a Class C misdemeanor regardless of harm
- [ ] A civil infraction with no criminal exposure
- [ ] An offense with no possible confinement

**Explanation:** Section 545.420 escalates punishment based on resulting harm, up to felony-level penalties when serious bodily injury or death results.

---

### Q889 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** PC 49.031

Under the open-container law, an "open container" is generally a bottle, can, or other receptacle that contains any amount of alcoholic beverage and:

- [x] Is open, has been opened, or has a broken seal, or the contents of which have been partially removed
- [ ] Is completely full and factory-sealed
- [ ] Was purchased more than 24 hours earlier
- [ ] Is located in a commercial passenger vehicle for hire

**Explanation:** Section 49.031's open-container definition turns on whether the container is open, previously opened, has a broken seal, or has had contents partially removed.

---

### Q890 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.420

HB 1442 tied a specific type of racing violation under Section 545.420 to potential asset forfeiture. Which subsection of 545.420 defines the narrow class of Class A misdemeanor conduct EXCLUDED from that forfeiture exposure?

- [x] Subsection (e)(2)(B)
- [ ] Subsection (a)(1)
- [ ] Subsection (c)
- [ ] Subsection (b)(3)

**Explanation:** Article 59.01(2)(B)(xiv), as amended by HB 1442, excludes Class A misdemeanor conduct based solely on Section 545.420(e)(2)(B) from forfeitable-contraband status.

### Q891 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.425(a)

Under Section 545.425(a), the general texting-while-driving prohibition bars a driver from reading, writing, or sending an electronic message using:

- [x] A portable wireless communication device, while operating a vehicle unless stopped
- [ ] Any device at all, including hands-free navigation systems
- [ ] Only a device mounted to the windshield
- [ ] Only a device connected to the vehicle's own electrical system

**Explanation:** Section 545.425(a) prohibits using a portable wireless communication device to read, write, or send an electronic message while the vehicle is in motion (unless stopped), subject to statutory exceptions.

---

### Q892 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.4251(a)

Under Section 545.4251(a), an "electronic message" for purposes of the distracted-driving law includes a text message, instant message, e-mail, or:

- [x] A command or request to access an internet website
- [ ] A voice phone call placed hands-free
- [ ] Music streamed through a vehicle's audio system
- [ ] A GPS route calculated automatically by the device

**Explanation:** Section 545.4251(a) defines electronic message broadly to include text messages, instant messages, e-mails, and commands/requests to access an internet site.

---

### Q893 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.425

The distracted-driving statute in Section 545.425 defines "hands-free device" to generally mean equipment that allows a driver to:

- [x] Use a wireless communication device without holding it in the driver's hand
- [ ] Drive without ever touching the steering wheel
- [ ] Bypass the vehicle's ignition interlock device
- [ ] Record video while driving

### Q894 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.425

- [x] Using the device to report illegal activity or summon emergency help
- [ ] Using the device to check social media at a red light
- [ ] Using the device while merging onto a highway
- [ ] Using the device to watch a video while driving slowly

**Explanation:** Section 545.425 exempts uses such as reporting illegal activity, summoning emergency assistance, and certain navigation/GPS uses, among others.

---

### Q895 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.425

A first violation of the general texting-while-driving prohibition in Section 545.425 is generally punishable as:

- [x] A misdemeanor punishable by a fine
- [ ] A felony of the state jail level
- [ ] Mandatory license revocation with no fine
- [ ] Not punishable unless a collision results

**Explanation:** Section 545.425 sets misdemeanor fine-level penalties for a standard violation, escalating for repeat offenses or offenses causing serious bodily injury or death.

---

### Q896 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.4251

Under Section 545.4251, which of these is explicitly listed as an "electronic message" component alongside text and instant messages?

- [x] Electronic mail (e-mail)
- [ ] A printed paper map
- [ ] A dashboard warning light
- [ ] A vehicle horn signal

**Explanation:** Section 545.4251(a) lists electronic mail among the communication types making up an "electronic message."

---

### Q897 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.451

Under Section 545.451's automated-driving-system framework, which SAE automation level corresponds to "full automation," where the automated driving system performs all driving tasks under all conditions?

- [x] Level 5
- [ ] Level 3
- [ ] Level 4
- [ ] Level 2

**Explanation:** Section 545.451(10) aligns with SAE Level 5 (full automation) as the highest tier in the automated-driving-system classification.

---

### Q898 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.451

Under Section 545.451, an automated motor vehicle operating with its automated driving system engaged is generally considered, for licensing purposes, to be operated by:

- [x] The automated driving system, satisfying the requirement that the vehicle have a licensed operator in certain respects
- [ ] No one — the vehicle is legally unoperated
- [ ] Only a remote operator physically located outside the state
- [ ] The vehicle manufacturer directly

**Explanation:** Chapter 545's automated-vehicle provisions treat the engaged automated driving system as satisfying operator requirements the statute assigns to it.

---

### Q899 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.451

Under Section 545.451(8)-(10), "Level 3," "Level 4," and "Level 5" automation are distinguished primarily by:

- [x] The extent to which the automated system, rather than a human, performs the driving task and monitors the environment
- [ ] The top speed the vehicle is rated for
- [ ] The number of passengers the vehicle may carry
- [ ] Whether the vehicle uses electric or gasoline power

**Explanation:** These SAE-based levels distinguish automation tiers by how much of the dynamic driving task and environmental monitoring shifts from the human driver to the automated system.

---

### Q900 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.451

Under the automated-vehicle provisions incorporating SAE levels, a Level 3 system is generally described as:

- [x] Conditional automation, where the system handles driving under certain conditions but a human must be ready to intervene
- [ ] A system requiring no human presence in the vehicle whatsoever
- [ ] Equivalent to standard cruise control
- [ ] Applicable only to commercial trucks

**Explanation:** Level 3 ("conditional automation") allows the system to handle driving under specified conditions while requiring a human to be available to take over when requested.

---

### Q901 — Miscellaneous Rules & Automated Vehicles
**LO:** 22.15 · **Source:** TC 545.451

Texas's automated motor vehicle statute generally requires that, before operating on a public highway, the automated driving system be capable of achieving a minimal risk condition if:

- [x] A malfunction occurs that renders the automated driving system unable to perform the dynamic driving task
- [ ] The vehicle exceeds the posted speed limit by any amount
- [ ] The vehicle's paint is a color other than white or silver
- [ ] The vehicle has fewer than four wheels

**Explanation:** The statute requires the system to be able to reach a minimal risk condition (e.g., safely stopping) upon a qualifying malfunction.

---

### Q902 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting

- [x] Required or permitted under Chapter 547 to be used on a vehicle
- [ ] Installed exclusively by the original manufacturer
- [ ] Required by insurance companies, not the state
- [ ] Limited strictly to lighting devices

### Q903 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting

- [x] Certified by the department to perform vehicle safety inspections
- [ ] Operated exclusively by a law enforcement agency
- [ ] Limited to inspecting only commercial vehicles
- [ ] Any repair shop, whether certified or not

**Explanation:** Section 548.001 defines an inspection station as one certified/authorized by the department to conduct the inspections Chapter 548 requires.

### Q904 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 548.001

Under Section 548.001, a "nonattainment area" is generally an area designated as such because it:

- [x] Fails to meet federal air quality standards for a given pollutant
- [ ] Has an unusually high rate of vehicle theft
- [ ] Lacks any certified inspection stations
- [ ] Has fewer than 1,000 registered vehicles

**Explanation:** Nonattainment area designations under Section 548.001 track federal Clean Air Act air-quality standard shortfalls, which is why emissions testing applies there.

---

### Q905 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 548.001

A "vehicle inspection report," as referenced in Section 548.001, is generally the document that:

- [x] Certifies the results of a vehicle's safety (and, where applicable, emissions) inspection
- [ ] Records only the vehicle's odometer reading at sale
- [ ] Is issued solely after a collision investigation
- [ ] Replaces the vehicle's certificate of title

**Explanation:** The vehicle inspection report documents the outcome of the required Chapter 548 inspection.

---

### Q906 — Vehicle Equipment & Lighting
**LO:** 22.17 · **Source:** TC 547.001

Under Chapter 547's vehicle equipment framework, operating a vehicle that lacks equipment required under the chapter (such as required lamps) generally exposes the operator to:

- [x] A misdemeanor equipment violation
- [ ] Automatic vehicle impoundment with no citation
- [ ] Only a written warning, by statute, on a first offense
- [ ] Federal, not state, prosecution

**Explanation:** Equipment violations under Chapter 547 are enforced as state misdemeanor traffic offenses.

### Q907 — Vehicle Registration & Titling
**LO:** 22.25 · **Source:** TC 501.002

Under Section 501.002, a "certificate of title" is the document that:

- [x] Establishes ownership of a motor vehicle in Texas
- [ ] Authorizes a vehicle's operation on a public highway
- [ ] Serves only as proof of financial responsibility
- [ ] Replaces the need for license plates

**Explanation:** The certificate of title, under Section 501.002, is the state's ownership document for a motor vehicle, distinct from registration (which authorizes operation).

---

### Q908 — Vehicle Registration & Titling
**LO:** 22.25 · **Source:** TC 501.002

Under Section 501.002, a "first sale" of a motor vehicle generally refers to:

- [x] The first transfer of a new motor vehicle by a manufacturer, importer, or dealer to a purchaser
- [ ] Any sale made at a government auction
- [ ] The sale of a vehicle after its second registered owner
- [ ] A sale that occurs entirely online

**Explanation:** Section 501.002 defines first sale as the initial transfer of a new vehicle from the manufacturer/importer/dealer chain to a buyer.

---

### Q909 — Vehicle Registration & Titling
**LO:** 22.25 · **Source:** TC 501.002

Under Section 501.002, a "lien" on a motor vehicle is generally defined as:

- [x] A security interest in the vehicle recorded to secure payment of an obligation
- [ ] Any traffic citation issued to the vehicle's owner
- [ ] A parking violation attached to the vehicle's registration
- [ ] The vehicle's insurance policy number

**Explanation:** A lien under Section 501.002 is the recorded security interest securing an obligation related to the vehicle.

---

### Q910 — Vehicle Registration & Titling
**LO:** 22.25 · **Source:** TC 501.002

Under Section 501.002, a "used motor vehicle" is generally one that:

- [x] Has been the subject of a first sale and is not a new vehicle
- [ ] Is more than 25 years old
- [ ] Has changed ownership at least three times
- [ ] Cannot pass a current safety inspection

**Explanation:** Once a vehicle has undergone its first sale, subsequent sales are treated as used-vehicle transactions under Section 501.002.

---

### Q911 — Vehicle Registration & Titling
**LO:** 22.25 · **Source:** TC 503.001

Under Section 503.001, a "franchised motor vehicle dealer" is generally distinguished from other dealers by:

- [x] Holding a franchise agreement with a vehicle manufacturer or distributor to sell new vehicles
- [ ] Selling only vehicles more than 10 years old
- [ ] Operating exclusively online with no physical lot
- [ ] Being owned by a government entity

**Explanation:** A franchised dealer, per Section 503.001, operates under a manufacturer/distributor franchise agreement for new-vehicle sales.

---

### Q912 — Vehicle Registration & Titling
**LO:** 22.25 · **Source:** TC 503.001

Under Section 503.001, a "wholesale motor vehicle dealer" generally sells vehicles:

- [x] To other dealers, rather than directly to retail consumers
- [ ] Only to government fleets
- [ ] Only vehicles under manufacturer recall
- [ ] Exclusively at public auctions open to consumers

**Explanation:** A wholesale dealer's business model, under Section 503.001, centers on dealer-to-dealer sales rather than retail sales to the public.

---

### Q913 — Vehicle Registration & Titling
**LO:** 22.25 · **Source:** TC 503.001

Under Section 503.001, a "drive-a-way operator" is generally a person in the business of:

- [x] Delivering vehicles by driving them, in a drive-away or tow-away operation, from one location to another for compensation
- [ ] Renting vehicles for single-day use
- [ ] Repairing vehicles damaged in a collision
- [ ] Installing aftermarket lighting equipment

**Explanation:** A drive-a-way operator, per Section 503.001, is in the business of delivering vehicles between locations by driving or towing them for compensation.

---

### Q914 — Vehicle Registration & Titling
**LO:** 22.25 · **Source:** TC 502.001

Under Section 502.001, a "commercial fleet" generally refers to:

- [x] A group of motor vehicles, meeting statutory minimums, owned or leased by the same person for commercial use
- [ ] Any two or more vehicles registered to the same household
- [ ] A fleet of emergency vehicles only
- [ ] Vehicles used exclusively for interstate trucking

**Explanation:** Section 502.001 defines commercial fleet by reference to common ownership/lease and a statutory minimum number of commercially used vehicles.

---

### Q915 — Vehicle Registration & Titling
**LO:** 22.25 · **Source:** TC 502.001

Under Section 502.001, a "token trailer" is generally:

- [x] A trailer registered under a special, reduced-fee registration scheme for trailers used only occasionally
- [ ] A trailer that has never been registered
- [ ] A trailer used exclusively to haul other trailers
- [ ] A miniature, non-roadworthy display trailer

**Explanation:** Section 502.001 provides a token-trailer registration classification with reduced fees for occasional-use trailers.

### Q916 — Vehicle Registration & Titling
**LO:** 22.25 · **Source:** TC 683.077(b)

Under Section 683.077(b), a "special interest vehicle" is generally one that is:

- [x] Owned and preserved primarily because of its historic or special interest value
- [ ] Currently used as a daily commuter vehicle
- [ ] Registered as a commercial fleet vehicle
- [ ] Younger than five model years old

**Explanation:** Section 683.077(b) defines special interest vehicle by reference to its owner preserving it for historic or special interest reasons, not everyday use.

---

### Q917 — Vehicle Registration & Titling
**LO:** 22.25 · **Source:** TC 683.077(b)

Under Section 683.077(b), a "motor vehicle collector" is generally a person who:

- [x] Owns one or more antique or special interest vehicles for personal use, restoration, and preservation rather than general transportation
- [ ] Sells more than 10 vehicles per year as a dealer
- [ ] Repairs vehicles for a living at a licensed shop
- [ ] Operates a vehicle salvage yard

**Explanation:** Section 683.077(b) defines motor vehicle collector as an owner of antique/special interest vehicles for preservation, not for general daily transportation.

---

### Q918 — Vehicle Registration & Titling
**LO:** 22.25 · **Source:** TC 504.501(f)

Under Section 504.501(f), a "street rod" is generally a vehicle:

- [x] Manufactured to resemble a vehicle manufactured before a certain model year, per the statute's specifications
- [ ] Manufactured within the current model year only
- [ ] Used exclusively for professional racing
- [ ] Owned by a licensed motor vehicle dealer

**Explanation:** Section 504.501(f) defines street rod by reference to its resemblance to an older-model-year vehicle design, as further specified in the statute.

---

### Q919 — Vehicle Registration & Titling
**LO:** 22.25 · **Source:** TC 504.501(f)

Under Section 504.501(f), a "custom vehicle" generally refers to a vehicle that:

- [x] Was manufactured to resemble a vehicle of a model year at least 25 years before the current year, altered from stock
- [ ] Is any vehicle with aftermarket wheels installed
- [ ] Was built entirely by its current owner in the past year
- [ ] Is limited to motorcycles only

**Explanation:** Section 504.501(f) ties custom vehicle status to resembling an older-model-year design, distinct from an unmodified antique vehicle.

---

### Q920 — Vehicle Registration & Titling
**LO:** 22.25 · **Source:** TC 683.077(b)

Under Section 683.077(b), an "antique vehicle" is generally distinguished from a "custom vehicle" or "street rod" because an antique vehicle:

- [x] Retains its original manufactured body style and equipment rather than being built to resemble an older design
- [ ] Must be registered as a commercial fleet vehicle
- [ ] Cannot be legally driven on public highways under any circumstance
- [ ] Has no minimum age requirement

**Explanation:** Antique vehicle status generally requires the vehicle to be an original (or authentically restored) vehicle of its stated age, unlike a custom vehicle or street rod built to merely resemble an older design.

### Q921 — Vehicle Registration & Titling
**LO:** 22.25 · **Source:** TC 552.003(e)(2)

Under Section 552.003(e)(2), for purposes of certain crosswalk/pedestrian-related provisions, a person is considered "disabled" if the person has a physical or mental disability that:

- [x] Substantially impairs the person's mobility, hearing, or vision
- [ ] Has been certified by a Social Security disability determination only
- [ ] Prevents the person from holding any employment
- [ ] Is limited strictly to a mobility impairment, excluding hearing or vision

**Explanation:** Section 552.003(e)(2) defines disabled, for these purposes, as substantially impairing mobility, hearing, or vision.

### Q922 — Pedestrians
**LO:** 22.19 · **Source:** TC 552.003

Section 552.003 addresses the right-of-way owed to a pedestrian who is disabled within the meaning of the statute. A driver approaching such a pedestrian must:

- [x] Take precautions necessary to avoid injuring the pedestrian, consistent with the heightened duty the statute creates
- [ ] Sound the horn to alert the pedestrian, then proceed normally
- [ ] Yield only if the pedestrian is using a marked crosswalk
- [ ] Have no heightened duty beyond ordinary pedestrian right-of-way rules

**Explanation:** Section 552.003 creates a heightened duty of care toward a disabled pedestrian as the statute defines that term.

---

### Q923 — Pedestrians
**LO:** 22.19 · **Source:** TC 552.003

Under Section 552.003, a driver's heightened duty toward a disabled pedestrian generally applies:

- [x] Regardless of whether the pedestrian is in a marked or unmarked crosswalk
- [ ] Only within a marked crosswalk
- [ ] Only during daylight hours
- [ ] Only on highways with a posted speed limit under 30 mph

**Explanation:** The heightened duty under Section 552.003 is not limited to marked crosswalks — it applies to the driver's general obligation toward a disabled pedestrian encountered on the roadway.

### Q924 — Pedestrians
**LO:** 22.19 · **Source:** TC 552.010

Section 552.010 addresses the right-of-way owed to a pedestrian using a service animal or a white cane. A driver who fails to yield the right-of-way to such a pedestrian:

- [x] Commits a traffic offense under the statute
- [ ] Commits no offense unless a collision actually results
- [ ] Is civilly, but never criminally, liable
- [ ] Is only liable if the pedestrian is crossing at a signalized intersection

**Explanation:** Section 552.010 makes failure to yield to a pedestrian using a guide/service animal or a white cane a traffic offense in its own right.

### Q925 — Pedestrians
**LO:** 22.19 · **Source:** TC 552.003

The pedestrian right-of-way statute in Chapter 552 generally places the primary duty to avoid a collision with a pedestrian on:

- [x] The driver, who must exercise due care regardless of the pedestrian's own right-of-way status
- [ ] The pedestrian exclusively, in all circumstances
- [ ] The municipality where the incident occurs
- [ ] Whichever party has automobile insurance

**Explanation:** Chapter 552 consistently places a due-care duty on drivers toward pedestrians, even where the pedestrian does not have the statutory right-of-way.

### Q926 — Traffic Signs, Signals & Markings
**LO:** 22.8 · **Source:** TC 544.007(j)

Under Section 544.007(j), a "pedestrian hybrid beacon" is generally a traffic control device that:

- [x] Is normally dark and activates to stop traffic only when a pedestrian needs to cross
- [ ] Operates identically to a standard three-color traffic signal at all times
- [ ] Is used exclusively to control freeway ramp metering
- [ ] Applies only to bicycle traffic, never pedestrians

**Explanation:** A pedestrian hybrid beacon, per Section 544.007(j), stays dark until activated by a pedestrian, then displays signals to stop traffic for the crossing.

---

### Q927 — Traffic Signs, Signals & Markings
**LO:** 22.8 · **Source:** TC 544.007(j)

Under Section 544.007(j), a "freeway entrance ramp control signal" is generally used to:

- [x] Regulate the rate at which vehicles enter a freeway from an entrance ramp
- [ ] Control pedestrian crossings on surface streets
- [ ] Signal railroad crossings near freeway interchanges
- [ ] Direct emergency vehicles exclusively

**Explanation:** This device, defined in Section 544.007(j), meters ramp traffic onto the freeway, commonly called "ramp metering."

---

### Q928 — Traffic Signs, Signals & Markings
**LO:** 22.8 · **Source:** TC 544.007

A driver approaching an activated pedestrian hybrid beacon displaying a steady red signal must:

- [x] Stop and remain stopped until the signal changes, similar to a standard red signal
- [ ] Treat it as a yield sign and proceed if clear
- [ ] Ignore it unless a pedestrian is physically visible in the roadway
- [ ] Only slow down, never stop

**Explanation:** A steady red indication on a pedestrian hybrid beacon requires the same stop-and-remain-stopped response as a standard traffic signal's steady red.

---

### Q929 — Traffic Signs, Signals & Markings
**LO:** 22.8 · **Source:** TC 544.007

A freeway entrance ramp control signal displaying a steady red indication requires an approaching driver to:

- [x] Stop until the signal permits entry onto the freeway
- [ ] Merge immediately regardless of the signal
- [ ] Treat the signal as advisory only
- [ ] Proceed if no other vehicles are visible

**Explanation:** Ramp control signals function like standard signals — a steady red requires the driver to stop and wait for a green indication before merging.

**LO:** 22.2 · **Source:** TC 623.008(a)

- [x] Warn other traffic of an oversize or overweight vehicle traveling under permit
- [ ] Transport prisoners between facilities
- [ ] Provide funeral procession escort exclusively
- [ ] Escort school buses on their daily routes

**Explanation:** Section 623.008(a)(1) defines an escort flag vehicle in connection with warning traffic about an oversize/overweight permitted vehicle.

### Q931 — Persons, Authorities & Penal Code Cross-References
**LO:** 22.2 · **Source:** TC 623.008(a)

Under Section 623.008(a), an "escort flagger" refers to:

- [x] A person who operates an escort flag vehicle or otherwise provides escort/flagging service for an oversize or overweight vehicle
- [ ] A construction worker directing traffic through a work zone only
- [ ] A DPS trooper assigned exclusively to highway patrol
- [ ] A crossing guard at a school zone

**Explanation:** Section 623.008(a)(2) defines escort flagger as the person performing the escort/flagging role for oversize/overweight permitted vehicles.

### Q932 — Persons, Authorities & Penal Code Cross-References
**LO:** 22.2 · **Source:** TC 623.008

The escort flag vehicle/flagger requirements under Section 623.008 typically apply in connection with a vehicle operating under:

- [x] An oversize or overweight vehicle permit issued under Chapter 623
- [ ] A standard passenger vehicle registration only
- [ ] A commercial driver's license restriction
- [ ] An occupational driver's license

**Explanation:** Chapter 623 governs oversize/overweight vehicle permits, and Section 623.008 sets the related escort vehicle/flagger requirements.
